BLUEPRINT
INSTRUCTIONS
Structure & Priority Guide
Questions 1–200 are MCQs; FRQs begin at Question 201 and end at Question 250. Answers are placed after the entire question paper.
Priority Ratings
★★★★★   Very Important
Core concepts and multi-step AP-style reasoning.
★★★   Mandatory
Essential procedures and standard applications.
★★   Important
Foundational checks and direct applications.
  • • Unit 1: Limits & Continuity — 20 MCQs, 5 FRQs.
  • • Unit 2: Differentiation: Fundamental Properties — 20 MCQs, 5 FRQs.
  • • Unit 3: Composite, Implicit & Inverse Functions — 20 MCQs, 5 FRQs.
  • • Unit 4: Contextual Applications of Differentiation — 20 MCQs, 5 FRQs.
  • • Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation — 20 MCQs, 5 FRQs.
  • • Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change — 20 MCQs, 5 FRQs.
  • • Unit 7: Differential Equations (including Logistic & Euler's Method) — 20 MCQs, 5 FRQs.
  • • Unit 8: Applications of Integration — 20 MCQs, 5 FRQs.
  • • Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates & Vector-Valued Functions — 20 MCQs, 5 FRQs.
  • • Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series (Taylor/Maclaurin & Convergence Tests) — 20 MCQs, 5 FRQs.
• Scoring for this practice set: 200 MCQs × 1 point and 50 FRQs × 9 points = 650 raw points. This is not an official AP score conversion.
• Provenance: Original AP-aligned practice; not copied College Board exam items.
Directions: Attempt all questions before consulting the answer section. For FRQs, show the setup and justify each conclusion.

A metal bar at temperature 180°C is placed in a room where the temperature is held constant at 20°C. The temperature T(t) of the bar (in °C) cools according to Newton's Law of Cooling: dT/dt = -k(T - 20), where t is in minutes and k is a positive constant. After 15 minutes, the bar cools to 100°C.
(a) Solve the differential equation to find an expression for T(t) in terms of k and t.
(b) Find the exact value of the cooling constant k.
(c) What is the temperature of the bar after 30 minutes? Round to the nearest tenth of a degree.
(d) After how many minutes will the temperature of the bar reach 35°C? Round to the nearest minute.

Q001–Q003
Question 001 · Unit 1: Limits & Continuity
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) 2/3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Identify indeterminate form. Direct substitution of x = 3 yields (32 - 2(3) - 3) / (32 - 9) = (9 - 6 - 3) / (9 - 9) = 0/0, which is indeterminate.

Step 2: Factor numerator and denominator. The numerator factors as (x - 3)(x + 1) and the denominator factors as (x - 3)(x + 3).

Step 3: Cancel common factors. For all x ≠ 3, the expression simplifies to (x + 1) / (x + 3).

Step 4: Evaluate the limit. Substituting x = 3 gives (3 + 1) / (3 + 3) = 4/6 = 2/3.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Incorrectly assumes the limit is 0 because the numerator evaluated to 0 initially.
  • ✗ (C)Result of arithmetic error in factoring or dividing as 4/3 instead of 4/6 = 2/3.
  • ✗ (D)Incorrect; the removable discontinuity at x = 3 allows a finite two-sided limit to exist.
Question 002 · Unit 1: Limits & Continuity
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) 1/8
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Check indeterminate form. Substituting x = 0 yields (√16 - 4)/0 = 0/0.

Step 2: Rationalize the numerator. Multiply numerator and denominator by the conjugate (√(x + 16) + 4):
((√(x + 16) - 4)(√(x + 16) + 4)) / (x(√(x + 16) + 4)) = ((x + 16) - 16) / (x(√(x + 16) + 4)) = x / (x(√(x + 16) + 4)).

Step 3: Cancel factor x for x ≠ 0. The expression becomes 1 / (√(x + 16) + 4).

Step 4: Take the limit as x → 0. 1 / (√(0 + 16) + 4) = 1 / (4 + 4) = 1/8.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Arises from forgetting that the conjugate has two terms added: 1/4 instead of 1/(4+4).
  • ✗ (C)Arises from squaring 4 as 16 in the denominator.
  • ✗ (D)Believing the zero in the numerator dominates.
Question 003 · Unit 1: Limits & Continuity
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) 0
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Establish bounding inequality. For all x ≠ 0, the cosine function is strictly bounded: -1 ≤ cos(3 / x2) ≤ 1.

Step 2: Multiply by positive term x4. Since x4 > 0 for all x ≠ 0, the inequality preserves direction: -x4 ≤ x4 cos(3 / x2) ≤ x4.

Step 3: Evaluate limits of bounding functions. lim(x→0) (-x4) = 0 and lim(x→0) (x4) = 0.

Step 4: Apply Squeeze Theorem. Because both bounds converge to 0 as x → 0, by the Squeeze Theorem, lim(x→0) x4 cos(3 / x2) = 0.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Misinterprets the argument 3 inside the cosine as a constant multiplier.
  • ✗ (B)Assumes cos(0) = 1 dominates the product.
  • ✗ (D)Oscillation occurs for cos(3/x2), but the factor x4 forces the amplitude to 0.

Q004–Q006
Question 004 · Unit 1: Limits & Continuity
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) 11
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Estimate lim(x→2) f(x) from numerical pattern. As x approaches 2 from the left (1.9 → 1.99 → 1.999), f(x) approaches 5.000. As x approaches 2 from the right (2.1 → 2.01 → 2.001), f(x) approaches 5.000.

Step 2: Conclude two-sided limit for f(x). lim(x→2) f(x) = 5.

Step 3: Apply limit properties to expression. lim(x→2) [3f(x) - 4] = 3 · lim(x→2) f(x) - 4 = 3(5) - 4.

Step 4: Calculate final numerical value. 15 - 4 = 11.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Omits subtracting 4, giving only 3(5) = 15.
  • ✗ (C)Only reports the limit of f(x) itself, 5.
  • ✗ (D)Arithmetic error: calculates 5 - 4 = 1 instead of 3(5) - 4 = 11.
Question 005 · Unit 1: Limits & Continuity
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) 5/3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Recognize fundamental trigonometric limit. Recall that lim(u→0) sin(u) / u = 1.

Step 2: Rewrite the expression to match argument. Let u = 5x. Then sin(5x) / (3x) = (5/3) · (sin(5x) / (5x)).

Step 3: Evaluate limit using scalar multiple rule. lim(x→0) [(5/3) · (sin(5x)/(5x))] = (5/3) · 1 = 5/3.

Step 4: Conclude result. The limit equals 5/3.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Incorrectly assumes sin(0) causes entire fraction to evaluate to 0.
  • ✗ (B)Inverts the ratio of coefficients as 3/5 instead of 5/3.
  • ✗ (D)Applies lim(x→0) sin(x)/x = 1 without accounting for the constants 5 and 3.
Question 006 · Unit 1: Limits & Continuity
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) lim(x→2) f(x) does not exist because left- and right-hand limits are unequal
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Evaluate right-hand limit (x > 2). For x > 2, |x - 2| = x - 2. Thus f(x) = (x - 2)(x + 2) / (x - 2) = x + 2. Then lim(x→2⁺) f(x) = 2 + 2 = 4.

Step 2: Evaluate left-hand limit (x < 2). For x < 2, |x - 2| = -(x - 2). Thus f(x) = (x - 2)(x + 2) / [-(x - 2)] = -(x + 2). Then lim(x→2⁻) f(x) = -(2 + 2) = -4.

Step 3: Compare one-sided limits. Since lim(x→2⁻) f(x) = -4 ≠ 4 = lim(x→2⁺) f(x), the two-sided limit does not exist.

Step 4: Identify type of discontinuity. This represents a jump discontinuity, not an infinite asymptote.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Only accounts for the right-hand limit.
  • ✗ (B)Only accounts for the left-hand limit.
  • ✗ (D)The discontinuity is a jump discontinuity of magnitude 8, not an infinite vertical asymptote.

ANSWER SECTION · MCQ SOLUTIONS
Q007–Q009
Question 007 · Unit 1: Limits & Continuity
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) y = 3/2 and y = -3/2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute limit as x → +∞. For x > 0, √(4x2 + 9) = x√(4 + 9/x2). Then lim(x→∞) (3x + 1) / [x√(4 + 9/x2)] = lim(x→∞) (3 + 1/x) / √(4 + 9/x2) = 3 / √4 = 3/2. Thus y = 3/2 is a horizontal asymptote.

Step 2: Compute limit as x → -∞. For x < 0, √x2 = |x| = -x. Thus √(4x2 + 9) = -x√(4 + 9/x2). Then lim(x→-∞) (3x + 1) / [-x√(4 + 9/x2)] = lim(x→-∞) (3 + 1/x) / [-√(4 + 9/x2)] = 3 / (-2) = -3/2. Thus y = -3/2 is also a horizontal asymptote.

Step 3: Combine both limits. The function has two distinct horizontal asymptotes: y = 3/2 and y = -3/2.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Misses the negative asymptote at -∞ by forgetting that √x2 = |x| = -x when x < 0.
  • ✗ (C)Incorrectly squares 2 to get 4 in denominator.
  • ✗ (D)Confuses with cases where the degree of denominator exceeds numerator.
Question 008 · Unit 1: Limits & Continuity
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (A) k = 5, m = -1
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Apply condition for removable discontinuity. For lim(x→2) g(x) to exist as a finite real number when the denominator is x - 2, the numerator must also equal 0 at x = 2.

Step 2: Solve for k. 22 - k(2) + 6 = 0 ⇒ 4 - 2k + 6 = 0 ⇒ 10 - 2k = 0 ⇒ k = 5.

Step 3: Simplify g(x) for x ≠ 2. With k = 5, the numerator is x2 - 5x + 6 = (x - 2)(x - 3). Then g(x) = (x - 2)(x - 3) / (x - 2) = x - 3.

Step 4: Find m by continuity condition. By continuity at x = 2, m = g(2) = lim(x→2) g(x) = 2 - 3 = -1.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Signs error in factor evaluation: evaluates 2 - 3 as +1 instead of -1.
  • ✗ (C)Arbitrary values that do not make numerator zero at x = 2.
  • ✗ (D)Solves 4 + 2k + 6 = 0 incorrectly.
Question 009 · Unit 1: Limits & Continuity
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) [1, 2]
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Verify continuity of f. f(x) is a polynomial function, hence continuous on all real numbers and on every closed interval [a, b].

Step 2: Evaluate f at interval endpoints.
For [1, 2]:
f(1) = (1)3 + 3(1) - 5 = 1 + 3 - 5 = -1 < 0.
f(2) = (2)3 + 3(2) - 5 = 8 + 6 - 5 = 9 > 0.

Step 3: Apply IVT. Since f is continuous on [1, 2] and f(1) = -1 < 0 < 9 = f(2), 0 lies strictly between f(1) and f(2). Therefore, by the Intermediate Value Theorem, there exists at least one value c in (1, 2) such that f(c) = 0.

Step 4: Check other intervals.
For [-1, 0]: f(-1) = -9 and f(0) = -5 (both negative).
For [0, 1]: f(0) = -5 and f(1) = -1 (both negative).
For [2, 3]: f(2) = 9 and f(3) = 31 (both positive). None of these guarantee a zero by IVT.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Both f(-1) = -9 and f(0) = -5 are negative, so IVT does not guarantee a sign change.
  • ✗ (C)Both f(2) = 9 and f(3) = 31 are positive, no sign change is guaranteed.
  • ✗ (D)Both f(0) = -5 and f(1) = -1 are negative, no sign change is guaranteed.

Q010–Q012
Question 010 · Unit 1: Limits & Continuity
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) 1/e
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Recognize definition of the derivative. Recall that f'(a) = lim(x→a) [f(x) - f(a)] / (x - a).

Step 2: Identify function and point of evaluation. Here, f(x) = ln(x) and a = e. Notice f(e) = ln(e) = 1.

Step 3: Differentiate f(x). f'(x) = d/dx [ln x] = 1/x.

Step 4: Evaluate derivative at a = e. f'(e) = 1/e.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Mistakenly evaluates 1/1 = 1 instead of 1/e.
  • ✗ (C)Inverts the derivative to e instead of 1/e.
  • ✗ (D)Incorrectly evaluates the numerator 0 without taking the derivative limit.
Question 011 · Unit 1: Limits & Continuity
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) x = 0 and x = 2 only
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Factor the denominator. x3 - x2 - 2x = x(x2 - x - 2) = x(x - 2)(x + 1).

Step 2: Factor the numerator. x2 - 1 = (x - 1)(x + 1).

Step 3: Analyze each zero of the denominator.
At x = -1: The factor (x + 1) appears in both numerator and denominator with multiplicity 1. lim(x→-1) f(x) = lim(x→-1) (x - 1) / [x(x - 2)] = (-2) / [(-1)(-3)] = -2/3. Since this limit is finite, x = -1 is a removable discontinuity (hole), not a vertical asymptote.
At x = 0: lim(x→0⁺) (x - 1) / [x(x - 2)] = (-1) / [0⁺(-2)] = +∞. Thus x = 0 is a vertical asymptote.
At x = 2: lim(x→2⁺) (x - 1) / [x(x - 2)] = (1) / [2(0⁺)] = +∞. Thus x = 2 is a vertical asymptote.

Step 4: Conclude vertical asymptotes. The graph has vertical asymptotes at x = 0 and x = 2 only.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Fails to recognize that x = -1 produces a removable hole rather than a vertical asymptote.
  • ✗ (C)Forgets that x = 0 makes the denominator zero with non-zero numerator.
  • ✗ (D)Forgets that x = 2 makes the denominator zero with non-zero numerator.
Question 012 · Unit 1: Limits & Continuity
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) -7
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Factor the quadratic numerator. 2x2 + 5x - 3 = (2x - 1)(x + 3).

Step 2: Simplify f(x) for x ≠ -3. f(x) = (2x - 1)(x + 3) / (x + 3) = 2x - 1.

Step 3: Compute the limit as x → -3. lim(x→-3) f(x) = lim(x→-3) (2x - 1) = 2(-3) - 1 = -7.

Step 4: Set c = lim(x→-3) f(x) for continuity. For f(x) to be continuous at x = -3, f(-3) = c must equal lim(x→-3) f(x) = -7.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Calculation error: 2(-3) + 5 = -1.
  • ✗ (C)Arithmetic mistake using 2(3) - 1 = 5.
  • ✗ (D)Sign error: evaluates -6 - 1 as +7 instead of -7.

Q013–Q015
Question 013 · Unit 2: Differentiation: Definition & Fundamental Properties
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) 9
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State definition of average velocity. v_avg = [s(4) - s(1)] / (4 - 1).

Step 2: Calculate position at endpoints.
s(1) = 2(1)3 - 9(1)2 + 12(1) = 2 - 9 + 12 = 5.
s(4) = 2(4)3 - 9(4)2 + 12(4) = 2(64) - 9(16) + 48 = 128 - 144 + 48 = 32.

Step 3: Compute average velocity. v_avg = (32 - 5) / (4 - 1) = 27 / 3 = 9.

Step 4: Verify result. The average velocity over [1, 4] is exactly 9.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Mistakenly averages the endpoint instantaneous velocities [s'(1) + s'(4)] / 2 = 11.
  • ✗ (C)Calculation error in numerator.
  • ✗ (D)Omits dividing by Δt = 3, giving displacement 32 - 5 = 27 or arithmetic slip 33.
Question 014 · Unit 2: Differentiation: Definition & Fundamental Properties
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (A) lim(h→0) [tan(2(π/6 + h)) - √3] / h
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State definition of derivative. f'(c) = lim(h→0) [f(c + h) - f(c)] / h.

Step 2: Identify f(c + h) and f(c). For f(x) = tan(2x) and c = π/6:
f(π/6 + h) = tan(2(π/6 + h)).
f(π/6) = tan(2 · π/6) = tan(π/3) = √3.

Step 3: Construct the limit. Substituting into the definition gives lim(h→0) [tan(2(π/6 + h)) - √3] / h.

Step 4: Verify equivalence. Option A matches this exact structure.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Incorrectly evaluates tan(π/3) as 1 instead of √3.
  • ✗ (C)Fails to include the factor of 2 inside the tangent argument for (c + h).
  • ✗ (D)Omits the center point π/6 from the increment term.
Question 015 · Unit 2: Differentiation: Definition & Fundamental Properties
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) a = -2, b = 6
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Apply continuity condition at x = 1. For f to be continuous at x = 1, lim(x→1⁻) f(x) = lim(x→1⁺) f(x):
a(1)2 + b(1) = 4√1 ⇒ a + b = 4.

Step 2: Apply differentiability condition at x = 1. The left- and right-hand derivatives must be equal at x = 1:
Left derivative: d/dx [ax2 + bx] = 2ax + b ⇒ at x = 1, f'₋(1) = 2a + b.
Right derivative: d/dx [4x^(1/2)] = 2 / √x ⇒ at x = 1, f'₊(1) = 2 / √1 = 2.
Thus, 2a + b = 2.

Step 3: Solve the linear system of equations.
(1) a + b = 4
(2) 2a + b = 2
Subtracting (1) from (2): (2a + b) - (a + b) = 2 - 4 ⇒ a = -2.
Then b = 4 - (-2) = 6.

Step 4: Verify values a = -2, b = 6.
Continuity: a + b = -2 + 6 = 4. Satisfied.
Differentiability: 2a + b = 2(-2) + 6 = -4 + 6 = 2. Satisfied.
Therefore, a = -2 and b = 6 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Evaluates 2a + b = 2(-1) + 5 = 3 ≠ 2.
  • ✗ (B)Evaluates 2a + b = 4 + 2 = 6 ≠ 2.
  • ✗ (D)Evaluates 2a + b = 2(1) + 3 = 5 ≠ 2.

Q016–Q018
Question 016 · Unit 2: Differentiation: Definition & Fundamental Properties
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) -15/x4 + 2/(3 x^(4/3))
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Rewrite f(x) with negative and rational exponents. f(x) = 5x⁻³ - 2x^(-1/3) + 4.

Step 2: Differentiate term-by-term using the power rule.
d/dx [5x⁻³] = 5(-3)x⁻⁴ = -15x⁻⁴ = -15 / x4.
d/dx [-2x^(-1/3)] = -2(-1/3)x^(-4/3) = (2/3)x^(-4/3) = 2 / (3 x^(4/3)).
d/dx [4] = 0.

Step 3: Combine terms. f'(x) = -15 / x4 + 2 / (3 x^(4/3)).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Subtracts 1 from exponent incorrectly: treats x⁻³ as x⁻².
  • ✗ (C)Incorrect sign for the second term: fails to realize (-2)(-1/3) = +2/3.
  • ✗ (D)Forgets negative sign on the first derivative term.
Question 017 · Unit 2: Differentiation: Definition & Fundamental Properties
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) 0
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute first derivative f'(x) using product rule. f'(x) = (d/dx [x3]) e2x + x3 (d/dx [e2x]) = 3x2 e2x + 2x3 e2x = (2x3 + 3x2) e2x.

Step 2: Compute second derivative f''(x) using product rule. f''(x) = (d/dx [2x3 + 3x2]) e2x + (2x3 + 3x2) (d/dx [e2x]) = (6x2 + 6x) e2x + 2(2x3 + 3x2) e2x = (4x3 + 12x2 + 6x) e2x.

Step 3: Evaluate at x = 0. f''(0) = (4(0)3 + 12(0)2 + 6(0)) e⁰ = 0 · 1 = 0.

Step 4: Alternative via Maclaurin expansion. x3 e2x = x3(1 + 2x + ...) = x3 + 2x4 + ... The degree 2 term has coefficient 0, so f''(0)/2! = 0 ⇒ f''(0) = 0.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Value of f'''(0) = 6.
  • ✗ (C)Confuses with coefficient 12 of x2 term.
  • ✗ (D)Arithmetic error.
Question 018 · Unit 2: Differentiation: Definition & Fundamental Properties
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) 3/2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Apply quotient rule. g'(x) = [ (d/dx [2x - 3])(x2 + 1) - (2x - 3)(d/dx [x2 + 1]) ] / (x2 + 1)2 = [ 2(x2 + 1) - (2x - 3)(2x) ] / (x2 + 1)2.

Step 2: Simplify numerator. 2x2 + 2 - (4x2 - 6x) = -2x2 + 6x + 2.

Step 3: Evaluate g'(1). At x = 1:
Numerator = -2(1)2 + 6(1) + 2 = -2 + 6 + 2 = 6.
Denominator = (12 + 1)2 = 22 = 4.

Step 4: Compute the slope. g'(1) = 6 / 4 = 3/2.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Evaluates numerator without subtracting correctly: 8/4 = 2.
  • ✗ (C)Calculation error: 4/4 = 1.
  • ✗ (D)Inverts fraction or evaluates as 2/4 = 1/2.
AP Calculus BC · Ten-Unit Mixed Practice Set Page 130
ANSWER SECTION · MCQ SOLUTIONS
Q019–Q021
Question 019 · Unit 2: Differentiation: Definition & Fundamental Properties
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) -cos(x)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Identify periodic cycle of derivatives of cos(x).
f⁽⁰⁾(x) = cos(x)
f⁽¹⁾(x) = -sin(x)
f⁽²⁾(x) = -cos(x)
f⁽³⁾(x) = sin(x)
f⁽⁴⁾(x) = cos(x). The cycle repeats every 4 derivatives.

Step 2: Determine remainder modulo 4. 50 = 4 · 12 + 2, so 50 ≡ 2 (mod 4).

Step 3: Match with cyclic derivative. The 50th derivative is identical to the 2nd derivative: f⁽⁵⁰⁾(x) = f⁽²⁾(x) = -cos(x).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Corresponds to derivatives of order 4k (e.g., 48th or 52nd derivative).
  • ✗ (C)Corresponds to order 4k + 3 (e.g., 51st derivative).
  • ✗ (D)Corresponds to order 4k + 1 (e.g., 49th derivative).
Question 020 · Unit 2: Differentiation: Definition & Fundamental Properties
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) 5
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Choose the interval containing x = 4.5. The points x = 3 and x = 6 bracket 4.5 symmetrically: 4.5 = (3 + 6) / 2.

Step 2: Set up the symmetric difference quotient. f'(4.5) ≈ [f(6) - f(3)] / (6 - 3).

Step 3: Substitute table values. [29 - 14] / (6 - 3) = 15 / 3 = 5.

Step 4: Conclude approximation. The best approximation for f'(4.5) is 5.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Evaluates [14 - 8]/(3 - 1) = 6/2 = 3 on interval [1, 3], which does not contain 4.5.
  • ✗ (C)Evaluates [53 - 29]/(10 - 6) = 24/4 = 6 on interval [6, 10].
  • ✗ (D)Forgets to divide by Δx = 3, giving only Δf = 15.
Question 021 · Unit 2: Differentiation: Definition & Fundamental Properties
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) 2x ln(x) + x
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Apply product rule. f'(x) = d/dx [x2] · ln(x) + x2 · d/dx [ln(x)].

Step 2: Differentiate each component. d/dx [x2] = 2x, and d/dx [ln(x)] = 1/x.

Step 3: Simplify the product. f'(x) = 2x · ln(x) + x2 · (1/x) = 2x ln(x) + x.

Step 4: Conclude result. Factored as x(2 ln x + 1) or 2x ln(x) + x (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Mistakenly cancels x2 · (1/x) as 1 instead of x.
  • ✗ (C)Differentiates numerator and denominator separately like a quotient.
  • ✗ (D)Forgets factor of 2 on the derivative of x2.

Question 022 · Unit 2: Differentiation: Definition & Fundamental Properties
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) √2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Recall derivative of sec(x). d/dx [sec(x)] = sec(x) tan(x).

Step 2: Evaluate trigonometric values at x = π/4.
sec(π/4) = 1 / cos(π/4) = 1 / (√2/2) = √2.
tan(π/4) = 1.

Step 3: Multiply to obtain the slope. y'(π/4) = sec(π/4) tan(π/4) = (√2)(1) = √2 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Squares √2 to obtain 2 (thinking of sec2(x) = tan'(x)).
  • ✗ (B)Evaluates only tan(π/4) = 1.
  • ✗ (D)Confuses sec(π/4) with cos(π/4) = √2/2.
Question 023 · Unit 3: Composite, Implicit, and Inverse Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) -192
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Identify outer and inner functions. Outer function u4, inner function u = 3x2 - 5.

Step 2: Apply the chain rule. h'(x) = 4(3x2 - 5)3 · d/dx [3x2 - 5] = 4(3x2 - 5)3 · (6x) = 24x(3x2 - 5)3.

Step 3: Evaluate at x = 1.
3(1)2 - 5 = 3 - 5 = -2.
h'(1) = 24(1)(-2)3 = 24(-8) = -192 (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets derivative of inner function 6x: 4(-2)3 = 4(-8) = -32.
  • ✗ (B)Sign error: (-2)3 = -8, but treats it as +8 yielding +192.
  • ✗ (C)Omits inner derivative and forgets negative sign.
Question 024 · Unit 3: Composite, Implicit, and Inverse Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 12
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State the chain rule for h(x) = f(g(x)). h'(x) = f'(g(x)) · g'(x).

Step 2: Evaluate inner function at x = 3. From the table, g(3) = 2.

Step 3: Evaluate f'(g(3)). f'(g(3)) = f'(2). From the table, f'(2) = -4.

Step 4: Find g'(3) and multiply. From the table, g'(3) = -3. Then h'(3) = f'(2) · g'(3) = (-4) · (-3) = 12 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Evaluates f'(3) · g'(3) = 6 · (-3) = -18 without evaluating g(3) = 2 first.
  • ✗ (C)Omits multiplying by g'(3) = -3, giving only f'(2) = -4.
  • ✗ (D)Sign error: multiplies (-4)(3) = -12.

Step 1: Express function as composite power. y = [sin(4x)]3.

Step 3: Differentiate inner trigonometric function. d/dx [sin(4x)] = cos(4x) · d/dx [4x] = 4 cos(4x).

Question 026 · Unit 3: Composite, Implicit, and Inverse Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) 4/3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State derivative of inverse function formula. g'(b) = 1 / f'(g(b)).

Step 2: Identify inverse mapping at b = 5. Since f(2) = 5, g(5) = 2.

Step 3: Evaluate derivative of f at x = 2. Given f'(2) = 3/4.

Step 4: Compute g'(5). g'(5) = 1 / f'(2) = 1 / (3/4) = 4/3 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Fails to take reciprocal: gives f'(2) = 3/4.
  • ✗ (B)Incorrectly negates the reciprocal as for perpendicular lines (-4/3).
  • ✗ (D)Negates original derivative (-3/4).
Question 027 · Unit 3: Composite, Implicit, and Inverse Functions
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) 6x / (1 + 9x4)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State chain rule for arctan(u). d/dx [arctan(u)] = (1 / (1 + u2)) · du/dx.

Step 2: Identify u and compute du/dx. u = 3x2, so du/dx = 6x.

Step 3: Square u. u2 = (3x2)2 = 9x4.

Step 4: Combine into single fraction. f'(x) = 6x / (1 + 9x4) (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets to square coefficient 3: writes 3x4 instead of 9x4.
  • ✗ (C)Omits chain rule factor du/dx = 6x in numerator.
  • ✗ (D)Confuses derivative of arctan with arcsin (which uses square root).

Question 028 · Unit 3: Composite, Implicit, and Inverse Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) -6/7
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Differentiate both sides with respect to x.
d/dx [x2] + d/dx [2xy] + d/dx [y3] = d/dx [9].

Step 2: Apply product and chain rules. 2x + [2y + 2x(dy/dx)] + 3y2(dy/dx) = 0.

Step 3: Substitute the point (x, y) = (2, 1).
2(2) + 2(1) + 2(2)(dy/dx) + 3(1)2(dy/dx) = 0
4 + 2 + 4(dy/dx) + 3(dy/dx) = 0
6 + 7(dy/dx) = 0.

Step 4: Solve for dy/dx. 7(dy/dx) = -6 ⇒ dy/dx = -6/7 (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Omits term 2x in the derivative: evaluates 2 + 7(dy/dx) = 0 ⇒ -2/7.
  • ✗ (B)Sign error: fails to move +6 to the other side with negative sign.
  • ✗ (C)Inverts the ratio as -7/6 instead of -6/7.
Question 029 · Unit 3: Composite, Implicit, and Inverse Functions
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) -5/8
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find first derivative dy/dx implicitly. 2x - 2y(dy/dx) = 0 ⇒ dy/dx = x/y. At (3, 2), dy/dx = 3/2.

Step 2: Differentiate dy/dx using quotient rule to find d2y/dx2.
d2y/dx2 = [ (1)(y) - x(dy/dx) ] / y2 = [ y - x(x/y) ] / y2 = [ y2 - x2 ] / y3.

Step 3: Use curve equation x2 - y2 = 5. Notice that y2 - x2 = -(x2 - y2) = -5.

Step 4: Substitute y = 2. d2y/dx2 = -5 / 23 = -5/8 (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Sign error: writes x2 - y2 = +5 in numerator instead of y2 - x2 = -5.
  • ✗ (C)Divides by y2 = 4 instead of y3 = 8.
  • ✗ (D)Reports first derivative dy/dx = 3/2.
Question 030 · Unit 3: Composite, Implicit, and Inverse Functions
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) -27 e-3x
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute first derivative. f'(x) = -3 e-3x.

Step 2: Compute second derivative. f''(x) = (-3)(-3) e-3x = 9 e-3x.

Step 3: Compute third derivative. f'''(x) = 9(-3) e-3x = -27 e-3x (Option C).

Step 4: Verify sign and power. (-3)3 = -27, so f'''(x) = -27 e-3x.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Sign error: calculates (-3)3 as +27.
  • ✗ (B)Stops at the second derivative multiplied by -1 or calculation error.
  • ✗ (D)Reports second derivative f''(x) = 9 e-3x.

Question 031 · Unit 3: Composite, Implicit, and Inverse Functions

Correct answer: (A) x^(sin x) [ cos(x) ln(x) + sin(x) / x ]
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Take natural logarithm of both sides. ln(y) = ln(x^(sin x)) = sin(x) ln(x).

Step 2: Differentiate implicitly with respect to x.
(1/y) (dy/dx) = d/dx [sin(x) ln(x)] = cos(x) ln(x) + sin(x) · (1/x).

Step 3: Multiply both sides by y. dy/dx = y [ cos(x) ln(x) + sin(x) / x ].

Step 4: Substitute y = x^(sin x). dy/dx = x^(sin x) [ cos(x) ln(x) + sin(x) / x ] (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Incorrectly applies power rule to a variable base with variable exponent.
  • ✗ (C)Forgets product rule second term sin(x)/x.
  • ✗ (D)Incorrect minus sign between terms.
Question 032 · Unit 3: Composite, Implicit, and Inverse Functions
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) 6x · 5^(3x2) ln(5)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State derivative rule for exponential base a. d/dx [a^u] = a^u · ln(a) · du/dx.

Step 2: Identify base a and exponent u. Here a = 5 and u = 3x2.

Step 3: Differentiate inner exponent u. du/dx = d/dx [3x2] = 6x.

Step 4: Combine factors. f'(x) = 5^(3x2) · ln(5) · (6x) = 6x · 5^(3x2) ln(5) (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Omits factor ln(5), confusing base 5 with base e.
  • ✗ (B)Treats 5 as variable and 3x2 as constant power.
  • ✗ (D)Combines power rule decrement with exponential rule erroneously.
Question 033 · Unit 4: Contextual Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 1 < t < 2 and t > 3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Understand condition for increasing speed. Speed is increasing when velocity v(t) and acceleration a(t) have the same sign (both positive or both negative).

Step 2: Find velocity function v(t). v(t) = x'(t) = 3t2 - 12t + 9 = 3(t2 - 4t + 3) = 3(t - 1)(t - 3).
v(t) > 0 on (0, 1) and (3, ∞); v(t) < 0 on (1, 3).

Step 3: Find acceleration function a(t). a(t) = v'(t) = 6t - 12 = 6(t - 2).
a(t) < 0 on (0, 2); a(t) > 0 on (2, ∞).

Step 4: Compare signs on intervals:
On (0, 1): v > 0, a < 0 (different signs → speed decreasing).
On (1, 2): v < 0, a < 0 (same sign: both negative → speed increasing!).
On (2, 3): v < 0, a > 0 (different signs → speed decreasing).
On (3, ∞): v > 0, a > 0 (same sign: both positive → speed increasing!).
Therefore, speed is increasing for 1 < t < 2 and t > 3 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Only looks at where velocity is positive.
  • ✗ (C)Gives interval where velocity is negative regardless of acceleration.
  • ✗ (D)Miscalculates sign changes of acceleration.

Question 034 · Unit 4: Contextual Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) 6.6, underestimate
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Write equation of tangent line at x = 4. L(x) = f(4) + f'(4)(x - 4) = 7 + (-2)(x - 4).

Step 2: Evaluate linear approximation at x = 4.2. L(4.2) = 7 - 2(4.2 - 4) = 7 - 2(0.2) = 7 - 0.4 = 6.6.

Step 3: Determine over/underestimate via concavity. Given f''(x) > 0 for all x, the graph of f is strictly concave up.
Since the graph of a concave up function lies entirely above its tangent lines, the tangent line approximation is an underestimate (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Correct numerical value 6.6, but incorrectly identifies tangent line as an overestimate.
  • ✗ (B)Arithmetic error in tangent slope: adds 0.4 instead of subtracting.
  • ✗ (C)Combines arithmetic error with wrong concavity reasoning.
Question 035 · Unit 4: Contextual Applications of Differentiation
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) 60π
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State relationship between area and radius. A = π r2.

Step 2: Differentiate with respect to time t. dA/dt = 2π r (dr/dt).

Step 3: Substitute given values. Given dr/dt = 3 m/min and r = 10 m.
dA/dt = 2π(10)(3) = 60π m2/min (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets factor of 2 from differentiating r2: writes π(10)(3) = 30π.
  • ✗ (C)Evaluates π r2 at r = 10 without rate of change.
  • ✗ (D)Multiplies area by dr/dt directly: 100π · 3 = 300π.
Question 036 · Unit 4: Contextual Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) 5/6 ft/sec
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Formulate geometric equation. By Pythagorean theorem, x2 + y2 = 132 = 169, where x is distance of base from wall and y is height on wall.

Step 2: Find y when x = 5. 52 + y2 = 169 ⇒ 25 + y2 = 169 ⇒ y2 = 144 ⇒ y = 12 ft.

Step 3: Differentiate with respect to t. 2x (dx/dt) + 2y (dy/dt) = 0 ⇒ x (dx/dt) + y (dy/dt) = 0.

Step 4: Substitute known values and solve for dy/dt. 5(2) + 12(dy/dt) = 0 ⇒ 10 + 12(dy/dt) = 0 ⇒ dy/dt = -10/12 = -5/6 ft/sec.
The rate at which it slides down is 5/6 ft/sec (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets factor 2 for dx/dt: 5(1)/12 = 5/12.
  • ✗ (B)Assumes the top slides at the same speed as the base (2 ft/sec).
  • ✗ (D)Inverts the ratio as 12/5.

Question 037 · Unit 4: Contextual Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) 2 / π m/min
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Relate radius r and height h using similar triangles. r / h = 4 / 12 = 1/3 ⇒ r = h/3.

Step 2: Express volume purely in terms of water depth h. V = (1/3)π (h/3)2 h = (1/3)π (h2 / 9) h = (π / 27) h3.

Step 3: Differentiate with respect to time t. dV/dt = (π / 27) · (3h2) · (dh/dt) = (π h2 / 9) (dh/dt).

Step 4: Substitute dV/dt = 8 and h = 6. 8 = (π (62) / 9) (dh/dt) = (36π / 9) (dh/dt) = 4π (dh/dt).
Solving gives dh/dt = 8 / (4π) = 2 / π m/min (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Omits factor 4: writes 8/π.
  • ✗ (B)Uses original radius 4 instead of similar triangles radius at h = 6.
  • ✗ (C)Calculation error in simplifying 8 / (4π).
Question 038 · Unit 4: Contextual Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 9/2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Check initial form at x = 0. Numerator: e⁰ - 1 - 0 = 0. Denominator: 02 = 0. Form is 0/0.

Step 2: Apply L'Hôpital's Rule (First derivative).
lim(x→0) d/dx [e3x - 1 - 3x] / d/dx [x2] = lim(x→0) (3e3x - 3) / (2x).
At x = 0, (3 - 3) / 0 = 0/0 (still indeterminate).

Step 3: Apply L'Hôpital's Rule (Second derivative).
lim(x→0) d/dx [3e3x - 3] / d/dx [2x] = lim(x→0) (9e3x) / 2.

Step 4: Evaluate the limit. Substituting x = 0 gives (9 · 1) / 2 = 9/2 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets denominator derivative factor 2, giving 9.
  • ✗ (C)Evaluates after only one application of L'Hôpital's rule without second step.
  • ✗ (D)Misses 32 = 9 in the second derivative, using 3/2 instead.
Question 039 · Unit 4: Contextual Applications of Differentiation
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) 0
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Check form as x → ∞. Numerator → ∞, Denominator → ∞ (Indeterminate form ∞/∞).

Step 2: Apply L'Hôpital's Rule once. lim(x→∞) (2x) / (2 e2x) = lim(x→∞) x / e2x (still ∞/∞).

Step 3: Apply L'Hôpital's Rule second time. lim(x→∞) 1 / (2 e2x).

Step 4: Evaluate the limit. As x → ∞, the denominator 2 e2x → ∞ while numerator is fixed at 1. Thus the limit is 0 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Ratio of coefficients 1/2 mistakenly deduced.
  • ✗ (B)Believes x2 and e2x grow at comparable rates.
  • ✗ (D)Assumes numerator polynomial power forces divergence.

Question 041 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) √3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Check conditions of Mean Value Theorem. f(x) is a polynomial, so it is continuous on [0, 3] and differentiable on (0, 3).

Step 2: Calculate average rate of change on [0, 3].
f(0) = 03 - 3(0) = 0.
f(3) = 33 - 3(3) = 27 - 9 = 18.
Avg rate = [f(3) - f(0)] / (3 - 0) = (18 - 0) / 3 = 6.

Step 3: Find derivative f'(x) and set equal to 6.
f'(x) = 3x2 - 3.
3c2 - 3 = 6 ⇒ 3c2 = 9 ⇒ c2 = 3 ⇒ c = ±√3.

Step 4: Select value inside (0, 3). Since c must lie in the open interval (0, 3), c = √3 ≈ 1.732 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Evaluates 3c2 - 3 = 0, which gives critical points c = 1, not MVT rate 6.
  • ✗ (C)Midpoint of the interval (0 + 3)/2 = 1.5.
  • ✗ (D)Approximate guess without exact square root.
Question 042 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) 37
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find critical points by setting f'(x) = 0. f'(x) = 6x2 - 6x - 12 = 6(x2 - x - 2) = 6(x - 2)(x + 1) = 0.
Critical points are x = 2 and x = -1, both of which lie inside [-2, 4].

Step 2: Evaluate f at critical points and interval endpoints:
f(-2) = 2(-8) - 3(4) - 12(-2) + 5 = -16 - 12 + 24 + 5 = 1.
f(-1) = 2(-1) - 3(1) - 12(-1) + 5 = -2 - 3 + 12 + 5 = 12.
f(2) = 2(8) - 3(4) - 12(2) + 5 = 16 - 12 - 24 + 5 = -15.
f(4) = 2(64) - 3(16) - 12(4) + 5 = 128 - 48 - 48 + 5 = 37.

Step 3: Compare values to find absolute maximum. The evaluated values are 1, 12, -15, and 37. The absolute maximum value is 37 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Local maximum at critical point x = -1, but not the global maximum on the interval.
  • ✗ (C)Value at x = 0 (y-intercept).
  • ✗ (D)Absolute minimum value on the interval (at x = 2).

Q043–Q045
Question 043 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) (-∞, 3)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute first derivative. f'(x) = 4x3 - 12x2 = 4x2(x - 3).

Step 2: Find partition numbers where f'(x) = 0. 4x2(x - 3) = 0 ⇒ x = 0 or x = 3.

Step 3: Analyze sign of f'(x) on test intervals:
For x < 0: 4x2 > 0 and (x - 3) < 0 ⇒ f'(x) < 0 (decreasing).
For 0 < x < 3: 4x2 > 0 and (x - 3) < 0 ⇒ f'(x) < 0 (decreasing).
For x > 3: 4x2 > 0 and (x - 3) > 0 ⇒ f'(x) > 0 (increasing).

Step 4: Combine intervals of decrease. Since f is continuous everywhere and f'(x) ≤ 0 for all x < 3 with f'(x) = 0 only at an isolated point x = 0, f is strictly decreasing on (-∞, 3) (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Misses interval (0, 3) where f is also decreasing.
  • ✗ (C)Only includes positive values of x where f is decreasing.
  • ✗ (D)Interval where f is increasing.
Question 044 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) x = 5
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Identify critical points of f. f'(x) = 0 at x = -3, x = 2, and x = 5.

Step 2: Analyze sign changes of f'(x) across each critical point:
Note that (x - 2)2 ≥ 0 for all x, so it never changes sign.
For x < -3: (x - 2)2 > 0, (x + 3) < 0, (x - 5) < 0 ⇒ f'(x) = (+) · (-) · (-) = +.
For -3 < x < 2: (x - 2)2 > 0, (x + 3) > 0, (x - 5) < 0 ⇒ f'(x) = (+) · (+) · (-) = -.
For 2 < x < 5: (x - 2)2 > 0, (x + 3) > 0, (x - 5) < 0 ⇒ f'(x) = (+) · (+) · (-) = -.
For x > 5: (x - 2)2 > 0, (x + 3) > 0, (x - 5) > 0 ⇒ f'(x) = (+) · (+) · (+) = +.

Step 3: Apply First Derivative Test:
At x = -3: f' changes from + to -, so f has a relative maximum.
At x = 2: f' does not change sign (- to -), so neither maximum nor minimum.
At x = 5: f' changes from - to +, so f has a relative minimum (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Relative maximum, because f' changes from positive to negative.
  • ✗ (B)Inflection point / saddle point because f' does not change sign.
  • ✗ (D)Incorrect; x = 5 is a valid relative minimum.
Question 045 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (D) The Second Derivative Test is inconclusive
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Evaluate f''(x) at the critical point x = 3.
f''(3) = 32 - 2(3) - 3 = 9 - 6 - 3 = 0.

Step 2: Recall conditions of the Second Derivative Test.
If f'(c) = 0 and f''(c) > 0, f has a relative minimum.
If f'(c) = 0 and f''(c) < 0, f has a relative maximum.
If f'(c) = 0 and f''(c) = 0, the Second Derivative Test is inconclusive.

Step 3: State conclusion. Because f''(3) = 0, the Second Derivative Test fails to provide a conclusion (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Would require f''(3) < 0.
  • ✗ (B)Would require f''(3) > 0.
  • ✗ (C)f''(3) = 0 is a necessary condition for an inflection point, but the Second Derivative Test itself only tests for local extrema and is inconclusive here.

Question 046 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) (-1, 1)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find first derivative. f'(x) = 4x3 - 12x.

Step 2: Find second derivative. f''(x) = 12x2 - 12 = 12(x2 - 1) = 12(x - 1)(x + 1).

Step 3: Set f''(x) < 0 for concave down behavior.
12(x - 1)(x + 1) < 0 holds when the factors have opposite signs, which occurs precisely when -1 < x < 1.

Step 4: Conclude interval. The graph of f is concave down on the interval (-1, 1) (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Interval where f'' > 0 (concave up).
  • ✗ (C)Interval where f'' > 0 (concave up).
  • ✗ (D)Evaluates roots of f'(x) = 0 (critical points x = ±√3) instead of f''(x) = 0.
Question 047 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 1
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Relate points of inflection to f'(x). An inflection point of f occurs where f''(x) changes sign, which corresponds to where f'(x) changes from increasing to decreasing or vice versa (local extrema of f').

Step 2: Examine graph of f'(x). The upper semi-circle f'(x) = √(9 - x2) starts at (-3, 0), increases strictly to a maximum value of 3 at x = 0, and then decreases strictly to 0 at x = 3.

Step 3: Identify slope changes of f'. For -3 < x < 0, f'(x) is increasing, so f''(x) > 0. For 0 < x < 3, f'(x) is decreasing, so f''(x) < 0.

Step 4: Count sign changes of f''. f''(x) changes sign from positive to negative exactly once, at x = 0. Therefore, the graph of f has exactly 1 point of inflection on (-3, 3) (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Believes since f'(x) ≥ 0 everywhere, there are no inflection points.
  • ✗ (C)Counts endpoints x = ±3 as inflection points.
  • ✗ (D)Counts roots and peaks together.
Question 048 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) 1800
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Set up perimeter constraint. Let x be the length of the side parallel to the wall, and y be the length of each of the two perpendicular sides.
Fencing constraint: x + 2y = 120 ⇒ x = 120 - 2y.

Step 2: Express area A as a function of y. A(y) = x · y = (120 - 2y)y = 120y - 2y2.

Step 3: Maximize A(y) by setting A'(y) = 0. A'(y) = 120 - 4y = 0 ⇒ 4y = 120 ⇒ y = 30 meters.
Then x = 120 - 2(30) = 60 meters.

Step 4: Verify maximum and compute area. A''(y) = -4 < 0 (concave down, confirms absolute maximum).
Maximum Area = x · y = 60 · 30 = 1800 m2 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Sets all 4 sides equal to each other (square of perimeter 120): (30)2 = 900.
  • ✗ (B)Divides 120 by 3: 40 × 40 = 1600.
  • ✗ (D)Multiplies 60 × 60 = 3600.

Question 049 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) Increasing at a decreasing rate
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Interpret sign of first derivative. f'(x) > 0 means the function values f(x) are strictly increasing.

Step 2: Interpret sign of second derivative. f''(x) < 0 means the slope f'(x) is strictly decreasing (concave down).

Step 3: Synthesize behavior. Since f is increasing but its rate of change (slope) is becoming less steep, f is increasing at a decreasing rate (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Corresponds to f' > 0 and f'' > 0.
  • ✗ (C)Corresponds to f' < 0 and f'' > 0.
  • ✗ (D)Corresponds to f' < 0 and f'' < 0.
Question 050 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) (3.5, √3.5)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Express squared distance D2 from (x, √x) to (4, 0).
D2 = (x - 4)2 + (√x - 0)2 = (x2 - 8x + 16) + x = x2 - 7x + 16 for x ≥ 0.

Step 2: Minimize f(x) = x2 - 7x + 16. f'(x) = 2x - 7 = 0 ⇒ x = 7/2 = 3.5.

Step 3: Confirm minimum. f''(x) = 2 > 0 (concave up, global minimum).

Step 4: Find coordinates. At x = 3.5, y = √3.5. Thus the closest point is (3.5, √3.5) (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Integer approximation rounded down.
  • ✗ (C)Vertical projection (4, 2), where D2 = 02 + 22 = 4, while at 3.5: (0.5)2 + 3.5 = 0.25 + 3.5 = 3.75 < 4.
  • ✗ (D)Arbitrary test point.
Question 051 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) 127.5
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State trapezoidal sum formula with variable subintervals.
Area = ∑ [ (f(x_{i-1}) + f(x_i)) / 2 ] · Δx_i.

Step 2: Calculate each trapezoidal area:
Subinterval 1: [0, 2], Δx = 2: [(4 + 7)/2] · 2 = 11.
Subinterval 2: [2, 5], Δx = 3: [(7 + 12)/2] · 3 = (19/2) · 3 = 28.5.
Subinterval 3: [5, 8], Δx = 3: [(12 + 18)/2] · 3 = (30/2) · 3 = 45.
Subinterval 4: [8, 10], Δx = 2: [(18 + 25)/2] · 2 = 43.

Step 3: Sum the areas. 11 + 28.5 + 45 + 43 = 127.5.

Step 4: Conclude result. 127.5 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Left Riemann sum gives: 4(2) + 7(3) + 12(3) + 18(2) = 101.
  • ✗ (B)Calculation error in averaging subintervals.
  • ✗ (D)Right Riemann sum gives: 7(2) + 12(3) + 18(3) + 25(2) = 154.

Question 052 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) ∫₂⁵ x4 dx

Step 1: Identify Δx and x_k. Here Δx = 3/n, and the sample point is x_k = a + k·Δx = 2 + k(3/n) = 2 + 3k/n.

Step 2: Identify the lower limit of integration a. Since x_0 = 2, lower limit a = 2.

Step 3: Identify the upper limit of integration b. b = a + n·Δx = 2 + n(3/n) = 2 + 3 = 5.

Step 4: Identify integrand f(x). f(x_k) = x_k4, so f(x) = x4.
Thus, the limit converts directly to ∫₂⁵ x4 dx (Option D).

Question 053 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) 19
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Use interval additivity property. ∫₁⁶ f(x) dx = ∫₁⁴ f(x) dx + ∫₄⁶ f(x) dx.

Step 2: Solve for ∫₁⁴ f(x) dx. 14 = ∫₁⁴ f(x) dx + (-3) ⇒ ∫₁⁴ f(x) dx = 14 - (-3) = 17.

Step 3: Apply linearity to evaluate the desired integral.
∫₁⁴ [ 2f(x) - 5 ] dx = 2 ∫₁⁴ f(x) dx - ∫₁⁴ 5 dx.

Step 4: Compute final value. 2(17) - 5(4 - 1) = 34 - 5(3) = 34 - 15 = 19 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets to subtract the constant integral: reports 2(17) = 34.
  • ✗ (C)Adds 15 instead of subtracting: 34 + 15 = 49.
  • ✗ (D)Reports only ∫₁⁴ f(x) dx = 17.
Question 054 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) 56
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Evaluate integral of odd function over symmetric interval [-5, 5]. Since f(x) is odd, ∫₋₅⁵ f(x) dx = 0. Therefore 3 ∫₋₅⁵ f(x) dx = 0.

Step 2: Evaluate integral of even function over symmetric interval [-5, 5]. Since g(x) is even, ∫₋₅⁵ g(x) dx = 2 ∫₀⁵ g(x) dx = 2(7) = 14.

Step 3: Combine terms. ∫₋₅⁵ [ 3f(x) + 4g(x) ] dx = 3(0) + 4(14) = 56 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets symmetry factor 2 for the even function: 4(7) = 28.
  • ✗ (C)Assumes both integrals evaluate to 0.
  • ✗ (D)Reports only ∫₋₅⁵ g(x) dx = 14.

STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Apply Fundamental Theorem of Calculus (Part 1) with chain rule. If g(x) = ∫_a^(u(x)) f(t) dt, then g'(x) = f(u(x)) · u'(x).

Step 2: Identify u(x) and f(t). u(x) = x2 ⇒ u'(x) = 2x, and f(t) = √(t3 + 1).

Step 3: Write expression for g'(x). g'(x) = √((x2)3 + 1) · (2x) = 2x √(x6 + 1).

Step 4: Evaluate at x = 2. g'(2) = 2(2) √(26 + 1) = 4 √(64 + 1) = 4√65 (Option C).

  • ✗ (B)Multiplies by x = 2 instead of 2x = 4.

Question 056 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) x = 3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Relate derivative of accumulation function to f. By FTC 1, g'(x) = f(x).

Step 2: Find where g has a relative maximum. By the First Derivative Test, g has a relative maximum where g'(x) = f(x) changes sign from positive to negative.

Step 3: Find zero of f(x) on the first line segment [0, 4]. Slope m = (-1 - 3) / (4 - 0) = -4/4 = -1. Line equation: f(x) = 3 - x. Setting f(x) = 0 gives 3 - x = 0 ⇒ x = 3.

Step 4: Check sign change of f(x) across x = 3. For x < 3, f(x) > 0. For 3 < x < 4, f(x) < 0. Thus f changes from positive to negative at x = 3 (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)x = 4 is a local minimum of f(x), but f'(4) is not relevant for extrema of g.
  • ✗ (B)x = 7 is where f changes from negative to positive (relative minimum of g).
  • ✗ (C)Endpoint.

Question 061 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change

STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Set up integration by parts. Let u = ln(x) and dv = x dx.
Then du = (1/x) dx, and v = x2 / 2.

Step 2: Apply parts formula. ∫ x ln(x) dx = (x2/2) ln(x) - ∫ (x2/2)(1/x) dx = (x2/2) ln(x) - (1/2) ∫ x dx = (x2/2) ln(x) - x2 / 4.

Step 3: Evaluate between limits 1 and e:

At x = 1: (12/2) ln(1) - 12/4 = 0 - 1/4 = -1/4.

Step 4: Subtract lower limit value. (e2/4) - (-1/4) = (e2 + 1) / 4 (Option C).

  • ✗ (B)Arithmetic error combining e2/2 and e2/4.
  • ✗ (D)Forgets to subtract the second integral ∫ v du.
Question 062 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (D) (e3x / 27) [ 9x2 - 6x + 2 ] + C
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Set up table of derivatives D and integrals I:
Row 1 (+): D = x2, I = e3x
Row 2 (-): D = 2x, I = (1/3) e3x
Row 3 (+): D = 2, I = (1/9) e3x
Row 4 (-): D = 0, I = (1/27) e3x.

Step 2: Form alternating diagonal products:
+ x2 · (1/3) e3x = (1/3) x2 e3x
- 2x · (1/9) e3x = -(2/9) x e3x
+ 2 · (1/27) e3x = (2/27) e3x.

Step 3: Factor out common term e3x / 27:
(e3x / 27) [ 9x2 - 6x + 2 ] + C (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Factors out 1/9 with incorrect powers of 3.
  • ✗ (B)Sign error on the middle term: +6x instead of -6x.

Question 063 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 3 ln(x - 2) + 2 ln(x - 1) + C
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Factor denominator. x2 - 3x + 2 = (x - 2)(x - 1).

Step 2: Set up partial fraction decomposition. (5x - 7) / [(x - 2)(x - 1)] = A / (x - 2) + B / (x - 1).
Multiplying clears fractions: 5x - 7 = A(x - 1) + B(x - 2).

Step 3: Solve for coefficients A and B via Heaviside cover-up:
Let x = 2: 5(2) - 7 = A(2 - 1) ⇒ 3 = A(1) ⇒ A = 3.
Let x = 1: 5(1) - 7 = B(1 - 2) ⇒ -2 = B(-1) ⇒ B = 2.

Step 4: Integrate separated terms. ∫ [ 3 / (x - 2) + 2 / (x - 1) ] dx = 3 ln|x - 2| + 2 ln|x - 1| + C.
Since x > 2, this is 3 ln(x - 2) + 2 ln(x - 1) + C (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Swaps coefficients A and B: writes 2 ln(x - 2) + 3 ln(x - 1).
  • ✗ (C)Erroneously uses original numerator coefficients 5 and -7.
  • ✗ (D)Incorrect signs and swapped variables.

Question 064 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change

Correct answer: (D) 1/2

Step 1: Express as limit with finite upper bound. lim(b→∞) ∫₁^b x⁻³ dx.

Step 2: Find antiderivative. ∫ x⁻³ dx = x⁻² / (-2) = -1 / (2x2).

Step 3: Evaluate at limits 1 and b. [ -1 / (2b2) ] - [ -1 / (2(1)2) ] = -1 / (2b2) + 1/2.

Step 4: Take the limit as b → ∞. lim(b→∞) [ -1 / (2b2) + 1/2 ] = 0 + 1/2 = 1/2 (Option D).

Question 065 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) 3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Identify infinite discontinuity at lower limit x = 0. Set up limit: lim(a→0⁺) ∫_a¹ x^(-2/3) dx.

Step 2: Find antiderivative. ∫ x^(-2/3) dx = x^(1/3) / (1/3) = 3 x^(1/3).

Step 3: Evaluate at limits a and 1. [ 3(1)^(1/3) ] - [ 3(a)^(1/3) ] = 3 - 3 a^(1/3).

Step 4: Take limit as a → 0⁺. lim(a→0⁺) [ 3 - 3 a^(1/3) ] = 3 - 0 = 3. The integral converges to 3 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Fails to multiply by reciprocal 3: writes 1/3.
  • ✗ (B)Arithmetic error evaluating 1^(1/3).
  • ✗ (D)Believes division by zero at x = 0 causes divergence (for ∫₀¹ 1/x^p dx, it converges when p < 1; here p = 2/3 < 1).

Question 068 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) y = 3 sin(2x)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Test y = 3 sin(2x).
y' = 6 cos(2x).
y'' = -12 sin(2x).

Step 2: Substitute into differential equation y'' + 4y:
-12 sin(2x) + 4(3 sin(2x)) = -12 sin(2x) + 12 sin(2x) = 0.

Step 3: Conclude verification. y = 3 sin(2x) satisfies the differential equation for all x (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)For y = 3 sin(4x): y'' = -48 sin(4x), so y'' + 4y = -48 sin(4x) + 12 sin(4x) = -36 sin(4x) ≠ 0.
  • ✗ (B)For y = e2x: y'' = 4 e2x, so y'' + 4y = 4e2x + 4e2x = 8e2x ≠ 0.
  • ✗ (D)Produces frequency 4 instead of 2.
Question 069 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) Along the line y = 1 and along the y-axis (x = 0)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Define condition for horizontal slope segments. Horizontal segments have slope dy/dx = 0.

Step 2: Set dy/dx = 0 in the differential equation. x(y - 1) = 0.

Step 3: Solve for all zero products. By the zero product property, dy/dx = 0 occurs whenever x = 0 (the y-axis) OR y = 1 (horizontal line y = 1).

Step 4: Conclude both loci. Horizontal line segments appear along both y = 1 and x = 0 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Omits the vertical line x = 0 (y-axis) where slope is also zero.
  • ✗ (C)Confuses y = 0 with y = 1.
  • ✗ (D)Confuses x = 1 with x = 0.

Q070–Q072
Question 070 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (D) dy/dx = x2 - 3x
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Interpret geometric property of slope field. If slopes are constant along vertical lines, then for any fixed value of x, the slope dy/dx does not change as y varies. Thus, dy/dx depends solely on x and is independent of y.

Step 2: Analyze options:
dy/dx = x2 - 3x contains only the variable x. For any fixed x, dy/dx is constant for all y.
dy/dx = y2 - 3y depends only on y (constant along horizontal lines).
dy/dx = x/y and dy/dx = x + y depend on both x and y.

Step 3: Conclude correct equation. dy/dx = x2 - 3x (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Slopes are constant along horizontal lines, not vertical lines.
  • ✗ (B)Slopes change as both x and y change.
  • ✗ (C)Slopes change along vertical lines because y varies.
Question 071 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) y = -1 / x2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Separate variables. (1 / y2) dy = 2x dx ⇒ y⁻² dy = 2x dx.

Step 2: Integrate both sides. ∫ y⁻² dy = ∫ 2x dx ⇒ -1/y = x2 + C.

Step 3: Apply initial condition y(1) = -1. -1 / (-1) = 12 + C ⇒ 1 = 1 + C ⇒ C = 0.

Step 4: Solve explicitly for y. -1/y = x2 ⇒ y = -1 / x2 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Calculation error for constant: sets C = 1.
  • ✗ (C)Forgets factor 2 integration: integrates 2x as 2x2.
  • ✗ (D)Neglects negative sign from integrating y⁻².
Question 072 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) y = √(x2 + 2x + 16)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Separate variables. y dy = (x + 1) dx.

Step 2: Integrate both sides. ∫ y dy = ∫ (x + 1) dx ⇒ y2 / 2 = x2 / 2 + x + C.

Step 3: Multiply by 2. y2 = x2 + 2x + C₁ where C₁ = 2C.

Step 4: Use initial condition (0, 4). 42 = 02 + 2(0) + C₁ ⇒ 16 = C₁.

Step 5: Solve for y with y > 0. y = √(x2 + 2x + 16) (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets square root on y.
  • ✗ (B)Fails to divide by 2 when integrating x, leaving 2x2.
  • ✗ (C)Adds constant outside the square root.

Q073–Q075
Question 073 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) ln(2) / 20
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Recall general solution for exponential decay. y(t) = y₀ e-kt.

Step 2: Apply half-life definition at t = 20. y(20) = y₀ / 2 ⇒ y₀ e-20k = y₀ / 2.

Step 3: Simplify and take natural logarithms. e-20k = 1/2 ⇒ -20k = ln(1/2) = -ln(2).

Step 4: Solve for k. 20k = ln(2) ⇒ k = ln(2) / 20 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Inverts the ratio as 20 / ln(2).
  • ✗ (B)Confuses arguments of the logarithm.
  • ✗ (D)Approximates 1/(2 · 20) = 1/40.
Question 074 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 2.75
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Set up first Euler step from x₀ = 1 to x₁ = 1.5 with Δx = 0.5.
Initial point: (x₀, y₀) = (1, 3).
Slope: m₀ = (dy/dx)|(1, 3) = 2(1) - 3 = 2 - 3 = -1.
y₁ = y₀ + m₀ · Δx = 3 + (-1)(0.5) = 3 - 0.5 = 2.5.

Step 2: Set up second Euler step from x₁ = 1.5 to x₂ = 2.0 with Δx = 0.5.
Current point: (x₁, y₁) = (1.5, 2.5).
Slope: m₁ = (dy/dx)|(1.5, 2.5) = 2(1.5) - 2.5 = 3.0 - 2.5 = 0.5.
y₂ = y₁ + m₁ · Δx = 2.5 + (0.5)(0.5) = 2.5 + 0.25 = 2.75.

Step 3: Conclude approximation. f(2) ≈ 2.75 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Value after only one step: y₁ = 2.50.
  • ✗ (C)Sign error in slope computation.
  • ✗ (D)Arithmetic error.
Question 075 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) Carrying capacity = 800, fastest growth at P = 400
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Identify carrying capacity from standard logistic equation.
Standard form: dP/dt = k P (1 - P/M), where M is the carrying capacity.
Here, M = 800.

Step 2: Determine population for maximum rate of growth.
The rate of growth dP/dt is a downward-opening quadratic function in P: f(P) = 0.04 P - (0.04/800) P2.
Its vertex occurs at P = M / 2 = 800 / 2 = 400.

Step 3: Conclude both values. Carrying capacity = 800, and maximum growth rate occurs at P = 400 (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)At P = 800, dP/dt = 0 (population growth stops, not fastest).
  • ✗ (B)Confuses carrying capacity with half-capacity.
  • ✗ (C)At P = 0, growth rate is zero.

Question 076 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) 500
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Identify equilibrium solutions of logistic differential equation. dP/dt = 0 when P = 0 or P = 500.

Step 2: Analyze stability for initial condition P(0) = 100. Since 0 < P(0) < 500, dP/dt > 0 for all t ≥ 0, so P(t) increases monotonically toward the stable carrying capacity M = 500.

Step 3: Evaluate long-term limit. lim(t→∞) P(t) = 500 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Point of inflection / maximum growth rate P = 250.
  • ✗ (B)Initial population P(0) = 100.

Question 077 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) 7
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Apply Net Change Theorem. s(3) = s(1) + ∫₁³ v(t) dt.

Step 2: Evaluate the definite integral of velocity:
∫₁³ (3t2 - 12) dt = [ t3 - 12t ]₁³ = [ (33 - 12(3)) ] - [ (13 - 12(1)) ] = [ 27 - 36 ] - [ 1 - 12 ] = -9 - (-11) = -9 + 11 = 2.

Step 3: Add initial position. s(3) = 5 + 2 = 7 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Reports only the displacement ∫₁³ v(t) dt = 2 without adding s(1) = 5.
  • ✗ (C)Arithmetic error: calculates 5 + 10 = 15.
  • ✗ (D)Ignores negative signs: evaluates 27 + 36 - 1 + 12.
Question 078 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) 13
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Recall definition of total distance. Total Distance = ∫₀⁵ |v(t)| dt.

Step 2: Find where particle changes direction (v(t) = 0). 6 - 2t = 0 ⇒ t = 3.

Step 3: Split integral at t = 3:
For 0 ≤ t ≤ 3, v(t) ≥ 0, so |v(t)| = 6 - 2t.
For 3 ≤ t ≤ 5, v(t) ≤ 0, so |v(t)| = -(6 - 2t) = 2t - 6.

Step 4: Evaluate each integral:
∫₀³ (6 - 2t) dt = [ 6t - t2 ]₀³ = 6(3) - 32 = 18 - 9 = 9.
∫₃⁵ (2t - 6) dt = [ t2 - 6t ]₃⁵ = (25 - 30) - (9 - 18) = -5 - (-9) = 4.

Step 5: Sum total distances. 9 + 4 = 13 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Net displacement ∫₀⁵ (6 - 2t) dt = 30 - 25 = 5.
  • ✗ (B)Distance traveled only while moving forward (t = 0 to 3).
  • ✗ (D)Distance traveled only while moving backward (t = 3 to 5).

Step 2: Evaluate definite integral over [0, 3]:
∫₀³ (3x2 - 2x + 1) dx = [ x3 - x2 + x ]₀³ = (33 - 32 + 3) - 0 = (27 - 9 + 3) = 21.

Question 080 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (A) 32/3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find points of intersection. x2 = 2x + 3 ⇒ x2 - 2x - 3 = 0 ⇒ (x - 3)(x + 1) = 0.
Intersection bounds: x = -1 and x = 3.

Step 2: Determine which curve is upper. On [-1, 3], choose test point x = 0: line y = 3 > parabola y = 0. Upper curve is 2x + 3, lower curve is x2.

Step 3: Set up area integral. Area = ∫₋₁³ [ (2x + 3) - x2 ] dx.

Step 4: Integrate and evaluate:
[ x2 + 3x - x3/3 ]₋₁³ = [ 32 + 3(3) - 27/3 ] - [ (-1)2 + 3(-1) - (-1)3/3 ] = [ 9 + 9 - 9 ] - [ 1 - 3 + 1/3 ] = 9 - [ -2 + 1/3 ] = 9 - (-5/3) = 9 + 5/3 = 32/3 (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Evaluates only from x = 0 to 3: 16/3.
  • ✗ (C)Forgets to subtract lower limit: doubles the area to 64/3.
  • ✗ (D)Neglects x3/3 term in antiderivative.
Question 081 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 256 / 15
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find limits of integration along x-axis. In the first quadrant, y = 4 - x2 intersects the x-axis where 4 - x2 = 0 ⇒ x = 2. Limits are x = 0 to x = 2.

Step 2: Determine side length s(x) of the square cross section. The base side is s(x) = y = 4 - x2.

Step 3: Express cross-sectional area A(x). For a square, A(x) = [s(x)]2 = (4 - x2)2 = 16 - 8x2 + x4.

Step 4: Integrate A(x) from 0 to 2 to find volume:
V = ∫₀² (16 - 8x2 + x4) dx = [ 16x - (8/3)x3 + x5/5 ]₀² = 16(2) - (8/3)(8) + 32/5 = 32 - 64/3 + 32/5.
Common denominator 15: (480 - 320 + 96) / 15 = 256 / 15 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Divides by 2 (semi-circle formula slip).
  • ✗ (C)Doubles the result for full symmetric parabola from -2 to 2.
  • ✗ (D)Integrates side s(x) rather than s(x)2.

Q082–Q084
Question 082 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) π
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Identify limits of integration. x varies from 0 to 4.

Step 2: Determine diameter d(x) and radius r(x). The diameter is the height of the region: d(x) = √x. Thus radius r(x) = d(x) / 2 = √x / 2.

Step 3: Express area of semicircle. A(x) = (1/2) π [r(x)]2 = (1/2) π (x / 4) = (π / 8) x.

Step 4: Integrate from 0 to 4. V = ∫₀⁴ (π / 8) x dx = (π / 8) [ x2 / 2 ]₀⁴ = (π / 8) · (16 / 2) = (π / 8) · 8 = π (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Uses full circle area formula instead of semicircle: 2π.
  • ✗ (B)Forgets to square the denominator 2 in r = d/2.
  • ✗ (D)Integrates diameter directly as radius.
Question 083 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) (π / 2) [ 1 - e⁻⁴ ]
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Set up disc method integral. V = π ∫_a^b [R(x)]2 dx.

Step 2: Identify radius R(x). R(x) = e-x - 0 = e-x.

Step 3: Square radius. [R(x)]2 = (e-x)2 = e-2x.

Step 4: Integrate from x = 0 to x = 2:
V = π ∫₀² e-2x dx = π [ e-2x / (-2) ]₀² = (π / (-2)) [ e⁻⁴ - e⁰ ] = -(π/2) [ e⁻⁴ - 1 ] = (π / 2) [ 1 - e⁻⁴ ] (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Fails to square e-x, integrating e-x instead of e-2x.
  • ✗ (B)Combines errors from linear integration.
  • ✗ (C)Multiplies by 2 instead of dividing by 2.
Question 084 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) π ∫₀¹ [ (2 - x2)2 - (2 - √x)2 ] dx
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find intersection points. √x = x2 ⇒ x = x4 ⇒ x(x3 - 1) = 0 ⇒ x = 0 and x = 1.

Step 2: Identify axis of rotation and radii. The axis is horizontal: y = 2.
Outer radius R(x): distance from axis y = 2 to the farther curve. On [0, 1], parabola y = x2 is lower than √x, so it is farther from y = 2. Thus R(x) = 2 - x2.
Inner radius r(x): distance from axis y = 2 to the closer curve y = √x. Thus r(x) = 2 - √x.

Step 3: Write washer method formula. V = π ∫₀¹ [ (R(x))2 - (r(x))2 ] dx = π ∫₀¹ [ (2 - x2)2 - (2 - √x)2 ] dx (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Inverts inner and outer radii, resulting in negative volume.
  • ✗ (C)Does not subtract from axis y = 2 correctly.
  • ✗ (D)Revolves around the x-axis (y = 0) rather than y = 2.

Question 085 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration

Question 086 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration

Question 088 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 3/2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find first derivative dy/dx in terms of t.
dx/dt = 2t, and dy/dt = 3t2 - 3.
dy/dx = (3t2 - 3) / (2t) = (3/2) t - (3/2) t⁻¹.

Step 2: State second derivative formula for parametric equations.
d2y/dx2 = [ d/dt (dy/dx) ] / (dx/dt).

Step 3: Differentiate dy/dx with respect to t:
d/dt [ (3/2) t - (3/2) t⁻¹ ] = 3/2 + (3/2) t⁻².
At t = 1, d/dt (dy/dx) = 3/2 + 3/2 = 3.

Step 4: Divide by dx/dt at t = 1.
(dx/dt)|_(t=1) = 2(1) = 2.
d2y/dx2 = 3 / 2 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets that (dx/dt) is in the denominator again, dividing by 4.
  • ✗ (C)Omits dividing by dx/dt = 2: reports only d/dt(dy/dx) = 3.
  • ✗ (D)Evaluates (d2y/dt2) / (d2x/dt2) = 6t / 2 = 3 at t=2.
Question 089 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) 2π

Step 2: Differentiate components:
dx/dt = -2 sin(2t).
dy/dt = 2 cos(2t).

Step 3: Compute sum of squares:
(dx/dt)2 + (dy/dt)2 = 4 sin2(2t) + 4 cos2(2t) = 4(sin2(2t) + cos2(2t)) = 4(1) = 4.

Step 4: Integrate constant speed over [0, π]:
L = ∫₀^π √4 dt = ∫₀^π 2 dt = 2(π - 0) = 2π (Option C).

  • ✗ (A)Omits factor 2 from chain rule: integrates 1 dt = π.
  • ✗ (B)Uses period 2π instead of interval length π.
  • ✗ (D)Divides by 2.

Question 092 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT

v_x(0) = 02 + C₁ = 3 ⇒ C₁ = 3 ⇒ v_x(t) = t2 + 3.

Question 093 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) ∫₀³ √(4t2 + t + 1) dt
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Recall total distance formula for planar motion. Distance = ∫_a^b √[ (dx/dt)2 + (dy/dt)2 ] dt.

Step 2: Square each velocity component:
(dx/dt)2 = (√(t + 1))2 = t + 1.
(dy/dt)2 = (2t)2 = 4t2.

Step 3: Form the integrand. √[ (dx/dt)2 + (dy/dt)2 ] = √(4t2 + t + 1).

Step 4: Form the definite integral. ∫₀³ √(4t2 + t + 1) dt (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Sums component velocities linearly instead of vector magnitude.
  • ✗ (B)Forgets to square 2 in (2t)2: writes 2t2.
  • ✗ (C)Omits square root on the integrand.

Question 094 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions

cos(2π/3) = -1/2.

Question 095 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) -2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State polar slope formula dy/dx.
dy/dx = (dy/dθ) / (dx/dθ) = [ (dr/dθ) sin θ + r cos θ ] / [ (dr/dθ) cos θ - r sin θ ].

Step 2: Evaluate r and dr/dθ at θ = π:
r(π) = 2 + sin(π) = 2 + 0 = 2.
dr/dθ = cos(θ) ⇒ (dr/dθ)|_(θ=π) = cos(π) = -1.

Step 3: Evaluate numerator dy/dθ at θ = π:
dy/dθ = (-1) sin(π) + (2) cos(π) = (-1)(0) + 2(-1) = -2.

Step 4: Evaluate denominator dx/dθ at θ = π:
dx/dθ = (-1) cos(π) - (2) sin(π) = (-1)(-1) - 2(0) = 1.

Step 5: Compute slope dy/dx. dy/dx = -2 / 1 = -2 (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Evaluates reciprocal 1/2 with wrong sign.
  • ✗ (B)Inverts slope to -1/2.
  • ✗ (C)Sign error in numerator: treats 2(-1) as +2.
Question 096 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) 2π
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find integration limits for one petal centered on the polar axis.
A petal starts and ends where r = 0:
4 cos(2θ) = 0 ⇒ 2θ = -π/2 and 2θ = π/2 ⇒ θ = -π/4 to θ = π/4.

Step 2: Set up polar area integral. Area = (1/2) ∫_(-π/4)^(π/4) r2 dθ = (1/2) ∫_(-π/4)^(π/4) [4 cos(2θ)]2 dθ.

Step 3: Simplify integrand using symmetry.
Area = 2 · (1/2) ∫₀^(π/4) 16 cos2(2θ) dθ = 16 ∫₀^(π/4) [ (1 + cos(4θ)) / 2 ] dθ = 8 ∫₀^(π/4) (1 + cos(4θ)) dθ.

Step 4: Integrate and evaluate:
8 [ θ + (1/4) sin(4θ) ]₀^(π/4) = 8 [ (π/4 + (1/4) sin(π)) - 0 ] = 8(π/4) = 2π (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Area of two petals (or fails to divide by 2 in 1/2 r2).
  • ✗ (B)Total area of all four petals: 4(2π) = 8π.
  • ✗ (D)Divides by 2 unnecessarily.

Question 097 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions

STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find points of intersection of the two curves.
3 sin(θ) = 1 + sin(θ) ⇒ 2 sin(θ) = 1 ⇒ sin(θ) = 1/2.
In [0, π], intersections occur at θ = π/6 and θ = 5π/6.

Step 2: Identify outer and inner polar curves. On [π/6, 5π/6], circle r = 3 sin(θ) has larger radius than cardioid r = 1 + sin(θ).
R_outer = 3 sin(θ), r_inner = 1 + sin(θ).

Step 3: State formula for area between two polar curves.
Area = (1/2) ∫_α^β [ (R_outer)2 - (r_inner)2 ] dθ.

Step 4: Substitute limits and curves. (1/2) ∫_(π/6)^(5π/6) [ (3 sin θ)2 - (1 + sin θ)2 ] dθ (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Inverts outer and inner curves, resulting in negative area.
  • ✗ (C)Incorrectly squares the difference (R - r)2 instead of the difference of squares (R2 - r2).
  • ✗ (D)Forgets factor of 1/2 from the polar area formula.
Question 098 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) 3/2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State relationship between series sum and partial sums. The sum S of an infinite series is defined as the limit of its sequence of partial sums S_n as n → ∞: S = lim(n→∞) S_n.

Step 2: Evaluate the limit as n → ∞. lim(n→∞) (3n2 + 5) / (2n2 - 1).

Step 3: Divide numerator and denominator by highest power n2:
lim(n→∞) (3 + 5/n2) / (2 - 1/n2) = (3 + 0) / (2 - 0) = 3/2.

Step 4: Conclude series sum. The series converges to 3/2 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Confuses lim(n→∞) a_n = 0 with the sum S.
  • ✗ (B)Ratio of constant terms 5 / (-1) or 5/2.
  • ✗ (D)Incorrectly concludes divergence.
Question 099 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) -2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Identify the first term a₁ (at n = 1). a₁ = 5 (-2/3)¹ = -10/3.

Step 2: Identify common ratio r. r = -2/3. Since |r| = |-2/3| = 2/3 < 1, the geometric series converges.

Step 3: Apply infinite geometric series sum formula. S = a₁ / (1 - r).

Step 4: Calculate numerical sum:
S = (-10/3) / [ 1 - (-2/3) ] = (-10/3) / (1 + 2/3) = (-10/3) / (5/3) = -10 / 5 = -2 (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Reports only the first term a₁ = -10/3.
  • ✗ (B)Evaluates 5 / (1 - (-2/3)) = 5 / (5/3) = 3 or arithmetic slip.
  • ✗ (C)Evaluates sum if first term were 5.

Q100–Q102
Question 100 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) 1
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Write out the first few terms of the sequence of partial sums S_N:
n = 1: (1/1 - 1/3)
n = 2: (1/3 - 1/5)
n = 3: (1/5 - 1/7)
...
n = N: (1/(2N - 1) - 1/(2N + 1)).

Step 2: Notice telescoping cancellation. The -1/3 cancels with +1/3, -1/5 cancels with +1/5, etc.
All intermediate terms cancel completely, leaving: S_N = 1 - 1 / (2N + 1).

Step 3: Take the limit as N → ∞. lim(N→∞) S_N = lim(N→∞) [ 1 - 1 / (2N + 1) ] = 1 - 0 = 1 (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Divides by 2 (difference in denominator).
  • ✗ (C)Adds first terms.
  • ✗ (D)Assumes all terms cancel to 0.
Question 101 · Unit 1: Limits & Continuity
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) 6
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Apply limit laws to polynomial terms under the radical. Since the square root function is continuous on [0, ∞), the limit passes inside the radical:
lim(x→2) √( [f(x)]2 + [g(x)]2 + 2 ) = √( lim(x→2) [f(x)]2 + lim(x→2) [g(x)]2 + 2 ).

Step 2: Substitute individual function limits.
lim(x→2) [f(x)]2 = 52 = 25.
lim(x→2) [g(x)]2 = (-3)2 = 9.

Step 3: Evaluate radicand. 25 + 9 + 2 = 36.

Step 4: Take the square root. √36 = 6 (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Forgets the constant +2 under the square root: √(25 + 9) = √34.
  • ✗ (C)Arithmetic error evaluating 25 - 9 = 16 ⇒ √16 = 4.
  • ✗ (D)Overestimates arithmetic sum.
Question 102 · Unit 1: Limits & Continuity
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) a = 2, b = 3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Apply continuity condition at x = 1 for the left piece.
lim(x→1⁻) f(x) = f(1) ⇒ a(1) + 3 = 5 ⇒ a + 3 = 5 ⇒ a = 2.

Step 2: Apply continuity condition at x = 1 for the right piece.
lim(x→1⁺) f(x) = f(1) ⇒ 2(1)2 + b(1) = 5 ⇒ 2 + b = 5 ⇒ b = 3.

Step 3: Verify two-sided continuity. With a = 2 and b = 3, lim(x→1⁻) f(x) = 5 = lim(x→1⁺) f(x) = f(1). Thus f is continuous everywhere on (-∞, ∞) (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Swaps the values of a and b.
  • ✗ (C)Fails to equate to f(1) = 5 correctly.
  • ✗ (D)Arithmetic slip solving 2 + b = 5.

Q103–Q105
Question 103 · Unit 2: Differentiation: Definition & Fundamental Properties
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) f(x) = x^(2/3)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Test continuity at x = 0 for f(x) = x^(2/3). f(0) = 0^(2/3) = 0 and lim(x→0) x^(2/3) = 0, so f is continuous at x = 0.

Step 2: Test differentiability using the limit of the difference quotient:
f'(0) = lim(h→0) [h^(2/3) - 0] / h = lim(h→0) h^(-1/3) = lim(h→0) 1 / (∛h).
As h → 0⁺, 1/(∛h) → +∞, and as h → 0⁻, 1/(∛h) → -∞.
Since the limit is infinite and one-sided limits disagree, f has a cusp at x = 0 and is not differentiable.

Step 3: Verify Option C. f(x) = x^(2/3) is continuous but not differentiable at x = 0 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)f(x) = x2 is smooth and differentiable everywhere: f'(0) = 0.
  • ✗ (B)f(x) = 1/x is not continuous at x = 0.
  • ✗ (D)f(x) = |x|³ has derivative f'(x) = 3x|x|, which is differentiable at x = 0 with f'(0) = 0.
Question 104 · Unit 2: Differentiation: Definition & Fundamental Properties
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (D) 48
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Recognize derivative definition form. lim(x→a) [f(x) - f(a)] / (x - a) = f'(a).

Step 2: Identify function and point. f(x) = x3 and a = 4. Note that f(4) = 43 = 64.

Step 3: Compute derivative f'(x). f'(x) = d/dx [x3] = 3x2.

Step 4: Evaluate derivative at a = 4. f'(4) = 3(42) = 3(16) = 48 (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Evaluates 42 = 16.
  • ✗ (B)Arithmetic error: 2(16) = 32.
  • ✗ (C)Reports original value f(4) = 64.

Step 2: Find g(7). From the table, f(3) = 7, which means g(7) = 3.

Step 3: Evaluate f' at the corresponding input x = 3. From the table, f'(3) = 5.

Step 4: Compute the reciprocal. g'(7) = 1 / f'(3) = 1 / 5 (Option A).

Question 106 · Unit 3: Composite, Implicit, and Inverse Functions
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) 2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find first derivative using chain rule. f(x) = (2x2 + 1)^(1/2).
f'(x) = (1/2)(2x2 + 1)^(-1/2) · (4x) = 2x / √(2x2 + 1).

Step 2: Find second derivative using quotient rule:
f''(x) = [ 2 √(2x2 + 1) - 2x · (2x / √(2x2 + 1)) ] / (2x2 + 1).

Step 3: Evaluate at x = 0:
Numerator: 2 √(0 + 1) - 0 = 2(1) = 2.
Denominator: 2(0)2 + 1 = 1.

Step 4: Compute f''(0). f''(0) = 2 / 1 = 2 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Arithmetic slip dividing by 2.
  • ✗ (C)Confuses f'(0) = 0 with f''(0).
  • ✗ (D)Multiplies by 2 again: 4.
Question 107 · Unit 3: Composite, Implicit, and Inverse Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) y - 2 = -(3/8)(x - 3)

Step 1: Differentiate implicitly with respect to x. 2x + 8y(dy/dx) = 0.

Step 2: Solve for dy/dx. dy/dx = -2x / (8y) = -x / (4y).

Step 3: Evaluate slope m at point (3, 2). m = -3 / (4 · 2) = -3/8.

Step 4: Write point-slope equation of tangent line. y - 2 = -(3/8)(x - 3) (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets factor 2 for y coordinate: writes -3/4.
  • ✗ (C)Sign error: uses positive slope +3/8.
  • ✗ (D)Inverts slope: -8/3.
Question 108 · Unit 3: Composite, Implicit, and Inverse Functions

Step 2: Identify u and compute du/dx. u = 4x ⇒ du/dx = 4.

Question 109 · Unit 2: Differentiation: Definition & Fundamental Properties
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) (3x - 1) / (2 x^(3/2))
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Split fraction into separate power terms. y = 3x / x^(1/2) + 1 / x^(1/2) = 3 x^(1/2) + x^(-1/2).

Step 2: Differentiate term-by-term using power rule:
dy/dx = 3(1/2) x^(-1/2) + (-1/2) x^(-3/2) = (3/2) x^(-1/2) - (1/2) x^(-3/2).

Step 3: Combine over a common denominator 2 x^(3/2):
dy/dx = [ 3x - 1 ] / (2 x^(3/2)) (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Ignores second term derivative.
  • ✗ (C)Sign error on negative exponent: +1 instead of -1.
  • ✗ (D)Incorrect power in denominator.
Question 110 · Unit 2: Differentiation: Definition & Fundamental Properties
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) x = -3 and x = 1
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find f'(x) using product rule. f'(x) = (2x) ex + (x2 - 3) ex = (x2 + 2x - 3) ex.

Step 2: Set f'(x) = 0 for horizontal tangent. (x2 + 2x - 3) ex = 0.

Step 3: Solve for x. Since ex > 0 for all real numbers, we must have x2 + 2x - 3 = 0.
Factoring gives (x + 3)(x - 1) = 0 ⇒ x = -3 or x = 1 (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Sign errors in factoring: (x - 3)(x + 1) = 0.
  • ✗ (C)Roots of f(x) = 0 (x = ±√3) rather than f'(x) = 0.
  • ✗ (D)Value where 2x = 0 only.
Question 111 · Unit 4: Contextual Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 0.096 rad/sec
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Relate angle θ to rocket height y. tan(θ) = y / 3000.

Step 2: Differentiate with respect to time t. sec2(θ) (dθ/dt) = (1 / 3000) (dy/dt).

Step 3: Evaluate sec(θ) when y = 4000. By Pythagorean theorem, the hypotenuse is √(30002 + 40002) = 5000 ft.
cos(θ) = 3000 / 5000 = 3/5 ⇒ sec(θ) = 5/3 ⇒ sec2(θ) = 25/9.

Step 4: Solve for dθ/dt with dy/dt = 800:
(25/9) (dθ/dt) = 800 / 3000 = 8 / 30 = 4 / 15.
dθ/dt = (4 / 15) · (9 / 25) = 36 / 375 = 0.096 rad/sec (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets sec2(θ) factor: evaluates (4/15) · (3/5) = 0.160.
  • ✗ (C)Omits trigonometric conversion entirely.
  • ✗ (D)Multiplies by cos(θ) instead of cos2(θ).

Question 112 · Unit 4: Contextual Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) 8/3 ft/sec
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Set up similar triangles. Let x be distance of person from light, and s be length of shadow.
Total distance from base of light to tip of shadow is x + s.
By similar triangles: 15 / (x + s) = 6 / s.

Step 2: Cross-multiply and simplify. 15s = 6(x + s) = 6x + 6s ⇒ 9s = 6x ⇒ s = (2/3) x.

Step 3: Differentiate with respect to time t. ds/dt = (2/3) (dx/dt).

Step 4: Substitute walking speed dx/dt = 4 ft/sec. ds/dt = (2/3)(4) = 8/3 ft/sec (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Inverts the ratio as 3/8 ft/sec.
  • ✗ (B)Assumes shadow grows at the same rate as walking speed (4 ft/sec).
  • ✗ (D)Rate at which the tip of the shadow moves: dx/dt + ds/dt = 4 + 8/3 = 20/3 ft/sec.
Question 113 · Unit 4: Contextual Applications of Differentiation
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (D) 18 mph
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Define coordinate positions and distance z. Let x be distance of Car A to intersection (decreasing: dx/dt = -50 mph).
Let y be distance of Car B from intersection (increasing: dy/dt = +60 mph).
Distance formula: z2 = x2 + y2.

Step 2: Find distance z at given instant. When x = 0.3 and y = 0.4: z = √(0.32 + 0.42) = √(0.09 + 0.16) = √0.25 = 0.5 miles.

Step 3: Differentiate with respect to time t. 2z (dz/dt) = 2x (dx/dt) + 2y (dy/dt) ⇒ z (dz/dt) = x (dx/dt) + y (dy/dt).

Step 4: Substitute known values:
0.5 (dz/dt) = (0.3)(-50) + (0.4)(60) = -15 + 24 = 9.
dz/dt = 9 / 0.5 = 18 mph (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets Car A's contribution: 24/0.5 = 48 or arithmetic error.
  • ✗ (B)Sign error: uses dx/dt = +50 and dy/dt = -60.
  • ✗ (C)Multiplies speeds without coordinate components.
Question 114 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 2 inches
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Express dimensions in terms of corner cut x. Height = x, Length = 12 - 2x, Width = 12 - 2x for 0 < x < 6.

Step 2: Write volume formula. V(x) = x(12 - 2x)2 = x(144 - 48x + 4x2) = 4x3 - 48x2 + 144x.

Step 3: Differentiate and set to zero. V'(x) = 12x2 - 96x + 144 = 12(x2 - 8x + 12) = 12(x - 2)(x - 6) = 0.
Critical points are x = 2 and x = 6.

Step 4: Confirm maximum on (0, 6). At x = 6, volume is 0. At x = 2: V(2) = 2(8)2 = 128 in3.
By First/Second Derivative Test (V''(2) = 24(2) - 96 = -48 < 0), x = 2 maximizes volume (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Guess based on 12/4 = 3.
  • ✗ (C)Midpoint of possible cuts.
  • ✗ (D)Arbitrary test cut.
