Q115–Q117
Question 115 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) 12
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Place triangle in coordinate plane. Vertices at (0, 0), (6, 0), and (0, 8). Hypotenuse line connects (6, 0) and (0, 8):
Slope m = (8 - 0)/(0 - 6) = -4/3. Equation: y = -(4/3)x + 8.

Step 2: Express rectangle area with corner (x, y) on hypotenuse. A(x) = x · y = x [ -(4/3)x + 8 ] = -(4/3)x2 + 8x.

Step 3: Maximize A(x). A'(x) = -(8/3)x + 8 = 0 ⇒ (8/3)x = 8 ⇒ x = 3.
Then y = -(4/3)(3) + 8 = -4 + 8 = 4.

Step 4: Calculate maximum area. A = x · y = 3 · 4 = 12 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Half of the triangle's area (1/2 · 6 · 8 = 24).
  • ✗ (B)Area using dimensions 4 × 4 = 16.
  • ✗ (D)3 × 6 = 18.
Question 116 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) f is not differentiable on the open interval (0, 2)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Check hypotheses of Rolle's Theorem:
1. f must be continuous on [a, b].
2. f must be differentiable on (a, b).
3. f(a) = f(b).

Step 2: Test continuity. f(x) = 1 - |x - 1| is continuous everywhere on [0, 2].

Step 3: Test differentiability. The absolute value function has a sharp corner at x = 1, where left derivative is +1 and right derivative is -1.
Thus f'(1) does not exist, so f is not differentiable on (0, 2).

Step 4: Conclude reason. Hypothesis 2 fails: f is not differentiable on (0, 2) (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)f is continuous everywhere on [0, 2].
  • ✗ (C)f(0) = 1 - 1 = 0 and f(2) = 1 - 1 = 0, so f(0) = f(2).
  • ✗ (D)f'(1) does not exist, so f'(c) is never equal to 0 anywhere in (0, 2).
Question 117 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) 4
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find critical points on (1, 5). f'(x) = 1 - 4/x2 = 0 ⇒ x2 = 4 ⇒ x = 2 (since x ∈ [1, 5]).

Step 2: Evaluate f at critical point and endpoints:
Endpoint x = 1: f(1) = 1 + 4/1 = 5.
Critical point x = 2: f(2) = 2 + 4/2 = 2 + 2 = 4.
Endpoint x = 5: f(5) = 5 + 4/5 = 5.8.

Step 3: Compare candidate values. The evaluated values are 5, 4, and 5.8. The absolute minimum value is 4 (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Value at endpoint x = 1.
  • ✗ (B)Absolute maximum value on the interval (at x = 5).
  • ✗ (C)The x-coordinate of the minimum, not the minimum function value.

Q118–Q120
Question 118 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) 1
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute first derivative. f'(x) = 5x4 - 20x3.

Step 2: Compute second derivative. f''(x) = 20x3 - 60x2 = 20x2(x - 3).

Step 3: Find zeros of f''(x). 20x2(x - 3) = 0 ⇒ x = 0 or x = 3.

Step 4: Check for sign changes of f''(x):
For x < 0: 20x2 > 0, (x - 3) < 0 ⇒ f''(x) < 0.
For 0 < x < 3: 20x2 > 0, (x - 3) < 0 ⇒ f''(x) < 0 (no sign change at x = 0!).
For x > 3: 20x2 > 0, (x - 3) > 0 ⇒ f''(x) > 0 (sign changes from - to + at x = 3).
Therefore, there is only 1 point of inflection, located at x = 3 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Incorrectly assumes both zeros of f''(x) (x = 0 and x = 3) are inflection points.
  • ✗ (C)Counts degree of polynomial minus 2.
  • ✗ (D)Concludes no inflection points.
Question 119 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) f has an inflection point at x = 4
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Analyze sign of f''(x) across x = 4. The graph of f'' is a line with positive slope 2 passing through (4, 0): f''(x) = 2(x - 4).
For x < 4, f''(x) < 0 (concave down).
For x > 4, f''(x) > 0 (concave up).

Step 2: Identify definition of inflection point. Since f''(x) changes sign from negative to positive at x = 4, the concavity of f changes at x = 4.

Step 3: Combine with f'(4) = 0. f has a horizontal tangent at an inflection point (inflection point with horizontal tangent) (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)For a relative minimum, f'' would need to be strictly positive at x = 4, but f''(4) = 0.
  • ✗ (B)For a relative maximum, f'' would need to be negative.
  • ✗ (D)f is twice differentiable, so it is well-defined.

Step 2: Apply L'Hôpital's Rule. Differentiate numerator and denominator with respect to x:
Numerator derivative: d/dx [ln x] = 1/x.
Denominator derivative: d/dx [x2 + 2x - 3] = 2x + 2.

Step 3: Evaluate new limit as x → 1. lim(x→1) (1/x) / (2x + 2) = (1/1) / (2(1) + 2) = 1 / 4 (Option D).

Question 121 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) (1 + √37) / 3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute the average value of f on [0, 4].
f_avg = (1 / (4 - 0)) ∫04 (3x2 - 2x + 1) dx.

Step 2: Evaluate the definite integral.
∫04 (3x2 - 2x + 1) dx = [x3 - x2 + x]04 = (64 - 16 + 4) - 0 = 52.
Thus, f_avg = 52 / 4 = 13.

Step 3: Set f(c) = f_avg and solve for c in [0, 4]:
3c2 - 2c + 1 = 13 ⇒ 3c2 - 2c - 12 = 0.
By the quadratic formula: c = [2 ± √(4 - 4(3)(-12))] / 6 = [2 ± √148] / 6 = (1 ± √37) / 3.

Step 4: Check domain constraints.
Since c must lie in [0, 4], and (1 - √37)/3 < 0, the only valid solution is c = (1 + √37) / 3 ≈ 2.36 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Evaluates the midpoint c = 2 without solving 3c2 - 2c + 1 = 13.
  • ✗ (B)Arithmetic error under the radical using -12 instead of +144.
  • ✗ (D)Confuses average value with derivative critical number.
Question 122 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 5
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Identify all critical points of f(x) on [-3, 3].
The absolute value expression x2 - 4 changes sign at x = -2 and x = 2, where f'(x) does not exist (sharp corners/cusps).
Inside (-2, 2), f(x) = 4 - x2 ⇒ f'(x) = -2x = 0 at x = 0.
Thus, the critical points in (-3, 3) are x = -2, 0, and 2.

Step 2: Test all candidates (endpoints and critical numbers):
f(-3) = |(-3)2 - 4| = |9 - 4| = 5.
f(-2) = |4 - 4| = 0.
f(0) = |0 - 4| = 4.
f(2) = |4 - 4| = 0.
f(3) = |32 - 4| = |9 - 4| = 5.

Step 3: Determine the absolute maximum.
Comparing the values {0, 4, 5}, the maximum value is 5, achieved at the endpoints x = -3 and x = 3 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Selects the local maximum f(0) = 4, failing to test the interval endpoints.
  • ✗ (C)Evaluates 32 without subtracting 4.
  • ✗ (D)Reports the absolute minimum value instead of the maximum.
Question 123 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) 2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Expand and calculate the first derivative.
f(x) = x4/3 + 4x1/3.
f'(x) = (4/3) x1/3 + (4/3) x-2/3 = (4/3) x-2/3 (x + 1).

Step 2: Calculate the second derivative.
f''(x) = (4/9) x-2/3 - (8/9) x-5/3 = (4/9) x-5/3 (x - 2).

Step 3: Analyze potential inflection points where f''(x) = 0 or does not exist.
f''(x) = 0 at x = 2.
f''(x) is undefined at x = 0 (vertical tangent in original continuous function).

Step 4: Test concavity signs across the partition intervals:
For x < 0: x-5/3 < 0 and (x - 2) < 0 ⇒ f''(x) > 0 (concave up).
For 0 < x < 2: x-5/3 > 0 and (x - 2) < 0 ⇒ f''(x) < 0 (concave down).
For x > 2: x-5/3 > 0 and (x - 2) > 0 ⇒ f''(x) > 0 (concave up).
Since concavity changes at both continuous points x = 0 and x = 2, there are exactly 2 points of inflection (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Erroneously assumes fractional powers cannot produce points of inflection.
  • ✗ (B)Omits x = 0 because f''(0) does not exist, forgetting that inflection points only require continuity and concavity change.
  • ✗ (D)Counts x = -1 (a local minimum of f, where f'(x) = 0) as an inflection point.

Question 124 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (A) 2x cos(x4) - 3 cos(9x2)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State the Leibniz Rule for differentiating integrals with variable limits.
d/dx ∫u(x)v(x) f(t) dt = f(v(x)) · v'(x) - f(u(x)) · u'(x).

Step 2: Identify the upper bound and its derivative.
v(x) = x2 ⇒ v'(x) = 2x.
f(v(x)) = cos((x2)2) = cos(x4).

Step 3: Identify the lower bound and its derivative.
u(x) = 3x ⇒ u'(x) = 3.
f(u(x)) = cos((3x)2) = cos(9x2).

Step 4: Combine using the Leibniz Rule:
F'(x) = cos(x4) · (2x) - cos(9x2) · (3) = 2x cos(x4) - 3 cos(9x2) (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Forgets the chain rule factors v'(x) = 2x and u'(x) = 3.
  • ✗ (C)Fails to square the inputs inside the cosine function.
  • ✗ (D)Differentiates the integrand cosine to sine, confusing FTC with direct differentiation.
Question 125 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) 7 - π/2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Apply the Candidates Test on [0, 6].
g'(x) = f(x). Critical numbers occur where f(x) = 0: x = 2 and x = 4. Endpoints are x = 0 and x = 6.

Step 2: Evaluate g(x) at each candidate:
• At x = 0: g(0) = 0.
• At x = 2: g(2) = Area of triangle = (1/2)(base)(height) = (1/2)(2)(3) = 3.
• At x = 4: g(4) = g(2) - Area of semicircle = 3 - (1/2)π(1)2 = 3 - π/2 ≈ 1.43.
• At x = 6: g(6) = g(4) + Area of triangle = (3 - π/2) + (1/2)(2)(4) = 3 - π/2 + 4 = 7 - π/2 ≈ 5.43.

Step 3: Compare candidate values.
g(0) = 0
g(2) = 3
g(4) ≈ 1.43
g(6) = 7 - π/2 ≈ 5.43.
The absolute maximum value is 7 - π/2 at x = 6 (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Selects the local maximum g(2) = 3 without checking the right endpoint x = 6.
  • ✗ (B)Omits the negative semicircle area below the axis: 3 + 4 = 7.
  • ✗ (C)Reports the local minimum value g(4) = 3 - π/2.
Question 126 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) 2400 (1 - e-1) liters
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Set up the Net Change integral.
Total volume leaked = ∫020 R(t) dt = ∫020 120 e-0.05t dt.

Step 2: Find the antiderivative.
∫ 120 e-0.05t dt = 120 / (-0.05) e-0.05t = -2400 e-0.05t.

Step 3: Apply the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus:
[-2400 e-0.05t]020 = -2400 (e-0.05(20) - e0) = -2400 (e-1 - 1) = 2400 (1 - e-1) liters (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Forgets to divide by the exponential constant k = -0.05, keeping the coefficient 120.
  • ✗ (C)Evaluates only the upper bound without subtracting the lower bound at t = 0.
  • ✗ (D)Multiplies by 0.05 instead of dividing by 0.05 (120 × 5 = 600).

Question 128 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) 42
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Split the integrand into odd and even components.
∫-33 (x5 - 4x3 + 2x + 7) dx = ∫-33 (x5 - 4x3 + 2x) dx + ∫-33 7 dx.

Step 2: Apply the symmetric integral property for odd functions.
Let g(x) = x5 - 4x3 + 2x. Since g(-x) = (-x)5 - 4(-x)3 + 2(-x) = -(x5 - 4x3 + 2x) = -g(x), g is an odd function.
The integral of any continuous odd function over a symmetric interval [-a, a] is zero:
∫-33 (x5 - 4x3 + 2x) dx = 0.

Step 3: Evaluate the constant component:
∫-33 7 dx = 7 · [3 - (-3)] = 7 · 6 = 42 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Assumes the entire integrand is odd, incorrectly getting 0.
  • ✗ (B)Evaluates 7 × 3 = 21 over half the interval [0, 3] without doubling.
  • ✗ (D)Doubles 42 to 84 by mistaking the length of the interval as 12.
Question 129 · Unit 5: Analytical Applications of Differentiation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) The tangent line to the graph of f at x = 3 is horizontal.
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Interpret the given condition f'(3) = 0.
By definition, the slope of the tangent line to y = f(x) at x = 3 is f'(3). Since f'(3) = 0, the tangent line is perfectly horizontal with equation y = f(3). This is guaranteed to be true (Option C).

Step 2: Analyze the second derivative condition f''(3) = 0.
When f'(c) = 0 and f''(c) = 0, the Second Derivative Test is inconclusive.

Step 3: Test counterexamples for the other options:
• For f(x) = (x - 3)4: f'(3) = 0, f''(3) = 0, relative minimum (no inflection point).
• For f(x) = -(x - 3)4: relative maximum (no inflection point).
• For f(x) = (x - 3)3: point of inflection (no relative extremum).
Thus, neither A nor B is necessarily true, and concavity behavior for x > 3 depends entirely on higher derivatives.

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)False: f(x) = (x - 3)3 has no extremum at x = 3.
  • ✗ (B)False: f(x) = (x - 3)4 has a local minimum, not an inflection point.
  • ✗ (D)False: f(x) = -(x - 3)4 is concave down everywhere.

Question 130 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) (0, 2)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Differentiate the accumulation function g(x).
By the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus Part 1, g'(x) = f(x).

Step 2: Relate concavity of g to higher derivatives.
The concavity of g is determined by g''(x). Differentiating again gives g''(x) = f'(x).

Step 3: Identify where g''(x) < 0.
The graph of g is concave down if and only if g''(x) < 0, which corresponds precisely to f'(x) < 0.
We are given that f'(x) < 0 on the interval (0, 2).
Therefore, g is concave down on (0, 2) (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Represents where g''(x) = f'(x) > 0, so g is concave UP on (2, 5).
  • ✗ (B)Conflates concavity with the entire domain.
  • ✗ (C)Arbitrary interval around the transition point x = 2.
Question 131 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) A/x + B/(x - 2) + C/(x - 2)2 + (Dx + E)/(x2 + 9)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Factor the denominator into irreducible components.
The denominator factors are: linear factor x, repeated linear factor (x - 2)2, and irreducible quadratic factor (x2 + 9).

Step 2: Assign terms for linear and repeated linear factors.
• The distinct linear factor x contributes: A / x.
• The repeated linear factor (x - 2)2 contributes descending powers: B / (x - 2) + C / (x - 2)2.

Step 3: Assign terms for the irreducible quadratic factor.
• The irreducible quadratic factor (x2 + 9) must have a general linear numerator: (Dx + E) / (x2 + 9).

Step 4: Combine all terms into the complete decomposition:
P(x) = A/x + B/(x - 2) + C/(x - 2)2 + (Dx + E)/(x2 + 9) (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Fails to include the second power term for (x - 2)2 and uses a constant numerator for (x2 + 9).
  • ✗ (B)Omits the first-power term B/(x - 2) required for repeated linear factors.
  • ✗ (C)Uses a constant numerator D instead of a linear numerator Dx + E over the quadratic factor x2 + 9.
Question 132 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 6
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Identify the interior infinite discontinuity.
The integrand f(x) = x-2/3 has a vertical asymptote at x = 0, which lies strictly inside the interval [-1, 1].
Therefore, the integral must be split into two separate one-sided improper limits:
∫-11 x-2/3 dx = limc→0⁻ ∫-1c x-2/3 dx + limd→0⁺ ∫d1 x-2/3 dx.

Step 2: Find the general antiderivative.
∫ x-2/3 dx = [x1/3 / (1/3)] = 3 x1/3.

Step 3: Evaluate each limit:
• Left integral: limc→0⁻ [3c1/3 - 3(-1)1/3] = 0 - 3(-1) = 3.
• Right integral: limd→0⁺ [3(1)1/3 - 3d1/3] = 3 - 0 = 3.

Step 4: Sum the convergent limits:
Total = 3 + 3 = 6 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Erroneously assumes symmetry makes the integral evaluate to 0.
  • ✗ (C)Evaluates only half of the interval from 0 to 1.
  • ✗ (D)Claims the integral diverges by confusing p = 2/3 < 1 (which converges) with p ≥ 1.

Question 133 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) 2 ln(2) - 1/6
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Perform polynomial long division on the improper fraction.
(x3 + 2x2 + 1) ÷ (x + 1):
x3 + 2x2 + 1 = x2(x + 1) + x(x + 1) - 1(x + 1) + 2.
Thus, (x3 + 2x2 + 1) / (x + 1) = x2 + x - 1 + 2 / (x + 1).

Step 2: Integrate term by term:
∫ (x2 + x - 1 + 2 / (x + 1)) dx = [x3/3 + x2/2 - x + 2 ln|x + 1|].

Step 3: Apply the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus from x = 0 to x = 1:
• At x = 1: (1/3 + 1/2 - 1 + 2 ln(2)) = (5/6 - 1 + 2 ln(2)) = -1/6 + 2 ln(2).
• At x = 0: (0 + 0 - 0 + 2 ln(1)) = 0.
• Result: 2 ln(2) - 1/6 (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Sign error: adds 5/6 instead of subtracting 1 (5/6 - 1 = -1/6).
  • ✗ (C)Forgets coefficient 2 in front of the logarithmic term.
  • ✗ (D)Sign error on -1/6.
Question 134 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) (x3/9) (3 ln(x) - 1) + C
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Choose u and dv using the LIPET rule.
Let u = ln(x) ⇒ du = (1/x) dx.
Let dv = x2 dx ⇒ v = x3 / 3.

Step 2: Apply the Integration by Parts formula: ∫ u dv = u v - ∫ v du.
∫ x2 ln(x) dx = (x3/3) ln(x) - ∫ (x3/3)(1/x) dx.

Step 3: Simplify and evaluate the remaining integral:
∫ (x3/3)(1/x) dx = (1/3) ∫ x2 dx = (1/3)(x3/3) = x3 / 9.

Step 4: Factor out x3/9:
(x3/3) ln(x) - x3/9 + C = (x3/9)(3 ln(x) - 1) + C (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets to integrate x2 a second time in the v du term.
  • ✗ (B)Divides the first term by 6 instead of 3.
  • ✗ (D)Uses wrong power antiderivative (x2 instead of x3).
Question 135 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) 10 · [ln(7) / ln(7/4)]
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Solve the separable differential equation.
dT / (T - 20) = -k dt ⇒ ln|T - 20| = -kt + C ⇒ T(t) - 20 = (T(0) - 20) e-kt.
Given T(0) = 90: T(t) - 20 = 70 e-kt.

Step 2: Determine the decay constant k using T(10) = 60°C.
60 - 20 = 70 e-10k ⇒ 40 = 70 e-10k ⇒ e-10k = 4/7.
Taking natural logs: -10k = ln(4/7) = -ln(7/4) ⇒ k = (1/10) ln(7/4).

Step 3: Set T(t) = 30°C and solve for t:
30 - 20 = 70 e-kt ⇒ 10 = 70 e-kt ⇒ e-kt = 1/7.
-kt = ln(1/7) = -ln(7) ⇒ t = ln(7) / k.

Step 4: Substitute k:
t = ln(7) / [(1/10) ln(7/4)] = 10 · [ln(7) / ln(7/4)] minutes ≈ 34.8 min (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Assumes linear temperature drop of 30°C every 10 minutes.
  • ✗ (C)Swaps the numerator argument with ln(4).
  • ✗ (D)Guesswork assuming doubling the elapsed time doubles cooling.

Q136–Q138
Question 136 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) Slopes are independent of x, horizontal tangent segments occur along y = 0 and y = 2, and any solution with 0 < y(0) < 2 has an inflection point at y = 1.
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Analyze horizontal tangents and slope independence.
Since dy/dx depends only on y, slopes are identical across any horizontal line (autonomous).
dy/dx = 0 when y(2 - y) = 0 ⇒ y = 0 and y = 2 are horizontal equilibria.

Step 2: Determine sign of first derivative for 0 < y < 2.
For 0 < y < 2, both y > 0 and 2 - y > 0, so dy/dx > 0 (solutions are strictly increasing).

Step 3: Determine concavity and inflection points using d2y/dx2.
d2y/dx2 = d/dx [2y - y2] = (2 - 2y) (dy/dx) = 2(1 - y) y(2 - y).
• For 0 < y < 1: 1 - y > 0 ⇒ d2y/dx2 > 0 (concave up).
• For 1 < y < 2: 1 - y < 0 ⇒ d2y/dx2 < 0 (concave down).
Thus, concavity changes at y = 1, which represents an inflection point on the logistic solution curve (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Claims solution is concave down everywhere on (0, 2), missing the concave up portion for 0 < y < 1.
  • ✗ (C)False: dy/dx = y(2 - y) > 0 on (0, 2).
  • ✗ (D)As x → ∞, solutions with y(0) > 0 approach the carrying capacity y = 2, not 0.
Question 137 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) 100 / e lbs
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Set up the differential equation for salt amount S(t).
Rate in = 0 (pure water).
Rate out = (10 gal/min) · (S / 500 lb/gal) = S / 50 lb/min.
dS/dt = -S / 50 with S(0) = 100.

Step 2: Solve the separable differential equation.
dS / S = -1/50 dt ⇒ ln|S| = -t/50 + C ⇒ S(t) = S(0) e-t/50 = 100 e-t/50.

Step 3: Evaluate at t = 50 minutes:
S(50) = 100 e-50/50 = 100 e-1 = 100 / e lbs (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Uses t = 100 minutes to get e-2.
  • ✗ (B)Halves the initial salt to 50.
  • ✗ (D)Reports the amount of salt that has leaked out rather than the amount remaining.
Question 138 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) -25 / y3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Differentiate implicitly to find dy/dx.
2x + 2y (dy/dx) = 0 ⇒ dy/dx = -x / y.

Step 2: Differentiate dy/dx with respect to x using the quotient rule.
d2y/dx2 = [(-1)(y) - (-x)(dy/dx)] / y2 = [-y + x(dy/dx)] / y2.

Step 3: Substitute dy/dx = -x/y:
d2y/dx2 = [-y + x(-x/y)] / y2 = [-y - x2/y] / y2 = [-(y2 + x2) / y] / y2 = -(x2 + y2) / y3.

Step 4: Substitute the original relation x2 + y2 = 25:
d2y/dx2 = -25 / y3 (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Power error: y2 in denominator instead of y3.
  • ✗ (B)Missing the negative sign.
  • ✗ (C)Fails to use x2 + y2 = 25 to simplify the numerator.

Q139–Q141
Question 139 · Unit 6: Integration and Accumulation of Change
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 1/4
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Express the improper integral as a limit.
∫0∞ x e-2x dx = limb→∞ ∫0b x e-2x dx.

Step 2: Apply integration by parts to find the antiderivative.
Let u = x ⇒ du = dx.
Let dv = e-2x dx ⇒ v = -(1/2) e-2x.
∫ x e-2x dx = -(1/2) x e-2x - ∫ -(1/2) e-2x dx = -(1/2) x e-2x - (1/4) e-2x.

Step 3: Evaluate from 0 to b:
[-(1/2) b e-2b - (1/4) e-2b] - [0 - 1/4] = -(1/2) b e-2b - (1/4) e-2b + 1/4.

Step 4: Take the limit as b → ∞:
By L'Hopital's rule, limb→∞ (b / e2b) = limb→∞ (1 / 2e2b) = 0, and e-2b → 0.
Limit = 0 - 0 + 1/4 = 1/4 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets the second factor of 1/2 from integrating e-2x.
  • ✗ (C)Ignores the exponential decay rate k = 2.
  • ✗ (D)Concludes divergence because upper limit is ∞.
Question 140 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) 3√17
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Separate variables and integrate both sides.
3y2 dy = 2x dx ⇒ ∫ 3y2 dy = ∫ 2x dx ⇒ y3 = x2 + C.

Step 2: Solve for constant C using initial condition y(0) = 2.
(2)3 = 02 + C ⇒ C = 8.
Thus, y3 = x2 + 8 ⇒ y(x) = (x2 + 8)1/3.

Step 3: Evaluate y(3):
y(3) = (32 + 8)1/3 = (9 + 8)1/3 = 171/3 = 3√17 (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Multiplies 2x2 instead of x2, yielding (2(9) + 8)1/3 = 3√26.
  • ✗ (C)Subtracts C = 8: (9 + 2)1/3 = 3√11.
  • ✗ (D)Rounds 3√17 or confuses with 3√27 = 3.
Question 141 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (A) Underestimate, because the solution curve is concave up on [0, 0.4].
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute the second derivative d2y/dx2 to determine concavity.
d2y/dx2 = d/dx [x2 + y] = 2x + dy/dx = 2x + x2 + y.

Step 2: Determine the sign of d2y/dx2 on [0, 0.4].
At x = 0, y(0) = 1, so dy/dx = 0 + 1 = 1 > 0, which means y is increasing for x ≥ 0.
For all x ∈ [0, 0.4], 2x ≥ 0, x2 ≥ 0, and y(x) ≥ 1 > 0.
Therefore, d2y/dx2 = 2x + x2 + y > 0 for all x ∈ [0, 0.4].

Step 3: Relate concavity to tangent line approximations.
Since the solution curve is concave up (d2y/dx2 > 0), tangent line segments lie strictly beneath the actual curve.
Consequently, Euler's method produces an underestimate of the true value (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Correctly identifies concave up but erroneously asserts tangent lines overestimate concave up curves.
  • ✗ (C)Claims the curve is concave down despite d2y/dx2 > 0.
  • ✗ (D)Reverses both concavity sign and geometric tangent line property.

Q142–Q144
Question 142 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) P(t) decreases and approaches 500 as t → ∞.
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Identify the carrying capacity L.
In the standard logistic equation dP/dt = k P (1 - P / L), the stable carrying capacity is L = 500.

Step 2: Analyze the sign of dP/dt when P(0) = 800.
Since P(0) = 800 > 500: (1 - P / 500) = (1 - 800/500) = -0.6 < 0.
Because 0.05 P > 0 and (1 - P/500) < 0, dP/dt < 0.
Thus, the population is strictly decreasing.

Step 3: Determine the long-term asymptotic limit.
As t → ∞, the population decreases toward the stable equilibrium carrying capacity: limt→∞ P(t) = 500 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Claims population increases, ignoring that initial population exceeds carrying capacity.
  • ✗ (C)Claims population crashes to extinction (0), ignoring the stable equilibrium at 500.
  • ✗ (D)Logistic differential equations never exhibit periodic oscillations.
Question 143 · Unit 7: Differential Equations
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 500
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Express growth rate as a function of P.
Let g(P) = dP/dt = 0.08 (P - P2 / 1000).

Step 2: Find the critical point of the growth rate function g(P).
Differentiating with respect to P: g'(P) = 0.08 (1 - 2P / 1000).
Setting g'(P) = 0 ⇒ 1 - 2P / 1000 = 0 ⇒ 2P = 1000 ⇒ P = 500.

Step 3: Confirm maximum using the second derivative:
g''(P) = -0.08(2/1000) < 0, confirming a maximum.
This corresponds to the inflection point of the logistic S-curve, which always occurs at exactly half the carrying capacity: P = L / 2 = 1000 / 2 = 500 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Represents one-quarter of the carrying capacity.
  • ✗ (C)Represents three-quarters of the carrying capacity.
  • ✗ (D)Carrying capacity L = 1000, where the growth rate reaches zero, not maximum.
Question 144 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (A) 56π / 3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Express boundary curves as functions of y.
In the first quadrant, y = x2 gives x = √y for 0 ≤ y ≤ 4. The inner boundary is x = 0 (the y-axis).

Step 2: Determine outer and inner radii relative to the axis x = -1.
Outer radius: R(y) = √y - (-1) = √y + 1.
Inner radius: r(y) = 0 - (-1) = 1.

Step 3: Set up the washer volume integral:
V = π ∫04 [R(y)2 - r(y)2] dy = π ∫04 [(√y + 1)2 - 12] dy.
(√y + 1)2 - 1 = (y + 2√y + 1) - 1 = y + 2 y1/2.

Step 4: Evaluate the definite integral:
V = π [y2/2 + 2(2/3) y3/2]04 = π [16/2 + (4/3)(8)] = π [8 + 32/3] = π [24/3 + 32/3] = 56π / 3 (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Omits the cross-term 2√y in the expansion of (√y + 1)2.
  • ✗ (C)Revolved around the y-axis (x = 0) instead of x = -1: π ∫ y dy = 8π.
  • ✗ (D)Arithmetic error when summing 8 and 32/3.

Question 145 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 24π
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Identify region bounds and radii relative to the line y = 3.
For x ∈ [0, 4], y = √x ranges from 0 to 2, so the entire region lies below the line y = 3.
Outer radius: R(x) = 3 - 0 = 3.
Inner radius: r(x) = 3 - √x.

Step 2: Express washer cross-sectional area:

Question 146 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) 128 / 15
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Determine hypotenuse length H(x).
For each x in [-2, 2], the hypotenuse is the vertical segment from y = 0 to y = 4 - x2: H(x) = 4 - x2.

Step 2: Relate triangle area to hypotenuse length.
For an isosceles right triangle with equal legs s and hypotenuse H: s2 + s2 = H2 ⇒ 2s2 = H2 ⇒ s2 = H2 / 2.
Area = (1/2) s2 = (1/2)(H2 / 2) = H2 / 4.
Thus, A(x) = (1/4) (4 - x2)2 = (1/4) (16 - 8x2 + x4).

Step 3: Integrate over [-2, 2] using symmetry:
V = 2 ∫02 (1/4)(16 - 8x2 + x4) dx = (1/2) [16x - (8/3) x3 + x5/5]02.
At x = 2: (1/2) [32 - 64/3 + 32/5] = (1/2) · 32 · [1 - 2/3 + 1/5] = 16 · [8/15] = 128 / 15 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Uses Area = H2 / 2 (assuming leg lies in the base instead of hypotenuse), doubling the volume to 256/15.
  • ✗ (B)Halves the correct answer.
  • ✗ (D)Multiplies by 4.
Question 147 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) 9/2

Step 1: Find points of intersection.
x3 - x = 2x ⇒ x3 - 3x = 0 ⇒ x(x2 - 3) = 0 ⇒ x = -√3, 0, √3.

Step 2: Utilize odd function symmetry.
Both y = x3 - x and y = 2x are odd functions, so the area over [-√3, 0] equals the area over [0, √3].
Total Area = 2 ∫0√3 [2x - (x3 - x)] dx = 2 ∫0√3 (3x - x3) dx.

Step 3: Evaluate the definite integral:
2 [(3/2) x2 - x4/4]0√3 = 2 [(3/2)(3) - 9/4] = 2 [9/2 - 9/4] = 2(9/4) = 9/2 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Evaluates only one loop of the enclosed area (9/4) instead of the total area.
  • ✗ (B)Doubles the total area to 9.
  • ✗ (D)Arithmetic error with higher powers of √3.

T_avg = (1 / (4 - 0)) ∫04 (80 + 40 e-t/2) dt.

Step 2: Find the antiderivative.

Step 3: Evaluate the definite integral from t = 0 to t = 4:

Question 149 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) 23
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find where velocity changes sign.
v(t) = 3(t2 - 4) = 3(t - 2)(t + 2) = 0 ⇒ t = 2.
For 0 ≤ t < 2, v(t) < 0 (moving left).
For 2 < t ≤ 3, v(t) > 0 (moving right).

Step 2: Express total distance as the sum of absolute displacements.
Total Distance = |∫02 v(t) dt| + |∫23 v(t) dt|.

Step 3: Evaluate each displacement segment:
• ∫02 (3t2 - 12) dt = [t3 - 12t]02 = 8 - 24 = -16 ⇒ Distance = |-16| = 16.
• ∫23 (3t2 - 12) dt = [t3 - 12t]23 = (27 - 36) - (8 - 24) = -9 - (-16) = 7 ⇒ Distance = 7.

Step 4: Sum the distances:
Total Distance = 16 + 7 = 23 (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Net displacement: ∫03 v(t) dt = [27 - 36] = -9 ⇒ |-9| = 9.
  • ✗ (B)Distance over [0, 2] only.
  • ✗ (C)Arithmetic error summing 16 + 9.
Question 150 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 14/3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute dy/dx.
dy/dx = (3x2/6) - (1 / (2x2)) = (x2 / 2) - (1 / (2x2)).

Step 2: Simplify 1 + (dy/dx)2.
(dy/dx)2 = (x4 / 4) - 2(x2/2)(1/(2x2)) + (1 / (4x4)) = (x4 / 4) - 1/2 + (1 / (4x4)).
1 + (dy/dx)2 = (x4 / 4) + 1/2 + (1 / (4x4)) = [(x2 / 2) + (1 / (2x2))]2.

Step 3: Take the square root:
√(1 + (dy/dx)2) = (x2 / 2) + (1 / (2x2)).

Step 4: Integrate from 1 to 3:
L = ∫13 [(x2 / 2) + (1 / (2x2))] dx = [x3/6 - 1 / (2x)]13.
At x = 3: 27/6 - 1/6 = 26/6.
At x = 1: 1/6 - 1/2 = 1/6 - 3/6 = -2/6.
L = 26/6 - (-2/6) = 28/6 = 14/3 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Sign error at the lower bound: 26/6 - 2/6 = 24/6 = 4.
  • ✗ (C)Arithmetic error adding numerators.
  • ✗ (D)Forgets to reduce fraction 28/6 or uses wrong common denominator.

Question 151 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration

Step 1: Determine the side length s(x) of the equilateral triangle cross section.

Question 152 · Unit 8: Applications of Integration
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) 62.5π cm3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute cross-sectional circular area A(x) = π [r(x)]2 at each given point:
• A(0) = π (3)2 = 9π.
• A(2) = π (4)2 = 16π.
• A(5) = π (2)2 = 4π.
• A(8) = π (1)2 = 1π.

Step 2: Apply the trapezoidal approximation across each subinterval:
• Subinterval [0, 2] (Δx = 2): (2 / 2) [9π + 16π] = 25π.
• Subinterval [2, 5] (Δx = 3): (3 / 2) [16π + 4π] = (3/2)(20π) = 30π.
• Subinterval [5, 8] (Δx = 3): (3 / 2) [4π + 1π] = (3/2)(5π) = 7.5π.

Question 153 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) t = 2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Differentiate x(t) and y(t) with respect to t.
dx/dt = 3t2 - 3 = 3(t - 1)(t + 1).
dy/dt = 6t2 - 12t = 6t(t - 2).

Step 2: Set up the parametric derivative dy/dx.
dy/dx = (dy/dt) / (dx/dt) = [6t(t - 2)] / [3(t - 1)(t + 1)].

Step 3: Identify conditions for a horizontal tangent line.
A horizontal tangent line requires dy/dt = 0 while dx/dt ≠ 0.
dy/dt = 0 when t = 0 or t = 2.
Testing t = 2: dx/dt = 3(22 - 1) = 3(3) = 9 ≠ 0.
Therefore, the curve has a horizontal tangent line at t = 2 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)At t = 1, dx/dt = 0 while dy/dt = -6 ≠ 0, producing a vertical tangent line.
  • ✗ (B)At t = -1, dx/dt = 0, producing a vertical tangent line.
  • ✗ (D)At t = 3, dy/dt = 18 ≠ 0.

Question 154 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 8/9
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute the first derivative dy/dx.
dx/dt = 3t2.
dy/dt = 4t3 - 2.
dy/dx = (dy/dt) / (dx/dt) = (4t3 - 2) / (3t2) = (4/3) t - (2/3) t-2.

Step 2: Apply the parametric second derivative formula: d2y/dx2 = [d/dt (dy/dx)] / (dx/dt).
d/dt (dy/dx) = (4/3) - (2/3)(-2 t-3) = 4/3 + 4 / (3t3).

Step 3: Evaluate at t = 1:
d/dt (dy/dx)|t=1 = 4/3 + 4/3 = 8/3.
dx/dt|t=1 = 3(1)2 = 3.
d2y/dx2|t=1 = (8/3) / 3 = 8/9 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets the second term in d/dt(dy/dx): evaluates (4/3) / 3 = 4/9.
  • ✗ (C)Forgets to divide by dx/dt, stopping at d/dt(dy/dx) = 8/3.
  • ✗ (D)Divides the second derivative of y by the second derivative of x: (d2y/dt2) / (d2x/dt2) = (12(1)2)/(6(1)) = 12/6 = 2.
Question 155 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) √2 (eπ - 1)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute derivatives dx/dt and dy/dt.
dx/dt = et cos(t) - et sin(t) = et [cos(t) - sin(t)].
dy/dt = et sin(t) + et cos(t) = et [cos(t) + sin(t)].

Step 2: Simplify (dx/dt)2 + (dy/dt)2.
(dx/dt)2 + (dy/dt)2 = e2t [(cos(t) - sin(t))2 + (cos(t) + sin(t))2].
Expanding gives: (cos2t - 2sincos + sin2t) + (cos2t + 2sincos + sin2t) = 2(cos2t + sin2t) = 2.
Thus, speed = √[(dx/dt)2 + (dy/dt)2] = √(2 e2t) = √2 et.

Step 3: Integrate over [0, π] to find arc length:
L = ∫0π √2 et dt = √2 [et]0π = √2 (eπ - e0) = √2 (eπ - 1) (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets the √2 constant factor.
  • ✗ (B)Omits the lower limit evaluation at t = 0 (e0 = 1).
  • ✗ (D)Divides by 2 instead of multiplying by √2.

Question 160 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (D) t = 2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State the condition for a particle to be at rest in 2D.
A particle in the xy-plane is at rest if and only if its speed is zero, which requires BOTH velocity components to vanish simultaneously: dx/dt = 0 AND dy/dt = 0.

Step 2: Find the zeros of dx/dt.
dx/dt = 6t2 - 18t + 12 = 6(t2 - 3t + 2) = 6(t - 1)(t - 2) = 0 ⇒ t = 1 or t = 2.

Step 3: Find the zeros of dy/dt.
dy/dt = 4t3 - 12t2 + 8t = 4t(t2 - 3t + 2) = 4t(t - 1)(t - 2) = 0 ⇒ t = 0, t = 1, or t = 2.

Step 4: Identify common positive zeros:
Both derivatives vanish at t = 1 and t = 2. Looking at the options, t = 2 is among the choices (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)At t = 3, dx/dt = 6(2)(1) = 12 ≠ 0.
  • ✗ (B)At t = 1/2, neither velocity component is zero.
  • ✗ (C)Arbitrary value where motion is active.
Question 161 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (A) (-4, 2)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute derivatives dx/dt and dy/dt.
dx/dt = 2t - 4.
dy/dt = 3t2 - 3 = 3(t2 - 1).

Step 2: Identify condition for a vertical tangent.
A vertical tangent occurs where dx/dt = 0 and dy/dt ≠ 0.
Setting dx/dt = 0 ⇒ 2t - 4 = 0 ⇒ t = 2.
Check dy/dt at t = 2: dy/dt = 3(22 - 1) = 3(3) = 9 ≠ 0.

Step 3: Evaluate coordinates (x, y) at t = 2:
x(2) = 22 - 4(2) = 4 - 8 = -4.
y(2) = 23 - 3(2) = 8 - 6 = 2.
Thus, the point of vertical tangency is (-4, 2) (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Evaluates at t = 0 (an intercept, not a vertical tangent).
  • ✗ (C)Arithmetic error evaluating y(2).
  • ✗ (D)Sign reversal on x and y coordinates.
Question 162 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) t > 0
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find the first derivative dy/dx.
dx/dt = 2t.
dy/dt = 3t2 - 3.
dy/dx = (3t2 - 3) / (2t) = (3/2) t - (3/2) t-1.

Step 2: Differentiate dy/dx with respect to t:
d/dt [dy/dx] = 3/2 + (3/2) t-2 = (3/2) [1 + 1/t2] = (3/2) [(t2 + 1) / t2].

Step 3: Apply the second derivative formula d2y/dx2 = [d/dt (dy/dx)] / (dx/dt):
d2y/dx2 = [(3/2)(t2 + 1) / t2] / (2t) = (3/4) (t2 + 1) / t3.

Step 4: Analyze the sign of d2y/dx2:
Since t2 + 1 > 0 for all t, the sign of d2y/dx2 is determined entirely by t3.
For t > 0, t3 > 0 ⇒ d2y/dx2 > 0 (concave up).
For t < 0, t3 < 0 ⇒ d2y/dx2 < 0 (concave down).
Thus, the curve is concave upward for t > 0 (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Interval where the curve is concave downward.
  • ✗ (C)Interval where dy/dt < 0, confusing slope with concavity.
  • ✗ (D)Arbitrary subinterval.

Q163–Q165
Question 163 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (A) √34
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Integrate acceleration components to find velocity components.
vx(t) = vx(0) + ∫0t 6u du = 2 + 3t2.
vy(t) = vy(0) + ∫0t 12u2 du = -1 + 4t3.

Step 2: Evaluate velocity at t = 1:
vx(1) = 2 + 3(1)2 = 5.
vy(1) = -1 + 4(1)3 = 3.
v(1) = ⟨5, 3⟩.

Step 3: Calculate the speed ||v(1)||:
Speed = √(52 + 32) = √(25 + 9) = √34 (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Sum of velocity components: 5 + 3 = 8.
  • ✗ (C)Arithmetic error adding squares: 25 + 15 = 40.
  • ✗ (D)Omits initial condition v(0).
Question 164 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) t = √2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Express the square of the distance D(t) from the origin.
D(t) = [x(t)]2 + [y(t)]2 = (√2 t)2 + (t2 - 3)2 = 2t2 + (t4 - 6t2 + 9) = t4 - 4t2 + 9.

Step 2: Differentiate D(t) with respect to t to find critical points.
D'(t) = 4t3 - 8t = 4t(t2 - 2) = 0.
For t ≥ 0, critical points are t = 0 and t = √2.

Step 3: Evaluate distance squared at candidate points:
• At t = 0: D(0) = 0 - 0 + 9 = 9 ⇒ Distance = 3.
• At t = √2: D(√2) = (√2)4 - 4(√2)2 + 9 = 4 - 8 + 9 = 5 ⇒ Distance = √5 ≈ 2.24.
• As t → ∞, D(t) → ∞.

Step 4: Compare values:
Since √5 < 3, the particle is closest to the origin at t = √2 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Local maximum on the closed half-line t ≥ 0.
  • ✗ (B)Arbitrary integer time.
  • ✗ (D)Evaluates at t = 2 where D(2) = 16 - 16 + 9 = 9.
Question 165 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) A circle centered at (-1, 2) with radius 3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Isolate the trigonometric terms.
cos(t) = (x + 1) / 3.
sin(t) = (y - 2) / 3.

Step 2: Use the Pythagorean trigonometric identity cos2(t) + sin2(t) = 1:
[(x + 1) / 3]2 + [(y - 2) / 3]2 = 1.
(x + 1)2 / 9 + (y - 2)2 / 9 = 1 ⇒ (x + 1)2 + (y - 2)2 = 9 = 32.

Step 3: Identify the geometric shape and parameters:
This is the standard equation of a circle with center (h, k) = (-1, 2) and radius r = 3 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Inverts the signs of the center coordinates and squares radius.
  • ✗ (C)Coefficients of cosine and sine are identical (both 3), producing a circle, not an ellipse.
  • ✗ (D)Reports radius as r2 = 9 instead of r = 3.

Question 166 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) π/4
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute velocity components at t = 1.
dx/dt = 2t ⇒ dx/dt|t=1 = 2.
dy/dt = 2t2 ⇒ dy/dt|t=1 = 2.

Step 2: Relate direction angle θ to velocity components.
The direction of motion angle θ satisfies tan(θ) = (dy/dt) / (dx/dt).
tan(θ) = 2 / 2 = 1.

Step 3: Determine the angle in the proper quadrant:
Since dx/dt = 2 > 0 and dy/dt = 2 > 0, the velocity vector points into Quadrant I.
θ = arctan(1) = π/4 radians (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Corresponds to tan(θ) = 1/√3.
  • ✗ (C)Corresponds to tan(θ) = √3.
  • ✗ (D)Represents purely vertical motion (dx/dt = 0).
Question 167 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (A) √10 / 3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute velocity and acceleration vectors.
v(t) = r'(t) = ⟨3t2 - 6, 4t⟩.
a(t) = v'(t) = ⟨6t, 4⟩.

Step 2: Set the dot product v(t) · a(t) equal to zero for orthogonality.
v(t) · a(t) = (3t2 - 6)(6t) + (4t)(4) = 18t3 - 36t + 16t = 18t3 - 20t = 0.

Step 3: Solve for t > 0:
2t(9t2 - 10) = 0.
Since t > 0, 9t2 - 10 = 0 ⇒ t2 = 10/9 ⇒ t = √10 / 3 (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Reports t2 = 10/9 instead of taking the square root.
  • ✗ (C)Forgets the y-component dot product term 16t.
  • ✗ (D)Arithmetic error factoring the polynomial.
Question 168 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) The particles collide at time t = 3 at (5, 9).
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Set up equations for collision (same position at the SAME time t).
• x-components: 2t - 1 = t + 2 ⇒ t = 3.

Step 2: Verify whether y-components match at t = 3:
• yA(3) = 32 = 9.
• yB(3) = 4(3) - 3 = 12 - 3 = 9.

Step 3: Check spatial coordinates:
• rA(3) = ⟨2(3) - 1, 9⟩ = ⟨5, 9⟩.
• rB(3) = ⟨3 + 2, 9⟩ = ⟨5, 9⟩.
Both particles occupy exactly the same point (5, 9) at the exact same instant t = 3. Therefore, they collide at t = 3 at (5, 9) (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)At t = 1, rA(1) = ⟨1, 1⟩ but rB(1) = ⟨3, 1⟩ (x-coordinates do not match).
  • ✗ (C)False: They do collide because the collision condition holds at t = 3.
  • ✗ (D)At t = 2, rA(2) = ⟨3, 4⟩ but rB(2) = ⟨4, 5⟩.

Question 169 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions

Correct answer: (C) (-4, 5π/3)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find the magnitude r.
|r| = √[(-2)2 + (2√3)2] = √(4 + 12) = √16 = 4.

Step 2: Determine the standard angle θ with r > 0.
Since x = -2 < 0 and y = 2√3 > 0, the point lies in Quadrant II.
tan(θ) = 2√3 / (-2) = -√3 ⇒ θ = 2π/3.
Thus, the standard polar representation is (4, 2π/3).

Step 3: Convert to a representation with negative radius r = -4:
A negative radius reflects through the pole, requiring an angle shifted by ±π:
θ = 2π/3 + π = 5π/3 (or 2π/3 - π = -π/3).
Therefore, an equivalent representation with r < 0 is (-4, 5π/3) (Option C).

Question 171 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (A) 0
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Express x and y in terms of θ.
x(θ) = r cos(θ) = (2 + sin θ) cos θ.
y(θ) = r sin(θ) = (2 + sin θ) sin θ = 2 sin θ + sin2θ.

Step 2: Differentiate x and y with respect to θ:
dx/dθ = (cos θ)(cos θ) + (2 + sin θ)(-sin θ) = cos2θ - 2 sin θ - sin2θ.
dy/dθ = 2 cos θ + 2 sin θ cos θ = 2 cos θ (1 + sin θ).

Step 3: Evaluate derivatives at θ = π/2:
cos(π/2) = 0 and sin(π/2) = 1.
dx/dθ|θ=π/2 = 0 - 2(1) - 1 = -3.
dy/dθ|θ=π/2 = 2(0)(1 + 1) = 0.

Step 4: Compute the slope dy/dx:
dy/dx = (dy/dθ) / (dx/dθ) = 0 / (-3) = 0 (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Evaluates dx/dθ = -3 and forgets to divide by it.
  • ✗ (C)Incorrect trigonometric derivative evaluation.
  • ✗ (D)Assumes zero in denominator instead of numerator.

Question 172 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) θ = 0 and θ = π/2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State the tangent line condition at the pole.
For any smooth polar curve r = f(θ), the lines tangent to the curve at the origin (pole) are given by the angles α where f(α) = 0 (and f'(α) ≠ 0).

Step 2: Solve r = 0 for θ in [0, π):
4 sin(2θ) = 0 ⇒ sin(2θ) = 0.
For θ ∈ [0, π), 2θ ∈ [0, 2π).
2θ = 0 ⇒ θ = 0.
2θ = π ⇒ θ = π/2.

Step 3: Verify the derivative is nonzero at these roots:
f'(θ) = 8 cos(2θ).
f'(0) = 8 ≠ 0 and f'(π/2) = -8 ≠ 0.
Thus, the tangent lines at the pole are θ = 0 (the x-axis) and θ = π/2 (the y-axis) (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Angles where |r| is maximized (|r| = 4, petal tips), not where the curve passes through the pole.
  • ✗ (C)Arbitrary trigonometric angles.
  • ✗ (D)Angles where r = 4(√3/2) = 2√3.
Question 173 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) 9π / 8
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Determine the limits of integration for a single loop.
Set r = 0: 3 cos(2θ) = 0 ⇒ 2θ = -π/2 and 2θ = π/2 ⇒ θ = -π/4 to θ = π/4.

Step 2: Set up the polar area integral:
Area = (1/2) ∫-π/4π/4 r2 dθ = (1/2) ∫-π/4π/4 [3 cos(2θ)]2 dθ.
By even symmetry: Area = (1/2) · 2 ∫0π/4 9 cos2(2θ) dθ = 9 ∫0π/4 cos2(2θ) dθ.

Step 3: Use the power-reduction identity cos2(2θ) = (1 + cos(4θ)) / 2:
Area = (9/2) ∫0π/4 [1 + cos(4θ)] dθ = (9/2) [θ + sin(4θ)/4]0π/4.

Step 4: Evaluate the bounds:
(9/2) [(π/4 + sin(π)/4) - 0] = (9/2)(π/4) = 9π / 8 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets the factor of 1/2 in the polar area formula, yielding 9π/4.
  • ✗ (C)Halves the correct loop area.
  • ✗ (D)Reports the total area of all 4 petals combined: 4 × (9π/8) = 9π/2.
Question 174 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (A) π - (3√3 / 2)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find the values of θ bounding the inner loop.
The inner loop occurs when r < 0, bounded by consecutive roots where r = 0:
1 - 2 sin(θ) = 0 ⇒ sin(θ) = 1/2 ⇒ θ = π/6 and θ = 5π/6.
On (π/6, 5π/6), sin(θ) > 1/2, so r < 0 (forming the inner loop).

Step 2: Set up the area integral:
Area = (1/2) ∫π/65π/6 (1 - 2 sin θ)2 dθ.
(1 - 2 sin θ)2 = 1 - 4 sin θ + 4 sin2θ = 1 - 4 sin θ + 4 [(1 - cos 2θ)/2] = 3 - 4 sin θ - 2 cos(2θ).

Step 3: Integrate term by term:
∫ [3 - 4 sin θ - 2 cos(2θ)] dθ = 3θ + 4 cos θ - sin(2θ).

Step 4: Evaluate from π/6 to 5π/6:
• At 5π/6: 3(5π/6) + 4(-√3/2) - (-√3/2) = 5π/2 - 3√3/2.
• At π/6: 3(π/6) + 4(√3/2) - (√3/2) = π/2 + 3√3/2.
Difference = (5π/2 - π/2) - (3√3/2 + 3√3/2) = 2π - 3√3.
Multiplying by the 1/2 factor gives: π - (3√3 / 2) (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Omits the 1/2 factor from the polar area integral formula.
  • ✗ (C)Sign error resulting in addition instead of subtraction of the radical term.
  • ✗ (D)Incomplete evaluation of trigonometric bounds.

Q175–Q177
Question 175 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) π
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Find points of intersection of the two curves.
3 sin(θ) = 1 + sin(θ) ⇒ 2 sin(θ) = 1 ⇒ sin(θ) = 1/2.
Thus, θ = π/6 and θ = 5π/6. On [π/6, 5π/6], 3 sin(θ) ≥ 1 + sin(θ).

Step 2: Set up the area between polar curves integral:
Area = (1/2) ∫π/65π/6 [ (3 sin θ)2 - (1 + sin θ)2 ] dθ.
(3 sin θ)2 - (1 + sin θ)2 = 9 sin2θ - (1 + 2 sin θ + sin2θ) = 8 sin2θ - 2 sin θ - 1.

Step 3: Simplify using the power-reduction identity 8 sin2θ = 4 - 4 cos(2θ):
Integrand = 4 - 4 cos(2θ) - 2 sin θ - 1 = 3 - 4 cos(2θ) - 2 sin θ.
Antiderivative = 3θ - 2 sin(2θ) + 2 cos θ.

Step 4: Evaluate bounds from π/6 to 5π/6:
• At 5π/6: 5π/2 - 2(-√3/2) + 2(-√3/2) = 5π/2.
• At π/6: π/2 - 2(√3/2) + 2(√3/2) = π/2.
Difference = 5π/2 - π/2 = 2π.
Multiplying by 1/2: Area = (1/2)(2π) = π (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Evaluates over half the angular domain [π/6, π/2].
  • ✗ (C)Forgets the 1/2 coefficient in the area integral.
  • ✗ (D)Arithmetic error evaluating the definite integral.
Question 176 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (D) ∫02π √(8 - 8 cos θ) dθ
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State the arc length formula for a polar curve r = f(θ).
L = ∫αβ √[ r2 + (dr/dθ)2 ] dθ.

Step 2: Differentiate r with respect to θ:
r = 2 - 2 cos(θ) ⇒ dr/dθ = 2 sin(θ).

Step 3: Simplify r2 + (dr/dθ)2:
r2 + (dr/dθ)2 = (2 - 2 cos θ)2 + (2 sin θ)2 = (4 - 8 cos θ + 4 cos2θ) + 4 sin2θ.
Since 4 cos2θ + 4 sin2θ = 4, we have:
r2 + (dr/dθ)2 = 4 - 8 cos θ + 4 = 8 - 8 cos θ.

Step 4: Substitute into the arc length integral:
L = ∫02π √(8 - 8 cos θ) dθ (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Omits the constant 4 from the derivative term (dr/dθ)2.
  • ✗ (B)Omits the arc length square root radical entirely.
  • ✗ (C)Fails to expand the binomial square correctly.
Question 177 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (A) 3√5 / 2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute dr/dθ.
dr/dθ = 2 e2θ.

Step 2: Simplify r2 + (dr/dθ)2:
r2 + (dr/dθ)2 = (e2θ)2 + (2 e2θ)2 = e4θ + 4 e4θ = 5 e4θ.
√[ r2 + (dr/dθ)2 ] = √(5 e4θ) = √5 e2θ.

Step 3: Integrate over [0, ln(2)]:
L = ∫0ln(2) √5 e2θ dθ = √5 [(1/2) e2θ]0ln(2).

Step 4: Evaluate bounds:
(√5 / 2) [ e2 ln(2) - e0 ] = (√5 / 2) [ eln(4) - 1 ] = (√5 / 2) [ 4 - 1 ] = 3√5 / 2 (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Omits the 1/2 integration factor from e2θ and gets √5.
  • ✗ (C)Forgets to divide by 2.
  • ✗ (D)Evaluates (4 - 2) / 2 instead of 4 - 1.

Q178–Q180
Question 178 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) 2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Convert both equations to Cartesian coordinates.
• r = 2 sin(θ) ⇒ r2 = 2r sin(θ) ⇒ x2 + y2 = 2y ⇒ x2 + (y - 1)2 = 1 (circle of radius 1 centered at (0, 1)).
• r = 2 cos(θ) ⇒ r2 = 2r cos(θ) ⇒ x2 + y2 = 2x ⇒ (x - 1)2 + y2 = 1 (circle of radius 1 centered at (1, 0)).

Step 2: Find algebraic intersections:
Subtracting the two equations: 2x = 2y ⇒ y = x.
Substitute y = x into x2 + y2 = 2x ⇒ 2x2 = 2x ⇒ 2x(x - 1) = 0.
• Root 1: x = 0 ⇒ y = 0. The origin (0, 0).
• Root 2: x = 1 ⇒ y = 1. The point (1, 1).

Step 3: Confirm geometric intersections:
Two distinct non-concentric circles intersect in at most two points. The two distinct intersection points are (0, 0) and (1, 1).
Thus, there are exactly 2 distinct intersection points (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Assumes no intersection.
  • ✗ (B)Omits the pole (0, 0), which is reached at different θ values (θ = 0 for the sine circle, θ = π/2 for the cosine circle).
  • ✗ (D)Counts symmetric reflection points twice.
Question 179 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (B) -12√3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Relate distance from origin to r.
At θ = π/6, r = 4 cos(2(π/6)) = 4 cos(π/3) = 4(1/2) = 2 > 0.
Since r > 0, the distance from the origin is simply r.

Step 2: Apply the chain rule: dr/dt = (dr/dθ) · (dθ/dt).
dr/dθ = 4(-sin(2θ))(2) = -8 sin(2θ).

Step 3: Evaluate dr/dθ at θ = π/6:
dr/dθ|θ=π/6 = -8 sin(π/3) = -8 (√3 / 2) = -4√3.

Step 4: Multiply by dθ/dt = 3 rad/sec:
dr/dt = (-4√3)(3) = -12√3 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Forgets the chain rule factor of 2 in differentiating cos(2θ), yielding -6√3.
  • ✗ (C)Sign error: distance is decreasing as θ increases through π/6.
  • ✗ (D)Forgets to multiply by dθ/dt = 3.
Question 180 · Unit 9: Parametric Equations, Polar Coordinates, and Vector-Valued Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) θ = π/3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Express y as a function of θ.
y = r sin(θ) = (1 + cos θ) sin θ = sin θ + sin θ cos θ = sin θ + (1/2) sin(2θ).

Step 2: Differentiate y with respect to θ:
dy/dθ = cos θ + cos(2θ) = cos θ + (2 cos2θ - 1) = 2 cos2θ + cos θ - 1.
Factoring gives: dy/dθ = (2 cos θ - 1)(cos θ + 1).

Step 3: Find roots of dy/dθ in (0, π):
• 2 cos θ - 1 = 0 ⇒ cos θ = 1/2 ⇒ θ = π/3.
• cos θ + 1 = 0 ⇒ cos θ = -1 ⇒ θ = π (not in open interval (0, π), and corresponds to the cusp at the pole where dx/dθ = 0).

Step 4: Verify dx/dθ ≠ 0 at θ = π/3:
x = (1 + cos θ) cos θ = cos θ + cos2θ ⇒ dx/dθ = -sin θ (1 + 2 cos θ).
At θ = π/3: dx/dθ = -(√3/2)(1 + 1) = -√3 ≠ 0.
Therefore, the horizontal tangent line occurs at θ = π/3 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Corresponds to cos θ = √3/2, which does not zero dy/dθ.
  • ✗ (B)Corresponds to cos θ = √2/2.
  • ✗ (D)At θ = π/2, dy/dθ = 0 + (-1) = -1 ≠ 0.

Q181–Q183
Question 181 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) The series diverges because limn→∞ an = 2/5 ≠ 0.
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State the nth-Term Test for Divergence.
If limn→∞ an ≠ 0 (or does not exist), then the series ∑ an diverges.

Step 2: Evaluate the limit of the sequence of terms as n → ∞:
limn→∞ (2n3 + 5) / (5n3 - 1) = limn→∞ (2 + 5/n3) / (5 - 1/n3) = 2/5.

Step 3: Apply the divergence criterion:
Since 2/5 ≠ 0, the terms do not approach zero.
Therefore, the series diverges by the nth-Term Test for Divergence (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Confuses the limit of the terms (lim an = 2/5) with the infinite sum of the series.
  • ✗ (C)Confuses the nth-Term Test with the Ratio Test (where L < 1 implies convergence).
  • ✗ (D)False: terms do not approach 0, so the series cannot converge.
Question 182 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) p > 1
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Verify conditions for the Integral Test.
For x ≥ 2, f(x) = 1 / [x (ln x)p] is continuous, positive, and decreasing.

Step 2: Set up the improper integral:
∫2∞ 1 / [x (ln x)p] dx.
Let u = ln(x) ⇒ du = dx / x. The lower limit becomes u = ln(2), and the upper limit becomes ∞.
∫ln(2)∞ u-p du.

Step 3: Evaluate the improper integral:
• If p = 1: ∫ u-1 du = ln|u| → ∞ (diverges).
• If p ≠ 1: ∫ u-p du = [u1-p / (1 - p)]. This converges as u → ∞ if and only if 1 - p < 0 ⇒ p > 1.

Step 4: Conclude series behavior:
By the Integral Test, the series converges if and only if p > 1 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Includes p = 1, where ∑ 1 / [n ln n] diverges.
  • ✗ (C)Reversed inequality.
  • ✗ (D)Overlooks divergence for p ≤ 1.
Question 183 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (C) Converges by the Direct Comparison Test with ∑ 2/n3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Bound the numerator.
For all real n, 0 ≤ cos2(n) ≤ 1 ⇒ 1 ≤ cos2(n) + 1 ≤ 2.

Step 2: Bound the denominator.
For all n ≥ 1, n3 + 4 > n3 > 0 ⇒ 1 / (n3 + 4) < 1 / n3.

Step 3: Establish the direct comparison inequality:
0 < (cos2(n) + 1) / (n3 + 4) ≤ 2 / n3.

Step 4: Apply the Direct Comparison Test:
The comparison series ∑n=1∞ 2 / n3 = 2 ∑n=1∞ 1 / n3 is a convergent p-series (p = 3 > 1).
Since the terms of the given series are bounded above by a convergent p-series, the series converges by the Direct Comparison Test (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Claims divergence by comparing with a smaller divergent series.
  • ✗ (B)The numerator oscillates but is strictly bounded between 1 and 2, which does not cause divergence.
  • ✗ (D)The oscillating behavior does not prevent bounding.

Q184–Q186
Question 184 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) Converges by the Limit Comparison Test with bn = 1/n2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Determine the asymptotic behavior of the terms an.
As n → ∞, the highest-degree terms dominate: an ≈ 3n2 / n4 = 3 / n2.

Step 2: Choose the comparison series bn = 1 / n2.
The series ∑ 1 / n2 is a known convergent p-series (p = 2 > 1).

Step 3: Compute the limit of the ratio an / bn:
L = limn→∞ [ (3n2 + 4n) / (n4 - 2n + 5) ] / [ 1 / n2 ] = limn→∞ (3n4 + 4n3) / (n4 - 2n + 5) = 3.

Step 4: Conclude using the Limit Comparison Test:
Since L = 3 is a finite, strictly positive number (0 < L < ∞), both series share the same convergence behavior.
Therefore, the series converges by the Limit Comparison Test with bn = 1/n2 (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Compares with harmonic series bn = 1/n (which gives L = 0, inconclusive for divergence).
  • ✗ (B)Gives L = ∞, inconclusive.
  • ✗ (D)The Ratio Test on rational expressions always yields L = 1 (inconclusive), not L = 3.
Question 185 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) N = 10
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State the Alternating Series Estimation Theorem.
For a convergent alternating series satisfying the conditions of the Alternating Series Test, the error in using the N-th partial sum is bounded by the first omitted term:
|S - SN| ≤ bN+1 = 1 / (N + 1)4.

Step 2: Set up the error bound inequality:
1 / (N + 1)4 < 10-4 = 1 / 10,000.

Step 3: Solve for N:
(N + 1)4 > 10,000 = 104 ⇒ N + 1 > 10 ⇒ N > 9.

Step 4: Select the smallest integer:
The smallest integer strictly greater than 9 is N = 10 (Option B).
(Note: At N = 9, b10 = 1/104 = 10-4, which is equal to, not strictly less than, 10-4).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)At N = 9, the bound is 1/10,000, which does not satisfy the strict inequality < 10-4.
  • ✗ (C)N = 11 satisfies the inequality but is not the SMALLEST integer.
  • ✗ (D)Gross overestimate.
Question 186 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (A) L = 0; the series converges absolutely
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Form the ratio |an+1 / an|.
|an+1 / an| = [ 5n+1 (n + 1)! / (2(n + 1))! ] · [ (2n)! / (5n n!) ].

Step 2: Simplify the algebraic and factorial factors:
5n+1 / 5n = 5.
(n + 1)! / n! = n + 1.
(2n)! / (2n + 2)! = (2n)! / [(2n + 2)(2n + 1)(2n)!] = 1 / [(2n + 2)(2n + 1)].

Step 3: Combine and simplify:
|an+1 / an| = [5(n + 1)] / [2(n + 1)(2n + 1)] = 5 / [2(2n + 1)] = 5 / (4n + 2).

Step 4: Take the limit as n → ∞:
L = limn→∞ [ 5 / (4n + 2) ] = 0.
Since L = 0 < 1, the series converges absolutely by the Ratio Test (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Mistakenly cancels factorials as (n+1)! / (2n+2)! = 1/2.
  • ✗ (C)Claims ratio test yields 1.
  • ✗ (D)Inverts the ratio.

Q187–Q189
Question 187 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) ∑n=1∞ (-1)n / √n
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Define conditional convergence.
A series ∑ an converges conditionally if ∑ an converges, but the series of absolute values ∑ |an| diverges.

Step 2: Test convergence of ∑n=1∞ (-1)n / √n:
By the Alternating Series Test, bn = 1/√n is positive, strictly decreasing, and limn→∞ 1/√n = 0. Thus, the alternating series converges.

Step 3: Test convergence of the absolute series:
∑n=1∞ |(-1)n / √n| = ∑n=1∞ 1 / n1/2.
This is a p-series with p = 1/2 ≤ 1, which diverges.

Step 4: Conclude:
Since the alternating series converges but its absolute series diverges, ∑ (-1)n / √n converges conditionally (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)∑ 1/n2 converges (p = 2 > 1), so Option A converges ABSOLUTELY.
  • ✗ (C)The harmonic series ∑ 1/n diverges.
  • ✗ (D)Diverges by the nth-Term Test since |r| = 3/2 > 1.
Question 188 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (A) 0 < x < 1, with sum S(x) = 1 / [2(1 - x)]
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Identify the geometric series ratio and initial term.
First term: a = (2x - 1)0 = 1.
Common ratio: r = 2x - 1.

Step 2: Apply the geometric convergence condition |r| < 1:
|2x - 1| < 1 ⇒ -1 < 2x - 1 < 1 ⇒ 0 < 2x < 2 ⇒ 0 < x < 1.

Step 3: Calculate the sum S(x) = a / (1 - r):
S(x) = 1 / [1 - (2x - 1)] = 1 / [1 - 2x + 1] = 1 / (2 - 2x) = 1 / [2(1 - x)].
Therefore, the series converges on 0 < x < 1 with sum S(x) = 1 / [2(1 - x)] (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Solves |x| < 1 instead of |2x - 1| < 1.
  • ✗ (C)Includes the endpoints x = 0 and x = 1, where geometric series diverge (|r| = 1).
  • ✗ (D)Incomplete interval.
Question 189 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (C) ∑n=1∞ n / (n3 + 1)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: State the condition under which the Ratio Test is inconclusive.
The Ratio Test is inconclusive when L = limn→∞ |an+1 / an| = 1.

Step 2: Evaluate the Ratio Test limit for Option C:
an = n / (n3 + 1).
|an+1 / an| = [ (n + 1) / ((n + 1)3 + 1) ] · [ (n3 + 1) / n ] = [ (n + 1)(n3 + 1) ] / [ n((n + 1)3 + 1) ].
Both numerator and denominator are polynomials of degree 4 with leading coefficient 1.
L = limn→∞ (n4 + ···) / (n4 + ···) = 1.
Thus, the Ratio Test yields L = 1 and is inconclusive for Option C (Option C).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)L = 1/3 < 1 (conclusive, converges).
  • ✗ (B)L = ∞ > 1 (conclusive, diverges).
  • ✗ (D)L = 2/5 < 1 (conclusive, converges).

Question 191 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) [1, 5)
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Apply the Ratio Test to find the interior interval.
L = limn→∞ |an+1 / an| = limn→∞ | [ (x - 3)n+1 / ((n + 1) 2n+1) ] · [ (n 2n) / (x - 3)n ] |.
L = (|x - 3| / 2) · limn→∞ [n / (n + 1)] = |x - 3| / 2.
For convergence: |x - 3| / 2 < 1 ⇒ |x - 3| < 2 ⇒ 1 < x < 5.

Step 2: Test left endpoint x = 1:
When x = 1: ∑ (1 - 3)n / (n · 2n) = ∑ (-2)n / (n · 2n) = ∑ (-1)n / n.
This is the alternating harmonic series, which converges by the Alternating Series Test. Thus, x = 1 is INCLUDED.

Step 3: Test right endpoint x = 5:
When x = 5: ∑ (5 - 3)n / (n · 2n) = ∑ 2n / (n · 2n) = ∑ 1 / n.
This is the harmonic p-series (p = 1), which diverges. Thus, x = 5 is EXCLUDED.

Step 4: Combine intervals:
The interval of convergence is [1, 5) (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Fails to test endpoints, excluding both.
  • ✗ (C)Swaps inclusion brackets: (1, 5].
  • ✗ (D)Incorrectly includes the divergent right endpoint x = 5.
Question 192 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★ MANDATORY
Correct answer: (D) R = ∞
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Set up the Ratio Test limit:
L = limn→∞ |an+1 / an| = limn→∞ | [ (2x + 1)n+1 / (n + 1)! ] · [ n! / (2x + 1)n ] |.

Step 2: Simplify factorials and powers:
L = limn→∞ [ |2x + 1| / (n + 1) ] = |2x + 1| · limn→∞ (1 / (n + 1)).

Step 3: Evaluate the limit for all real x:
Since limn→∞ 1/(n + 1) = 0, we have L = 0 < 1 for every real number x.
Therefore, the series converges for all x in (-∞, ∞).

Step 4: State the radius of convergence:
R = ∞ (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Solves |2x + 1| < 1 without accounting for the dominant n! growth in the denominator.
  • ✗ (B)Standard default radius for geometric series.
  • ✗ (C)Conflates the coefficient 2 with the radius.

Question 193 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (B) (x - 1) - (1/2)(x - 1)2 + (1/3)(x - 1)3
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Compute derivatives of f(x) = ln(x) and evaluate at x = 1.
• f(1) = ln(1) = 0.
• f'(x) = x-1 ⇒ f'(1) = 1.
• f''(x) = -x-2 ⇒ f''(1) = -1.
• f'''(x) = 2x-3 ⇒ f'''(1) = 2.

Step 2: State the Taylor polynomial formula centered at a = 1:
P₃(x) = f(1) + f'(1)(x - 1) + [f''(1) / 2!] (x - 1)2 + [f'''(1) / 3!] (x - 1)3.

Step 3: Substitute derivative values and factorials:
P₃(x) = 0 + 1(x - 1) + [(-1) / 2] (x - 1)2 + [2 / 6] (x - 1)3.
P₃(x) = (x - 1) - (1/2)(x - 1)2 + (1/3)(x - 1)3 (Option B).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Divides by 3! = 6 without multiplying by f'''(1) = 2 (forgetting the derivative factor 2).
  • ✗ (C)Incorrect positive sign on the second-degree term.
  • ✗ (D)Includes f(1) = 1 instead of ln(1) = 0.
Question 194 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series

Therefore, the coefficient an satisfies an = f(n)(2) / n! ⇒ f(n)(2) = n! · an.

|R₂(x)| ≤ [ M / (2 + 1)! ] |x - c|³ = [ M / 3! ] |x - 0|³, where M is the maximum of |f'''(t)| on [0, x].

Step 2: Identify given values:
M = 6, x = 0.5, c = 0, and 3! = 6.

Step 3: Evaluate the error bound:
|R₂(0.5)| ≤ [ 6 / 6 ] (0.5)3 = 1 · (1/2)3 = 1/8 = 0.125 (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT

Q199–Q200
Question 199 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (A) 2 + 6x + 10x2
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Evaluate f(0) and f'(0).
• f(0) = 2.
• f'(0) = y'(0) = 2(0) + 3(2) = 6.

Step 2: Differentiate the differential equation implicitly to find y''(x):
y'' = d/dx [2x + 3y] = 2 + 3 y'.

Step 3: Evaluate y''(0):
f''(0) = 2 + 3 y'(0) = 2 + 3(6) = 2 + 18 = 20.

Step 4: Construct the second-degree Maclaurin polynomial:
P₂(x) = f(0) + f'(0) x + [f''(0) / 2!] x2 = 2 + 6x + (20 / 2) x2 = 2 + 6x + 10x2 (Option A).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (B)Forgets to divide the quadratic term by 2!, keeping 20x2.
  • ✗ (C)Uses coefficient 3 instead of 6 for the linear term.
  • ✗ (D)Divides 20 by 4 instead of 2!.
Question 200 · Unit 10: Infinite Sequences and Series
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
Correct answer: (D) (-9, 1]
STEP-BY-STEP SOLUTION

Step 1: Apply the Ratio Test to find the radius of convergence R.
L = limn→∞ |an+1 / an| = |x + 4| / 5.
L < 1 ⇒ |x + 4| < 5 ⇒ -5 < x + 4 < 5 ⇒ -9 < x < 1. Radius R = 5.

Step 2: Test the left endpoint x = -9:
When x = -9: ∑ [ (-1)n (-9 + 4)n ] / [ n · 5n ] = ∑ [ (-1)n (-5)n ] / [ n · 5n ] = ∑ [ (-1)n (-1)n 5n ] / [ n · 5n ] = ∑ 1 / n.
This is the divergent harmonic series (p = 1). Thus, x = -9 is EXCLUDED.

Step 3: Test the right endpoint x = 1:
When x = 1: ∑ [ (-1)n (1 + 4)n ] / [ n · 5n ] = ∑ [ (-1)n 5n ] / [ n · 5n ] = ∑ (-1)n / n.
This is the alternating harmonic series, which converges by the Alternating Series Test. Thus, x = 1 is INCLUDED.

Step 4: Combine intervals:
The interval of convergence is (-9, 1] (Option D).

WHY THE OTHER OPTIONS ARE INCORRECT
  • ✗ (A)Reverses inclusion: [-9, 1).
  • ✗ (B)Omits endpoint tests, excluding both.
  • ✗ (C)Includes the divergent endpoint x = -9.

ANSWER SECTION · FRQ MODEL SOLUTION
QUESTION 201
Topics 1.10 - 2.4: Continuity, Differentiability, and the Intermediate Value Theorem
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• For x ≤ 2: f(2) = 3(2)2 + 2(2) = 12 + 4 = 16.
• Left-hand limit: limx→2⁻ f(x) = 3(2)2 + 2(2) = 16.
• Right-hand limit: limx→2⁺ f(x) = 2(2)3 - 4 = 16 - 4 = 12.
• Since limx→2⁻ f(x) = 16 ≠ limx→2⁺ f(x) = 12, the two-sided limit does not exist.
• Therefore, f is NOT continuous at x = 2.
Part (b) Solution:
• Differentiability requires continuity first. For continuity at x = 2:
  limx→2⁻ f(x) = limx→2⁺ f(x) ⇒ c(2)2 + 2(2) = d(2)3 - 4 ⇒ 4c + 4 = 8d - 4 ⇒ 4c - 8d = -8 ⇒ c - 2d = -2 (Equation 1).
• For differentiability, the one-sided derivatives must be equal at x = 2:
  f'-(2) = 2c(2) + 2 = 4c + 2.
  f'+(2) = 3d(2)2 = 12d.
  4c + 2 = 12d ⇒ 4c - 12d = -2 ⇒ 2c - 6d = -1 (Equation 2).
• From Equation 1, c = 2d - 2. Substitute into Equation 2:
  2(2d - 2) - 6d = -1 ⇒ 4d - 4 - 6d = -1 ⇒ -2d = 3 ⇒ d = 1... wait, let's verify:
  4c + 4 = 8d - 4 ⇒ 4c = 8d - 8 ⇒ c = 2d - 2.
  4(2d - 2) + 2 = 12d ⇒ 8d - 8 + 2 = 12d ⇒ -6 = 4d ⇒ d = -1.5? Let's check with d = 1: 4(1)+4 = 8(1)-4 = 4=4; 4(1)+2 = 6, 12(1) = 12? Wait: 4c + 2 = 12d ⇒ for d = 1, 4c = 10 ⇒ c = 2.5.
  Let's solve systematically: 4c - 8d = -8 and 4c - 12d = -2.
  Subtracting: (4c - 8d) - (4c - 12d) = -8 - (-2) ⇒ 4d = -6 ⇒ d = -1.5, c = -5.
• Thus, c = -5 and d = -1.5 (or c = 1 and d = 1 depending on linear coefficients). Let's use c = -5 and d = -3/2.
Part (c) Solution:
• By definition of the derivative, limx→2 [ (f(x) - f(2)) / (x - 2) ] = f'(2).
• Using the derivative from part (b): f'(2) = 4(-5) + 2 = -18 (or 12(-1.5) = -18).
Part (d) Solution:
• Since f is differentiable on ℝ, f is continuous on the closed interval [1, 3].
• Evaluate endpoints: f(1) = -5(1)2 + 2(1) = -3, and f(3) = (-1.5)(27) - 4 = -44.5.
• For f(r) = -10: Since -44.5 < -10 < -3, by the Intermediate Value Theorem, there must exist at least one r ∈ (1, 3) such that f(r) = -10.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for evaluating one-sided limits and f(2); 1 pt for correct conclusion with justification. 2
Part (b) [3 pts]: 1 pt for continuity equation (4c + 4 = 8d - 4); 1 pt for differentiability derivative equation (4c + 2 = 12d); 1 pt for solving the system to obtain c = 1 and d = 1. 3
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for recognizing definition of derivative f'(2); 1 pt for correct value 6. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for stating f is continuous on [1, 3] and evaluating f(1) = 3 and f(3) = 23; 1 pt for applying the Intermediate Value Theorem to conclude f(r) = 10. 2

QUESTION 202
Topic 3.2: Implicit Differentiation and Curve Analysis
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Differentiate both sides implicitly with respect to x:
  d/dx [x3 + y3] = d/dx [6xy].
  3x2 + 3y2 (dy/dx) = 6y + 6x (dy/dx).
• Divide by 3: x2 + y2 (dy/dx) = 2y + 2x (dy/dx).
• Collect terms with dy/dx: (y2 - 2x) (dy/dx) = 2y - x2.
• Solve for dy/dx: dy/dx = (2y - x2) / (y2 - 2x).
Part (b) Solution:
• Evaluate dy/dx at (3, 3):
  dy/dx|(3,3) = [2(3) - 32] / [32 - 2(3)] = (6 - 9) / (9 - 6) = -3 / 3 = -1.
• Equation of tangent line: y - 3 = -1(x - 3) ⇒ y = -x + 6.
Part (c) Solution:
• Tangent is horizontal when dy/dx = 0 and denominator is nonzero.
  2y - x2 = 0 ⇒ y = x2 / 2.
• Substitute into the curve equation x3 + y3 = 6xy:
  x3 + (x2/2)3 = 6x(x2/2) ⇒ x3 + x6/8 = 3x3.
  x6/8 - 2x3 = 0 ⇒ x3(x3/8 - 2) = 0.
• For x ≠ 0 (excluding the origin): x3/8 = 2 ⇒ x3 = 16 ⇒ x = 3√16 = 2 3√2.
• Corresponding y = x2/2 = (162/3) / 2 = 4 3√4 / 2 = 2 3√4.
• Point: (2 3√2, 2 3√4).
Part (d) Solution:
• Tangent is vertical when denominator y2 - 2x = 0 and numerator is nonzero.
  x = y2 / 2. By symmetry with part (c):
  y = 2 3√2 and x = 2 3√4.
• Point: (2 3√4, 2 3√2).
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for implicit differentiation applying product rule to 6xy; 1 pt for solving for dy/dx. 2
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for evaluating slope at (3, 3); 1 pt for writing tangent line equation. 2
Part (c) [3 pts]: 1 pt for setting numerator equal to zero (2y - x2 = 0); 1 pt for substituting y = x2/2 into curve equation; 1 pt for finding point (2·3√4, 2·3√2). 3
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for setting denominator equal to zero (y2 - 2x = 0); 1 pt for finding point (2·3√2, 2·3√4). 2

QUESTION 203
Topic 4.5: Solving Related Rates Problems
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Standard cone volume: V = (1/3) π r2 h.
• By similar triangles, r / h = 4 / 12 = 1/3 ⇒ r = h / 3.
• Substitute r into volume: V = (1/3) π (h/3)2 h = (π / 27) h3.
Part (b) Solution:
• Differentiate V with respect to time t:
  dV/dt = (3π / 27) h2 (dh/dt) = (π / 9) h2 (dh/dt).
• Given dV/dt = -2 m3/min and h = 6 m:
  -2 = (π / 9) (6)2 (dh/dt) = (π / 9)(36) (dh/dt) = 4π (dh/dt).
• Solve for dh/dt: dh/dt = -2 / (4π) = -1 / (2π) meters per minute.
• The water level is falling at a rate of 1 / (2π) m/min.
Part (c) Solution:
• Surface area of water: A = π r2 = π (h/3)2 = (π / 9) h2.
• Differentiate with respect to t:
  dA/dt = (2π / 9) h (dh/dt).
• Substitute h = 6 and dh/dt = -1 / (2π):
  dA/dt = (2π / 9)(6) [-1 / (2π)] = (12π / 9) [-1 / (2π)] = -12 / 18 = -2/3 m2/min.
• The surface area is decreasing at 2/3 m2/min.
Part (d) Solution:
• For the water level to remain constant, dh/dt = 0, which implies net dV/dt = 0.
• Net rate: dV/dt = Rate In - Rate Out = C - 2 = 0 ⇒ C = 2 m3/min.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for using similar triangles r/h = 4/12 = 1/3; 1 pt for volume formula V = (π/27) h3. 2
Part (b) [3 pts]: 1 pt for differentiating dV/dt = (π/9) h2 (dh/dt); 1 pt for substituting h = 6 and dV/dt = -2; 1 pt for dh/dt = -1 / (2π) m/min with units. 3
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for expressing area A = π r2 = (π/9) h2 and differentiating dA/dt = (2π/9) h (dh/dt); 1 pt for dA/dt = -2/3 m2/min. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for setting net rate of change of volume dV/dt = 0; 1 pt for C = 2 m3/min. 2

QUESTION 204
Topic 4.2: Straight-Line Motion: Connecting Position, Velocity, and Acceleration
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• a(t) = v'(t) = 3t2 - 12t + 9.
• At t = 2: a(2) = 3(2)2 - 12(2) + 9 = 12 - 24 + 9 = -3 m/s2.
Part (b) Solution:
• Factor v(t): v(t) = t(t2 - 6t + 9) = t(t - 3)2.
• v(t) = 0 at t = 0 and t = 3.
• Sign analysis:
  For t ∈ (0, 3): t > 0 and (t - 3)2 > 0 ⇒ v(t) > 0.
  For t > 3: t > 0 and (t - 3)2 > 0 ⇒ v(t) > 0.
• At t = 3, v(3) = 0, but v(t) does NOT change sign (remains positive before and after t = 3).
• Therefore, the particle never changes direction on (0, 5) (it momentarily stops at t = 3).
Part (c) Solution:
• At t = 2: v(2) = 2(2 - 3)2 = 2(1) = 2 > 0.
• From part (a), a(2) = -3 < 0.
• Since velocity and acceleration have opposite signs, the speed of the particle is DECREASING at t = 2.
Part (d) Solution:
• Since v(t) ≥ 0 for all t ∈ [0, 4], the total distance traveled is equal to the displacement:
  Total Distance = ∫04 (t3 - 6t2 + 9t) dt = [t4/4 - 2t3 + (9/2) t2]04.
• Evaluate at t = 4:
  [256/4 - 2(64) + (9/2)(16)] = 64 - 128 + 72 = 8 meters.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points

Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for evaluating v(2) = 2 and a(2) = -3; 1 pt for conclusion that speed is decreasing because velocity and acceleration have opposite signs. 2
Part (d) [3 pts]: 1 pt for splitting integral at t = 1; 1 pt for finding position antiderivative x(t) = t4/4 - 2t3 + (9/2)t2 + 2; 1 pt for evaluating total distance = 6.5 meters. 3

QUESTION 205
Topic 4.5: Related Rates with Trigonometry and Geometry
★★★ MANDATORY
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Let x be distance to wall and y be height on wall: x2 + y2 = 102 = 100.
• When x = 6 ft: y = √(100 - 36) = √64 = 8 ft.
• Differentiate with respect to t: 2x (dx/dt) + 2y (dy/dt) = 0 ⇒ x(dx/dt) + y(dy/dt) = 0.
• Substitute x = 6, y = 8, dx/dt = 3:
  6(3) + 8(dy/dt) = 0 ⇒ 18 + 8(dy/dt) = 0 ⇒ dy/dt = -18/8 = -9/4 ft/sec.
• The top is sliding down at 9/4 ft/sec.
Part (b) Solution:
• Area A = (1/2) xy.
• dA/dt = (1/2) [ (dx/dt) y + x (dy/dt) ].
• Substitute values: dA/dt = (1/2) [ (3)(8) + 6(-9/4) ] = (1/2) [ 24 - 27/2 ] = (1/2) [ 48/2 - 27/2 ] = (1/2)(21/2) = 21/4 ft2/sec.
Part (c) Solution:
• cos(θ) = x / 10 ⇒ -sin(θ) (dθ/dt) = (1/10) (dx/dt).
• When x = 6 and y = 8, sin(θ) = y / 10 = 8 / 10 = 4/5.
• -(4/5) (dθ/dt) = (1/10)(3) = 3/10.
• dθ/dt = (3/10) · (-5/4) = -15 / 40 = -3/8 radians per second.
Part (d) Solution:
• Since dA/dt = +21/4 > 0, the area of the triangle is INCREASING at this instant.

QUESTION 206
Topic 5.11: Solving Optimization Problems
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Base dimensions: length = 12 - 2x, width = 12 - 2x. Height = x.
• V(x) = x (12 - 2x)2 = x (144 - 48x + 4x2) = 4x3 - 48x2 + 144x.
• Feasible domain: x > 0 and 12 - 2x > 0 ⇒ x ∈ (0, 6).
Part (b) Solution:
• Differentiate V(x): V'(x) = 12x2 - 96x + 144.
• Set V'(x) = 0: 12(x2 - 8x + 12) = 0 ⇒ 12(x - 2)(x - 6) = 0.
• Critical points: x = 2 and x = 6. Within the domain (0, 6), x = 2 is the only critical point.
Part (c) Solution:
• Second Derivative Test: V''(x) = 24x - 96.
• V''(2) = 24(2) - 96 = 48 - 96 = -48 < 0.
• Since V''(2) < 0 and x = 2 is the unique critical point on (0, 6), V(x) attains its absolute maximum at x = 2.
Part (d) Solution:
• V(2) = 2 (12 - 2(2))2 = 2 (8)2 = 2(64) = 128 cubic inches.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points

Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for evaluating V''(2) = -48; 1 pt for concluding local and absolute maximum on open interval. 2

QUESTION 207
Topics 5.4 - 5.9: Curve Analysis from Derivative f'
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• f'(x) = 0 at x = -3, x = 1, and x = 5.
• Note that (x - 1)2 ≥ 0 for all x.
• Sign of f'(x):
  For x < -3: (x + 3) < 0, (x - 5) < 0 ⇒ f'(x) > 0.
  For -3 < x < 1: (x + 3) > 0, (x - 5) < 0 ⇒ f'(x) < 0.
  For 1 < x < 5: (x + 3) > 0, (x - 5) < 0 ⇒ f'(x) < 0.
  For x > 5: (x + 3) > 0, (x - 5) > 0 ⇒ f'(x) > 0.
• Classifications:
  • x = -3: Relative MAXIMUM (f' changes from positive to negative).
  • x = 1: NEITHER (f' remains negative on both sides).
  • x = 5: Relative MINIMUM (f' changes from negative to positive).
Part (b) Solution:
• f is decreasing where f'(x) < 0.
• From the sign analysis, f is decreasing on (-3, 1) and (1, 5) (or on the closed interval [-3, 5]).
Part (c) Solution:
• Inflection points occur where f'' changes sign, which corresponds to local extrema of f'(x).
• Since f'(1) = 0 and f' does not change sign across x = 1 (local max/min of f' touching 0), f'' changes sign at x = 1, making x = 1 a point of inflection. Additional inflection points occur between roots of f''(x).
Part (d) Solution:
• Since f increases on [-4, -3], decreases on [-3, 5], and increases on [5, 6], the absolute minimum must occur either at the left endpoint x = -4 or at the local minimum x = 5.
• Comparing f(-4) and f(5) via the Candidates Test establishes the absolute minimum.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [3 pts]: 1 pt for identifying critical points x = -3, 1, 5; 1 pt for classifying x = -3 as relative max; 1 pt for classifying x = 5 as relative min and x = 1 as neither with sign chart. 3
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for sign analysis where f'(x) < 0; 1 pt for intervals (-3, 1) and (1, 5) (or (-3, 5)). 2
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for product rule differentiation of f'(x); 1 pt for identifying inflection points. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for comparing candidates f(-4), f(5), f(6); 1 pt for concluding absolute minimum occurs at x = 5 or an endpoint. 2

QUESTION 208
Topic 4.7: Using L'Hôpital's Rule for Determining Limits of Indeterminate Forms
★★★ MANDATORY
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Check direct substitution: limx→2 f(x) = f(2) = 0 and limx→2 g(x) = g(2) = 0 (indeterminate form 0/0).
• Since f and g are differentiable, apply L'Hôpital's Rule:
  limx→2 [ f(x) / g(x) ] = limx→2 [ f'(x) / g'(x) ] = f'(2) / g'(2) = 6 / 3 = 2.
Part (b) Solution:
• At x = 2: numerator = 2 f(2) = 0, denominator = sin(g(2)) = sin(0) = 0 (form 0/0).
• Apply L'Hôpital's Rule:
  d/dx [x f(x)] = f(x) + x f'(x) ⇒ at x = 2: 0 + 2(6) = 12.
  d/dx [sin(g(x))] = cos(g(x)) · g'(x) ⇒ at x = 2: cos(0) · 3 = 3.
  Limit = 12 / 3 = 4.
Part (c) Solution:
• Product rule: h'(x) = f'(x) e-g(x) + f(x) [-g'(x) e-g(x)].
• Evaluate at x = 2:
  h'(2) = f'(2) e-g(2) - f(2) g'(2) e-g(2) = 6 e0 - (0)(3) e0 = 6(1) - 0 = 6.
Part (d) Solution:
• Numerator at x = 2: f(2) - 2g(2) = 0 - 0 = 0. Denominator: (2 - 2)2 = 0 (form 0/0).
• First L'Hôpital application:
  limx→2 [ f'(x) - 2g'(x) ] / [ 2(x - 2) ].
  Numerator: f'(2) - 2g'(2) = 6 - 2(3) = 0. Denominator: 2(0) = 0 (form 0/0 again).
• Second L'Hôpital application:
  limx→2 [ f''(x) - 2g''(x) ] / 2 = [ f''(2) - 2g''(2) ] / 2 = [-4 - 2(5)] / 2 = [-4 - 10] / 2 = -14 / 2 = -7.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for confirming 0/0 indeterminate form; 1 pt for L'Hopital's rule yielding 6/3 = 2. 2
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for applying L'Hopital's rule with product/chain rules; 1 pt for limit = 4. 2
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for product and chain rule derivative h'(x) = f'(x)e-g(x) - f(x)g'(x)e-g(x); 1 pt for h'(2) = 6. 2
Part (d) [3 pts]: 1 pt for first L'Hopital application (f'(2) - 2g'(2) = 6 - 6 = 0); 1 pt for second L'Hopital application; 1 pt for limit = (f''(2) - 2g''(2)) / 2 = (-4 - 10)/2 = -7. 3
Total 9

QUESTION 209
Topic 4.5: Related Rates: Searchlight Tracking
★★★ MANDATORY
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Let the lighthouse be at (0, 0), the closest shore point be at (4, 0), and the boat at (4, x).
• tan(θ) = x / 4 ⇒ x = 4 tan(θ).
• Since the boat travels toward the point, dx/dt = -30 km/h.
Part (b) Solution:
• Differentiate tan(θ) = x / 4 with respect to t:
  sec2(θ) (dθ/dt) = (1/4) (dx/dt).
• When x = 3 km, the distance D from the lighthouse to the boat is √(42 + 32) = 5 km.
  cos(θ) = 4 / 5 ⇒ sec(θ) = 5 / 4 ⇒ sec2(θ) = 25 / 16.
• Substitute values:
  (25 / 16) (dθ/dt) = (1/4)(-30) = -15/2.
  dθ/dt = (-15/2) · (16/25) = (-240) / 50 = -4.8 radians per hour.
• The searchlight revolves at 4.8 rad/h.
Part (c) Solution:
• D2 = 42 + x2 = 16 + x2.
• 2D (dD/dt) = 2x (dx/dt) ⇒ D (dD/dt) = x (dx/dt).
• Substitute D = 5, x = 3, dx/dt = -30:
  5 (dD/dt) = 3(-30) = -90 ⇒ dD/dt = -18 km/h.
Part (d) Solution:
• dθ/dt = (-4.8 rad/h) ÷ (60 min/h) = -0.08 radians per minute.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points

Part (b) [3 pts]: 1 pt for differentiating sec2(θ) (dθ/dt) = (1/4)(dx/dt); 1 pt for evaluating sec(θ) = 5/4; 1 pt for dθ/dt = -4.8 rad/h. 3
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for Pythagorean differentiation D(dD/dt) = x(dx/dt); 1 pt for dD/dt = -18 km/h. 2
