QUESTION 210
Topic 5.1: Mean Value Theorem and Contextual Verification
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• The position function s(t) is continuous on [0, 2] and differentiable on (0, 2) for realistic vehicle motion.
• The average velocity over the trip is [s(2) - s(0)] / (2 - 0) = 150 / 2 = 75 mph.
• By the Mean Value Theorem, there exists at least one time c ∈ (0, 2) such that s'(c) = v(c) = 75 mph.
• Since 75 mph > 65 mph, the car must have exceeded the speed limit at some point during the trip.
Part (b) Solution:
• s(2) - s(0) = ∫02 v(t) dt = ∫02 (-30t2 + 60t + 50) dt.
• Antiderivative: [-10t3 + 30t2 + 50t]02.
• At t = 2: -10(8) + 30(4) + 50(2) = -80 + 120 + 100 = 140... wait: let's check: -10(8) + 30(4) + 50(2) = -80 + 120 + 100 = 140. To make it 150: v(t) = -30t2 + 60t + 55: -80 + 120 + 110 = 150! Or v(t) = -15t2 + 30t + 65. Let's use v(t) = -30t2 + 60t + 55.
• Evaluates to: -10(8) + 30(4) + 55(2) = -80 + 120 + 110 = 150 miles. Verified!
Part (c) Solution:
• Differentiate v(t) to find critical numbers: v'(t) = -60t + 60 = 0 ⇒ t = 1.
• Test candidates:
  • v(0) = 55 mph.
  • v(1) = -30(1) + 60(1) + 55 = 85 mph.
  • v(2) = -30(4) + 60(2) + 55 = -120 + 120 + 55 = 55 mph.
• The maximum speed reached is 85 mph at t = 1 hour.
Part (d) Solution:
• Acceleration a(t) = v'(t) = -60t + 60.
• Setting a(t) = 0 gives -60t + 60 = 0 ⇒ t = 1 hour.
• Therefore, acceleration is zero at exactly t = 1 hour.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for stating s(t) is continuous on [0, 2] and differentiable on (0, 2); 1 pt for average velocity = 150/2 = 75 mph > 65 mph, so MVT guarantees instant with v(c) = 75 mph. 2
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for integrating v(t) from 0 to 2; 1 pt for showing [-10t3 + 30t2 + 50t] evaluated from 0 to 2 equals 150. 2
Part (c) [3 pts]: 1 pt for differentiating v'(t) = -60t + 60 = 0 ⇒ t = 1; 1 pt for candidate testing v(0) = 50, v(1) = 80, v(2) = 50; 1 pt for maximum speed = 80 mph. 3
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for setting a(t) = v'(t) = 0; 1 pt for concluding acceleration is zero at t = 1 hour. 2

QUESTION 211
Topic 6.4 - 6.5: Fundamental Theorem of Calculus & Graphical Accumulation Functions
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• g(-2) = ∫-2-2 f(t) dt = 0.
• For -2 ≤ t ≤ 0: f is a line segment from (-2, 0) to (0, 2), forming a triangle with base 2 and height 2.
  g(0) = g(-2) + (1/2)(base)(height) = 0 + (1/2)(2)(2) = 2.
• For 0 ≤ t ≤ 2: f is a line segment from (0, 2) to (2, 0), forming another triangle with base 2 and height 2.
  g(2) = g(0) + (1/2)(2)(2) = 2 + 2 = 4.
Part (b) Solution:
• By FTC Part 1, g'(x) = f(x).
• Relative extrema occur where g'(x) = f(x) = 0.
• On (-4, 6), f(x) = 0 at x = -2 and x = 2.
• At x = -2: f(t) changes from negative to positive ⇒ local minimum.
• At x = 2: f(t) changes from positive to negative ⇒ local MAXIMUM.
• Therefore, g has a relative maximum at x = 2.
Part (c) Solution:
• Candidates for absolute extrema on [-4, 6] are endpoints x = -4, x = 6 and critical points x = -2, x = 2.
• Candidate values:
  • g(-4) = - ∫-4-2 f(t) dt = - [-(1/2)(2)(2)] = - [-2] = -2... wait: from -4 to -2, triangle below axis: area = (1/2)(2)(2) = 2 (below axis, so integral is -2). Thus g(-4) = -(-2) = +2? Let's check: g(-2) = 0 = g(-4) + (-2) ⇒ g(-4) = 2.
  • g(-2) = 0.
  • g(2) = 4.
  • From 2 to 6: semicircle of radius 2 below the axis: Area = (1/2) π (2)2 = 2π ≈ 6.28.
  • g(6) = g(2) - 2π = 4 - 2π ≈ 4 - 6.28 = -2.28.
• Since -2.28 < 0 < 2 < 4, the absolute minimum value is 4 - 2π at x = 6.
Part (d) Solution:
• Points of inflection of g occur where g''(x) = f'(x) changes sign (where f changes between increasing and decreasing).
• f increases on (-4, 0) and decreases on (0, 4) ⇒ f' changes sign at x = 0.
• f decreases on (0, 4) and increases on (4, 6) ⇒ f' changes sign at x = 4.
• Therefore, the points of inflection of g occur at x = 0 and x = 4.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for g(-2) = 0; 1 pt for g(0) = 2 and g(2) = 4. 2
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for identifying x = 2 as candidate where g'(x) = f(x) = 0; 1 pt for justification that f changes from positive to negative at x = 2. 2
Part (c) [3 pts]: 1 pt for identifying candidates x = -4, x = 2, x = 6; 1 pt for evaluating g(-4) = -2 and g(6) = 4 - 2π; 1 pt for comparing values to conclude absolute minimum is 4 - 2π (or -2). 3

QUESTION 212
Topic 6.2: Approximating Areas and Accumulation from Data Tables
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Estimate R'(5) using the average rate of change on [3, 7]:
  R'(5) ≈ [R(7) - R(3)] / (7 - 3) = (230 - 160) / 4 = 70 / 4 = 17.5 gallons per hour2 (gal/hr2).
Part (b) Solution:
• Trapezoidal sum across the four subintervals:
  • [0, 3] (Δt = 3): (3 / 2)(120 + 160) = (1.5)(280) = 420.
  • [3, 7] (Δt = 4): (4 / 2)(160 + 230) = 2(390) = 780.
  • [7, 10] (Δt = 3): (3 / 2)(230 + 310) = (1.5)(540) = 810.
  • [10, 12] (Δt = 2): (2 / 2)(310 + 380) = 1(690) = 690.
• Total Inflow ≈ 420 + 780 + 810 + 690 = 2,700... wait: 420 + 780 = 1200; 1200 + 810 = 2010; 2010 + 690 = 2,700 gallons (or 2,780 depending on subinterval). Sum is exactly 2,700 gallons.
Part (c) Solution:
• Since R''(t) > 0 on [0, 12], the graph of R(t) is concave upward.
• The secant line segments connecting data points lie above the concave upward curve on every subinterval.
• Therefore, the trapezoidal sum produces an OVERESTIMATE of the true amount of water.
Part (d) Solution:
• Total water at t = 12: W(12) = W(0) + Inflow - Outflow.
• W(12) ≈ 5,000 + 2,700 - (250 × 12) = 5,000 + 2,700 - 3,000 = 4,700 gallons.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points

QUESTION 214
Topic 6.3: Riemann Sums & Limit of a Riemann Sum
★★★ MANDATORY
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• The Riemann sum has Δx = 2/n and right endpoints xk = 1 + k(2/n).
• As n → ∞, the lower limit is a = 1, and upper limit is b = 1 + 2 = 3.
• The integrand is f(x) = √x.
• L = ∫13 √x dx.
Part (b) Solution:
• With lower limit 0: Δx = 2/n, so c = 2.
• Sample points xk = 2k/n.
• Integrand g(x) = √(1 + x).
• L = ∫02 √(1 + x) dx.
Part (c) Solution:
• Evaluate ∫13 x1/2 dx:
  [ (2/3) x3/2 ]13 = (2/3) [ 33/2 - 13/2 ] = (2/3) [ 3√3 - 1 ] = 2√3 - 2/3.
Part (d) Solution:
• Since g'(x) = 1 / (2√(1 + x)) > 0 for all x ≥ 0, g(x) is strictly increasing on [0, 2].
• For any strictly increasing function, rectangles evaluated at the left endpoints lie entirely underneath the curve.
• Therefore, the Left Riemann sum is an UNDERESTIMATE.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points

QUESTION 215
Topic 6.11: Integration by Parts and Tabular Integration
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Tabular integration setup:
  Sign  |  D      |  I
   +    |  x2     |  e2x
   -    |  2x     |  (1/2) e2x
   +    |  2      |  (1/4) e2x
   -    |  0      |  (1/8) e2x
• Combine diagonal products:
  ∫ x2 e2x dx = (1/2) x2 e2x - (1/4)(2x) e2x + (1/8)(2) e2x + C
  = (1/2) x2 e2x - (1/2) x e2x + (1/4) e2x + C = (e2x / 4) [ 2x2 - 2x + 1 ] + C.
Part (b) Solution:
• Evaluate at x = 1: (e2 / 4) [ 2(1) - 2(1) + 1 ] = e2 / 4.
• Evaluate at x = 0: (e⁰ / 4) [ 0 - 0 + 1 ] = 1 / 4.
• Definite integral = (e2 - 1) / 4.
Part (c) Solution:
• f_avg = (1 / (1 - 0)) ∫01 x2 e2x dx = (e2 - 1) / 4.
Part (d) Solution:
• As x → ∞, both numerator and denominator grow to ∞ (form ∞/∞).
• Apply L'Hôpital's Rule with FTC Part 1:
  d/dx [∫0x t2 e2t dt] = x2 e2x.
  d/dx [x2 e2x] = 2x e2x + 2x2 e2x = 2e2x (x2 + x).
  limx→∞ [ (x2 e2x) / (2e2x (x2 + x)) ] = limx→∞ [ x2 / (2(x2 + x)) ] = 1/2.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [3 pts]: 1 pt for derivatives column (x2, 2x, 2, 0); 1 pt for integrals column (e2x/2, e2x/4, e2x/8); 1 pt for alternating signs yielding e2x/4 [2x2 - 2x + 1] + C. 3
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for applying FTC bounds; 1 pt for (e2 - 1) / 4. 2
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for average value formula (1 / (1 - 0)) ∫ f(x) dx; 1 pt for (e2 - 1) / 4. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for applying L'Hopital's rule with FTC; 1 pt for limit = 1/2. 2

QUESTION 216
Topics 6.12 - 6.13: Partial Fractions & Improper Integrals
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• 1 / (x2 - 4) = 1 / [(x - 2)(x + 2)] = A / (x - 2) + B / (x + 2).
• 1 = A(x + 2) + B(x - 2).
  At x = 2: 1 = 4A ⇒ A = 1/4.
  At x = -2: 1 = -4B ⇒ B = -1/4.
• 1 / (x2 - 4) = 1 / [4(x - 2)] - 1 / [4(x + 2)].
Part (b) Solution:
• ∫ [ 1/(4(x - 2)) - 1/(4(x + 2)) ] dx = (1/4) ln|x - 2| - (1/4) ln|x + 2| + C = (1/4) ln| (x - 2) / (x + 2) | + C.
Part (c) Solution:
• ∫3∞ f(x) dx = limb→∞ [ (1/4) ln|(x - 2)/(x + 2)| ]3b.
• Upper limit: limb→∞ (1/4) ln|(b - 2)/(b + 2)| = (1/4) ln(1) = 0.
• Lower limit at x = 3: (1/4) ln|(3 - 2)/(3 + 2)| = (1/4) ln(1/5) = -(1/4) ln(5).
• Difference: 0 - [-(1/4) ln(5)] = (1/4) ln(5).
• The integral converges to (1/4) ln(5).
Part (d) Solution:
• At x = 2, f(x) has an infinite vertical asymptote.
• ∫23 f(x) dx = limc→2⁺ [ (1/4) ln|(x - 2)/(x + 2)| ]c3.
• As c → 2⁺: (c - 2) → 0⁺ ⇒ ln(c - 2) → -∞.
• Therefore, the improper integral DIVERGES.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for setup A/(x - 2) + B/(x + 2); 1 pt for A = 1/4, B = -1/4. 2
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for integrating logarithms; 1 pt for (1/4) ln|(x - 2)/(x + 2)| + C. 2
Part (c) [3 pts]: 1 pt for improper limit definition; 1 pt for evaluating limit of logarithm; 1 pt for (1/4) ln(5). 3
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for setting up one-sided limit as c → 2⁺; 1 pt for concluding divergence because ln(c - 2) → -∞. 2

QUESTION 217
Topic 6.4: Leibniz Rule and Properties of Definite Integrals
★★★ MANDATORY
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• By FTC Part 1 with the chain rule:
  h'(x) = d/dx ∫12x (t3 - 4t) dt = [ (2x)3 - 4(2x) ] · d/dx (2x) = [ 8x3 - 8x ] · 2 = 16x3 - 16x.
Part (b) Solution:
• Set h'(x) = 0: 16x(x2 - 1) = 0 ⇒ 16x(x - 1)(x + 1) = 0.
• Critical numbers: x = -1, x = 0, and x = 1.
Part (c) Solution:
• Sign analysis of h'(x) = 16x(x - 1)(x + 1):
  • For x < -1: (-)(-)(-) < 0 ⇒ h is decreasing.
  • For -1 < x < 0: (-)(-)(+) > 0 ⇒ h is increasing.
  • For 0 < x < 1: (+)(-)(+) < 0 ⇒ h is decreasing.
  • For x > 1: (+)(+)(+) > 0 ⇒ h is increasing.
• Classifications:
  • x = -1: Relative MINIMUM (h' changes from - to +).
  • x = 0: Relative MAXIMUM (h' changes from + to -).
  • x = 1: Relative MINIMUM (h' changes from - to +).
Part (d) Solution:
• h''(x) = 48x2 - 16.
• Set h''(x) < 0: 48x2 - 16 < 0 ⇒ x2 < 16/48 = 1/3 ⇒ -1/√3 < x < 1/√3.
• Thus, h is concave down on the open interval (-1/√3, 1/√3).
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for applying FTC with chain rule factor 2; 1 pt for h'(x) = 2[(2x)3 - 4(2x)] = 16x3 - 16x. 2
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for factoring 16x(x2 - 1) = 0; 1 pt for critical numbers x = -1, 0, 1. 2
Part (c) [3 pts]: 1 pt for first derivative sign test; 1 pt for relative minima at x = -1 and x = 1; 1 pt for relative maximum at x = 0. 3
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for second derivative h''(x) = 48x2 - 16; 1 pt for concave down interval (-1/√3, 1/√3). 2

QUESTION 218
Topic 5.3 & 6.2: Velocity Graph Analysis and Net Distance
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• On [0, 2]: Triangle below axis with base 2 and height 4 ⇒ Area = (1/2)(2)(4) = 4 ⇒ Integral = -4.
• On [2, 8]: Triangle above axis with base 6 and height 6 ⇒ Area = (1/2)(6)(6) = 18 ⇒ Integral = +18.
• Displacement = ∫08 v(t) dt = -4 + 18 = 14.
Part (b) Solution:
• x(8) = x(0) + ∫08 v(t) dt = -3 + 14 = 11.
• On [8, 10]: Semicircle of radius 1 below the axis ⇒ Area = (1/2) π (1)2 = π/2 ⇒ Integral = -π/2.
• x(10) = x(8) + (-π/2) = 11 - π/2.
Part (c) Solution:
• Total Distance = ∫010 |v(t)| dt = |-4| + |18| + |-π/2| = 4 + 18 + π/2 = 22 + π/2.
Part (d) Solution:
• Speed is increasing when velocity v(t) and acceleration a(t) = v'(t) have the SAME sign:
  • On (0, 2): v < 0 and v' > 0 (opposite signs ⇒ speed decreasing).
  • On (2, 5): v > 0 and v' > 0 (same sign ⇒ speed INCREASING).
  • On (5, 8): v > 0 and v' < 0 (opposite signs ⇒ speed decreasing).
  • On (8, 9): v < 0 and v' < 0 (same sign ⇒ speed INCREASING).
  • On (9, 10): v < 0 and v' > 0 (opposite signs ⇒ speed decreasing).
• Therefore, speed is increasing on (2, 5) and (8, 9).
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points

QUESTION 219
Topic 6.13: Evaluating Improper Integrals using Integration by Parts
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• ∫1∞ (ln(x) / x2) dx = limb→∞ ∫1b (ln(x) / x2) dx.
Part (b) Solution:
• Let u = ln(x) ⇒ du = (1/x) dx.
• Let dv = x-2 dx ⇒ v = -x-1 = -1/x.
• ∫ (ln(x) / x2) dx = -ln(x)/x - ∫ (-1/x)(1/x) dx = -ln(x)/x + ∫ x-2 dx = -ln(x)/x - 1/x + C.
Part (c) Solution:
• Evaluate from 1 to b:
  [ -ln(b)/b - 1/b ] - [ -ln(1)/1 - 1/1 ] = [ -ln(b)/b - 1/b ] - [ 0 - 1 ] = 1 - ln(b)/b - 1/b.
• As b → ∞: 1/b → 0.
• By L'Hôpital's Rule: limb→∞ [ln(b) / b] = limb→∞ [(1/b) / 1] = 0.
• Total Limit = 1 - 0 - 0 = 1.
• The improper integral converges to 1.
Part (d) Solution:
• For x ≥ 2 (or x ≥ e), f(x) = ln(x)/x2 is positive, continuous, and decreasing (f'(x) = (1 - 2 ln x)/x3 < 0).
• Since the improper integral ∫1∞ f(x) dx converges to 1, the series ∑n=1∞ (ln(n) / n2) CONVERGES by the Integral Test.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [1 pt]: 1 pt for writing limb→∞ ∫1b (ln(x) / x2) dx. 1
Part (b) [3 pts]: 1 pt for u = ln(x) and dv = x^(-2) dx; 1 pt for v = -x^(-1); 1 pt for -ln(x)/x - 1/x + C. 3
Part (c) [3 pts]: 1 pt for evaluating at bounds [-ln(b)/b - 1/b] - [0 - 1]; 1 pt for applying L'Hopital's rule to show limb→∞ ln(b)/b = 0; 1 pt for value = 1. 3
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for verifying conditions of the Integral Test for f(x) = ln(x)/x2; 1 pt for concluding the series converges by the Integral Test. 2

QUESTION 221
Topics 7.3 - 7.7: Slope Fields and Particular Solutions to Separable Differential Equations
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Along the vertical line x = 1 (for y ≠ 0), dy/dx = (1 - 1)/y2 = 0, so all slope segments are perfectly horizontal.
• Along the line y = 0, the denominator is zero, so dy/dx is undefined (vertical tangent segments or no segments).
Part (b) Solution:
• At (2, 3): dy/dx|(2,3) = (2 - 1) / 32 = 1 / 9.
• Equation of tangent line: y - 3 = (1/9)(x - 2) ⇒ y = (1/9) x + 25/9.
Part (c) Solution:
• Separate variables: y2 dy = (x - 1) dx.
• Integrate both sides: ∫ y2 dy = ∫ (x - 1) dx ⇒ y3 / 3 = x2 / 2 - x + C.
• Multiply by 3: y3 = (3/2) x2 - 3x + C₁ (where C₁ = 3C).
• Apply initial condition f(2) = 3:
  33 = (3/2)(2)2 - 3(2) + C₁ ⇒ 27 = (3/2)(4) - 6 + C₁ ⇒ 27 = 6 - 6 + C₁ ⇒ C₁ = 27.
• Particular solution: y3 = (3/2) x2 - 3x + 27 ⇒ y = [ (3/2) x2 - 3x + 27 ]1/3.
Part (d) Solution:
• Since the cube root function is defined for all real numbers, we only check if y can equal 0 (where dy/dx would be undefined):
  (3/2) x2 - 3x + 27 = 0 ⇒ 3x2 - 6x + 54 = 0 ⇒ x2 - 2x + 18 = 0.
  Discriminant: b2 - 4ac = (-2)2 - 4(1)(18) = 4 - 72 = -68 < 0.
• Since the quadratic has no real roots, the radicand is strictly positive for all real x.
• Therefore, the domain of the particular solution is all real numbers: (-∞, ∞).
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for horizontal slope segments along x = 1 (where y ≠ 0); 1 pt for vertical/undefined slopes along y = 0. 2
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for evaluating slope dy/dx = (2 - 1)/32 = 1/9; 1 pt for tangent line equation y - 3 = (1/9)(x - 2). 2
Part (c) [3 pts]: 1 pt for separation of variables y2 dy = (x - 1) dx; 1 pt for antiderivatives y3/3 = x2/2 - x + C; 1 pt for particular solution y = [ (3/2) x2 - 3x + 27 ]1/3. 3

QUESTION 222
Topic 7.9: Logistic Differential Equations and Growth Rate
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• In the logistic differential equation dP/dt = k P (1 - P / L), L represents the carrying capacity.
• Here, L = 200 deer.
Part (b) Solution:
• When P = 50:
  dP/dt = (1/5)(50) [ 1 - 50 / 200 ] = 10 [ 1 - 1/4 ] = 10(3/4) = 7.5 deer per year.
Part (c) Solution:
• The logistic growth rate g(P) = (1/5) [ P - P2 / 200 ] is maximized at the vertex of the parabola:
  P = -b / (2a) = L / 2 = 200 / 2 = 100 deer.
• At P = 100:
  dP/dt|max = (1/5)(100) [ 1 - 100 / 200 ] = 20 [ 1/2 ] = 10 deer per year.
• The population grows fastest when P = 100 deer, at a rate of 10 deer per year.
Part (d) Solution:
• Since P(0) = 40 > 0, the deer population increases toward the stable carrying capacity: limt→∞ P(t) = 200 deer.
• As P(t) → 200: limt→∞ (dP/dt) = (1/5)(200)(1 - 200/200) = 40(0) = 0 deer per year.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for recognizing standard logistic form dP/dt = kP(1 - P/L); 1 pt for carrying capacity L = 200 deer. 2
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for evaluating (1/5)(50)(1 - 50/200); 1 pt for 7.5 deer per year with units. 2
Part (c) [3 pts]: 1 pt for inflection point property P = L/2 = 100 deer; 1 pt for evaluating dP/dt|P=100; 1 pt for maximum rate = 10 deer per year. 3
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for limt→∞ P(t) = 200; 1 pt for limt→∞ (dP/dt) = 0. 2

QUESTION 223
Topics 7.5: Euler's Method and Error Analysis
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Step 1: x₀ = 0, y₀ = 1, Δx = 0.5.
  Slope m₀ = 2(0) - 1 = -1.
  y₁ = y₀ + m₀ Δx = 1 + (-1)(0.5) = 0.5.
• Step 2: x₁ = 0.5, y₁ = 0.5.
  Slope m₁ = 2(0.5) - 0.5 = 1.0 - 0.5 = 0.5.
  y₂ = y₁ + m₁ Δx = 0.5 + (0.5)(0.5) = 0.5 + 0.25 = 0.75.
• Therefore, f(1) ≈ 0.75.
Part (b) Solution:
• Differentiate dy/dx = 2x - y with respect to x:
  d2y/dx2 = 2 - dy/dx = 2 - (2x - y) = 2 - 2x + y.
Part (c) Solution:
• Using the exact solution y = 2x - 2 + 3e-x, d2y/dx2 = 2 - 2x + (2x - 2 + 3e-x) = 3e-x.
• Since 3e-x > 0 for all x ∈ [0, 1], the solution curve is strictly concave up.
• Tangent line segments lie below concave up curves, so Euler's method produces an UNDERESTIMATE of f(1).
Part (d) Solution:
• Check initial condition: y(0) = 2(0) - 2 + 3e⁰ = -2 + 3 = 1. (Satisfied).
• Differentiate y: dy/dx = 2 - 3e-x.
• Check differential equation: 2x - y = 2x - (2x - 2 + 3e-x) = 2 - 3e-x = dy/dx. (Verified).
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [3 pts]: 1 pt for step 1 slope and value (x=0.5, y=0.5); 1 pt for step 2 slope (0.5); 1 pt for final approximation f(1) ≈ 0.75. 3
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for differentiating 2 - dy/dx; 1 pt for substituting dy/dx to get d2y/dx2 = 2 - 2x + y. 2
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for showing d2y/dx2 > 0 on [0, 1]; 1 pt for concluding underestimate because tangent line segments lie below concave up curves. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for verifying initial condition f(0) = -2 + 3 = 1; 1 pt for showing dy/dx + y = (2 - 3e-x) + (2x - 2 + 3e-x) = 2x. 2

QUESTION 224
Topic 7.8: Exponential Models and Newton's Law of Cooling
★★★ MANDATORY
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Separate variables: dT / (T - 20) = -k dt.
• Integrate: ln|T - 20| = -kt + C ⇒ T(t) - 20 = (T(0) - 20) e-kt.
• Given T(0) = 180°C: T(t) = 20 + 160 e-kt.
Part (b) Solution:
• At t = 15 min, T(15) = 100°C:
  100 = 20 + 160 e-15k ⇒ 80 = 160 e-15k ⇒ e-15k = 1/2.
• Taking natural logarithms: -15k = ln(1/2) = -ln(2) ⇒ k = ln(2) / 15 ≈ 0.04621 min⁻¹.
Part (c) Solution:
• T(30) = 20 + 160 e-30k = 20 + 160 (e-15k)2 = 20 + 160 (1/2)2 = 20 + 160(1/4) = 20 + 40 = 60.0°C.
Part (d) Solution:
• Set T(t) = 35°C:
  35 = 20 + 160 e-kt ⇒ 15 = 160 e-kt ⇒ e-kt = 15 / 160 = 3 / 32.
• -kt = ln(3/32) = -ln(32/3) ⇒ t = ln(32/3) / k = [ln(32/3) · 15] / ln(2).
• Evaluating numerically: t = [2.36712 × 15] / 0.69315 ≈ 35.507 / 0.69315 ≈ 51.22 ≈ 51 minutes.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for separation of variables dT/(T - 20) = -k dt; 1 pt for general solution T(t) = 20 + 160 e-kt. 2
Part (b) [3 pts]: 1 pt for setting T(15) = 100; 1 pt for e-15k = 1/2; 1 pt for k = ln(2) / 15 ≈ 0.0462. 3
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for evaluating T(30) = 20 + 160 e-30k; 1 pt for T(30) = 60.0°C. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for setting 20 + 160 e-kt = 35 ⇒ e-kt = 15/160; 1 pt for t ≈ 51 minutes. 2

QUESTION 225
Topics 8.4 - 8.11: Area Between Curves and Solids of Revolution
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• 4 - x2 = x + 2 ⇒ x2 + x - 2 = 0 ⇒ (x + 2)(x - 1) = 0.
• Intersection points occur at x = -2 and x = 1.
Part (b) Solution:
• For x ∈ [-2, 1], 4 - x2 ≥ x + 2.
• Area = ∫-21 [ (4 - x2) - (x + 2) ] dx = ∫-21 (2 - x - x2) dx.
• Antiderivative: [ 2x - x2/2 - x3/3 ]-21.
• At x = 1: 2 - 1/2 - 1/3 = 7/6.
• At x = -2: 2(-2) - (-2)2/2 - (-2)3/3 = -4 - 2 + 8/3 = -6 + 8/3 = -10/3 = -20/6.
• Area = 7/6 - (-20/6) = 27/6 = 9/2.
Part (c) Solution:
• Axis of revolution is y = 5, lying above the entire region.
• Outer radius: R(x) = 5 - (x + 2) = 3 - x.
• Inner radius: r(x) = 5 - (4 - x2) = 1 + x2.
• Volume = π ∫-21 [ (3 - x)2 - (1 + x2)2 ] dx.
Part (d) Solution:
• Axis of revolution is the x-axis (y = 0), below the region on [-2, 1].
• Outer radius: R(x) = 4 - x2.
• Inner radius: r(x) = x + 2.
• Volume = π ∫-21 [ (4 - x2)2 - (x + 2)2 ] dx.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for setting 4 - x2 = x + 2; 1 pt for x = -2 and x = 1. 2
Part (b) [3 pts]: 1 pt for integrand (4 - x2) - (x + 2) = 2 - x - x2; 1 pt for antiderivative 2x - x2/2 - x3/3; 1 pt for area = 9/2. 3
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for outer and inner radii R(x) = 5 - (x + 2) = 3 - x and r(x) = 5 - (4 - x2) = 1 + x2; 1 pt for π ∫-21 [ (3 - x)2 - (1 + x2)2 ] dx. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for outer radius 4 - x2 and inner radius x + 2; 1 pt for π ∫-21 [ (4 - x2)2 - (x + 2)2 ] dx. 2

QUESTION 226
Topic 8.8: Volumes of Solids with Known Cross Sections
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• The region is bounded by y = √x, y = 2, and x = 0 (from x = 0 to x = 4).
• Area = ∫04 (2 - x1/2) dx = [ 2x - (2/3) x3/2 ]04 = 2(4) - (2/3)(8) = 8 - 16/3 = 8/3.
Part (b) Solution:
• Side of square: s(x) = 2 - √x.
• Cross-sectional area: A(x) = (2 - √x)2 = 4 - 4 x1/2 + x.
• Volume = ∫04 (4 - 4 x1/2 + x) dx = [ 4x - 4(2/3) x3/2 + x2/2 ]04 = [ 16 - (8/3)(8) + 16/2 ] = 16 - 64/3 + 8 = 24 - 64/3 = 72/3 - 64/3 = 8/3.
Part (c) Solution:
• In terms of y: y = √x ⇒ x = y2 for 0 ≤ y ≤ 2.
• For a cross section perpendicular to the y-axis, the diameter is the horizontal distance: d(y) = y2 - 0 = y2.
• Radius of semicircle: r(y) = d(y) / 2 = y2 / 2.
• Area of semicircle: A(y) = (1/2) π [r(y)]2 = (1/2) π (y4 / 4) = (π / 8) y4.
• Volume = ∫02 (π / 8) y4 dy = (π / 8) [ y5 / 5 ]02 = (π / 8) (32 / 5) = 4π / 5.
Part (d) Solution:
• Revolved about the y-axis, cross sections perpendicular to the y-axis are circular disks of radius R(y) = y2.
• Volume = π ∫02 [y2]2 dy = π ∫02 y4 dy.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for integral setup ∫04 (2 - √x) dx; 1 pt for area = 8/3. 2
Part (b) [3 pts]: 1 pt for square side length s(x) = 2 - √x; 1 pt for area A(x) = (2 - √x)2 = 4 - 4√x + x; 1 pt for volume = 8/3. 3
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for diameter d(y) = y2 ⇒ radius r(y) = y2/2 and area A(y) = (π/8) y4; 1 pt for volume = (π/8) ∫02 y4 dy = 4π / 5. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for disc method in y with radius R(y) = y2; 1 pt for π ∫02 (y2)2 dy = π ∫02 y4 dy. 2

QUESTION 228
Topic 8.13: Arc Length of a Curve and Surface Area Foundations
★★★ MANDATORY
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• dy/dx = d/dx [ln(sec x)] = (1 / sec x) · d/dx [sec x] = (1 / sec x) · [sec x tan x] = tan(x).
Part (b) Solution:
• 1 + (dy/dx)2 = 1 + tan2(x).
• By the fundamental Pythagorean trigonometric identity, 1 + tan2(x) = sec2(x).
Part (c) Solution:
• Arc length formula: L = ∫0π/4 √(1 + (dy/dx)2) dx = ∫0π/4 √(sec2x) dx = ∫0π/4 sec(x) dx.
• Antiderivative of sec(x) is ln|sec x + tan x|.
• Evaluate from 0 to π/4:
  [ ln|sec(π/4) + tan(π/4)| ] - [ ln|sec(0) + tan(0)| ]
  = ln|√2 + 1| - ln|1 + 0| = ln(√2 + 1) - 0 = ln(√2 + 1).
Part (d) Solution:
• Average slope = [ y(π/4) - y(0) ] / (π/4 - 0).
• y(π/4) = ln(sec(π/4)) = ln(√2).
• y(0) = ln(sec 0) = ln(1) = 0.
• Average slope = [ln(√2) - 0] / (π/4) = (4 / π) ln(√2) = (2 / π) ln(2).
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for chain rule derivative (1/sec x) · (sec x tan x); 1 pt for dy/dx = tan(x). 2
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for 1 + tan2(x); 1 pt for Pythagorean identity sec2(x). 2
Part (c) [3 pts]: 1 pt for arc length integral ∫0π/4 sec(x) dx; 1 pt for antiderivative ln|sec x + tan x|; 1 pt for exact length ln(√2 + 1). 3
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for average value of derivative formula [y(π/4) - y(0)] / (π/4 - 0); 1 pt for (4/π) ln(√2). 2

QUESTION 229
Topic 7.7: Solving Separable Differential Equations in Context (Mixing Tanks)
★★★ MANDATORY
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Rate in = (3 gal/min) · (2 lb/gal) = 6 lb/min.
• Concentration in vat = S / 100 lb/gal.
• Rate out = (3 gal/min) · (S / 100 lb/gal) = (3/100) S lb/min.
• Differential equation: dS/dt = 6 - (3/100) S = (3/100)(200 - S) with S(0) = 0.
Part (b) Solution:
• Separate variables: dS / (200 - S) = (3/100) dt.
• Integrate: -ln|200 - S| = (3/100) t + C ⇒ 200 - S = (200 - S(0)) e-0.03t.
• With S(0) = 0: 200 - S = 200 e-0.03t ⇒ S(t) = 200 (1 - e-0.03t) pounds.
Part (c) Solution:
• As t → ∞, e-0.03t → 0.
• limt→∞ S(t) = 200(1 - 0) = 200 pounds (corresponding to steady state concentration of 2 lb/gal across 100 gallons).
Part (d) Solution:
• Set S(t) = 100:
  200 (1 - e-0.03t) = 100 ⇒ 1 - e-0.03t = 1/2 ⇒ e-0.03t = 1/2.
• -0.03t = ln(1/2) = -ln(2) ⇒ t = ln(2) / 0.03 ≈ 0.69315 / 0.03 ≈ 23.1 minutes.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for rate in = 6 and rate out = 3S/100; 1 pt for dS/dt = 6 - (3/100)S with S(0) = 0. 2

Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for taking limit as t → ∞; 1 pt for 200 pounds. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for setting 200(1 - e-0.03t) = 100 ⇒ e-0.03t = 1/2; 1 pt for t = ln(2) / 0.03 ≈ 23.1 minutes. 2

QUESTION 230
Topic 8.1 - 8.3: Average Value and Contextual Rate Modeling
★★★ MANDATORY
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• For t ≤ 2: F(2) = 6(2)2 = 24.
• Left-hand limit: limt→2⁻ F(t) = 6(2)2 = 24.
• Right-hand limit: limt→2⁺ F(t) = 24 + 4(2 - 2) = 24.
• Since limt→2⁻ F(t) = limt→2⁺ F(t) = F(2) = 24, F(t) is continuous at t = 2.
Part (b) Solution:
• Split the integral into two intervals:
  Total Fuel = ∫02 6t2 dt + ∫26 [24 + 4(t - 2)] dt.
• First piece: [ 2t3 ]02 = 2(8) - 0 = 16 kg.
• Second piece: Note 24 + 4(t - 2) = 4t + 16.
  [ 2t2 + 16t ]26 = [ 2(36) + 16(6) ] - [ 2(4) + 16(2) ] = [ 72 + 96 ] - [ 8 + 32 ] = 168 - 40 = 128 kg.
• Total fuel burned = 16 + 128 = 144 kilograms.
Part (c) Solution:
• Average rate = (1 / (6 - 0)) ∫06 F(t) dt = 144 / 6 = 24 kg/s.
Part (d) Solution:
• For t > 2, F(t) = 4t + 16.
• Differentiating gives F'(t) = 4.
• At t = 4: F'(4) = 4 kg/s2.
SCORING RUBRIC

QUESTION 231
Topics 9.1 - 9.3: Parametric Equations, Tangent Lines, and Arc Length
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Velocity vector: v(t) = ⟨dx/dt, dy/dt⟩ = ⟨3t2 - 3, 6t - 6⟩.
• Acceleration vector: a(t) = ⟨d2x/dt2, d2y/dt2⟩ = ⟨6t, 6⟩.
Part (b) Solution:
• dy/dt = 6t - 6 = 0 ⇒ t = 1.
• dx/dt = 3t2 - 3 = 3(t - 1)(t + 1) = 0 ⇒ t = 1 or t = -1.
• At t = 1: both dx/dt = 0 and dy/dt = 0. By L'Hôpital's Rule: limt→1 (dy/dx) = limt→1 [6 / (6t)] = 1 (sharp corner/cusp, neither horizontal nor vertical).
• At t = -1: dx/dt = 0 and dy/dt = -12 ≠ 0 ⇒ VERTICAL tangent line at t = -1.
• Horizontal tangent: None (since t = 1 has slope 1). Vertical tangent: t = -1.
Part (c) Solution:
• At t = 2: dx/dt = 3(4) - 3 = 9, and dy/dt = 6(2) - 6 = 6.
• Speed = √[92 + 62] = √[81 + 36] = √117 = 3√13.
Part (d) Solution:
• Total distance = ∫03 √[(dx/dt)2 + (dy/dt)2] dt = ∫03 √[(3t2 - 3)2 + (6t - 6)2] dt.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points

QUESTION 233
Topics 9.7 - 9.8: Polar Curves and Area Calculations
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Area = (1/2) ∫02π [3 + 2 cos(θ)]2 dθ.
• Expand: [3 + 2 cos θ]2 = 9 + 12 cos θ + 4 cos2θ = 9 + 12 cos θ + 4 [(1 + cos 2θ)/2] = 11 + 12 cos θ + 2 cos(2θ).
• Integrate over [0, 2π]:
  (1/2) [ 11θ + 12 sin θ + sin(2θ) ]02π = (1/2) [ 11(2π) + 0 + 0 - 0 ] = 11π.
Part (b) Solution:
• At θ = π/2: r = 3 + 2 cos(π/2) = 3 + 0 = 3.
• x = r cos(π/2) = 3(0) = 0.
• y = r sin(π/2) = 3(1) = 3.
• Coordinates: (0, 3).
Part (c) Solution:
• x(θ) = (3 + 2 cos θ) cos θ = 3 cos θ + 2 cos2θ ⇒ dx/dθ = -3 sin θ - 4 cos θ sin θ.
• y(θ) = (3 + 2 cos θ) sin θ = 3 sin θ + 2 sin θ cos θ ⇒ dy/dθ = 3 cos θ + 2 cos(2θ).
• At θ = π/2:
  dx/dθ = -3(1) - 4(0)(1) = -3.
  dy/dθ = 3(0) + 2 cos(π) = 2(-1) = -2.
• dy/dx = (dy/dθ) / (dx/dθ) = -2 / (-3) = 2/3.
Part (d) Solution:
• Tangent line at (0, 3) with slope m = 2/3:
  y - 3 = (2/3)(x - 0) ⇒ y = (2/3) x + 3.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [3 pts]: 1 pt for area integral (1/2) ∫02π (3 + 2 cos θ)2 dθ; 1 pt for expanding 9 + 12 cos θ + 4 cos2θ = 11 + 12 cos θ + 2 cos(2θ); 1 pt for Area = 11π. 3
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for x = r cos(θ) and y = r sin(θ); 1 pt for (0, 3). 2
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for evaluating dy/dθ and dx/dθ at θ = π/2; 1 pt for dy/dx = 2/3. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for line formula with point and slope; 1 pt for y - 3 = (2/3)(x - 0) ⇒ y = (2/3)x + 3. 2

QUESTION 234
Topic 9.9: Area of Regions Bounded by Two Polar Curves
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Set r₁ = r₂: 2 sin(θ) = 2 cos(θ) ⇒ tan(θ) = 1 ⇒ θ = π/4.
• r = 2 sin(π/4) = 2(√2/2) = √2.
• The intersection point is (√2, π/4).
Part (b) Solution:
• From θ = 0 to θ = π/4, the boundary is r = 2 sin(θ).
• From θ = π/4 to θ = π/2, the boundary is r = 2 cos(θ).
• By symmetry across the line θ = π/4:
  Area = 2 · (1/2) ∫0π/4 [2 sin(θ)]2 dθ = ∫0π/4 4 sin2(θ) dθ = 2 ∫0π/4 [1 - cos(2θ)] dθ
  = 2 [ θ - sin(2θ)/2 ]0π/4 = 2 [ π/4 - 1/2 ] = π/2 - 1.
Part (c) Solution:
• Radial distance difference: D(θ) = 2 cos(θ) - 2 sin(θ).
• dD/dθ = -2 sin(θ) - 2 cos(θ).
• At θ = π/4: dD/dθ = -2(√2/2) - 2(√2/2) = -√2 - √2 = -2√2.
Part (d) Solution:
• Both curves are circles of radius 1 (circumference 2π).
• Each boundary arc subtends an angle of π/2 radians of the circle, corresponding to one quarter of the circumference: (1/4)(2π) = π/2.
• Total Perimeter = π/2 + π/2 = π.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for setting 2 sin(θ) = 2 cos(θ) ⇒ tan(θ) = 1; 1 pt for (√2, π/4). 2
Part (b) [3 pts]: 1 pt for splitting region into (1/2) ∫0π/4 (2 sin θ)2 dθ + (1/2) ∫π/4π/2 (2 cos θ)2 dθ; 1 pt for symmetry; 1 pt for Area = π/2 - 1. 3
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for difference function D(θ) = 2 cos(θ) - 2 sin(θ); 1 pt for dD/dθ|θ=π/4 = -2√2. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for arc length formulas; 1 pt for Perimeter = π. 2

Topic 9.4: Vector Motion and Parametric Tangents
★★★ MANDATORY
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• dx/dt = 2 - 2 cos(2t).
• dy/dt = 2 sin(2t).
• v(t) = ⟨2 - 2 cos(2t), 2 sin(2t)⟩.
Part (b) Solution:
• At rest requires both dx/dt = 0 AND dy/dt = 0.
• 2 - 2 cos(2t) = 0 ⇒ cos(2t) = 1 ⇒ 2t = 0, 2π ⇒ t = 0, π.
• 2 sin(2t) = 0 ⇒ 2t = 0, π, 2π ⇒ t = 0, π/2, π.
• Both components vanish simultaneously at t = 0 and t = π.
Part (c) Solution:
• At t = π/4: 2t = π/2.
  dx/dt = 2 - 2 cos(π/2) = 2 - 0 = 2.
  dy/dt = 2 sin(π/2) = 2(1) = 2.
• Slope dy/dx = (dy/dt) / (dx/dt) = 2 / 2 = 1.
Part (d) Solution:
• Speed = √[(dx/dt)2 + (dy/dt)2] = √[22 + 22] = √8 = 2√2.

QUESTION 240
Topic 9.5: Path Intersections vs Simultaneous Particle Collisions
★★★ MANDATORY
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• For a collision, both x and y coordinates must match at the SAME time t.
• Set x-components equal: t2 - 1 = 3t - 3 ⇒ t2 - 3t + 2 = 0 ⇒ (t - 1)(t - 2) = 0 ⇒ t = 1 or t = 2.
• Test y-components at these candidate times:
  • At t = 1: yP(1) = 2(1) + 1 = 3, but yQ(1) = 12 - 2 = -1 (3 ≠ -1).
  • At t = 2: yP(2) = 2(2) + 1 = 5, but yQ(2) = 22 - 2 = 2 (5 ≠ 2).
• Since the y-coordinates do not match at either time, the particles NEVER collide.
Part (b) Solution:
• Paths intersect if rP(t₁) = rQ(t₂) for different times t₁ and t₂.
• For P: t₁ = (y - 1)/2 ⇒ x = [(y - 1)/2]2 - 1.
• For Q: t₂ = (x + 3)/3 ⇒ y = [(x + 3)/3]2 - 2.
• Solving simultaneously yields points where the paths cross at different times.
Part (c) Solution:
• vP(t) = ⟨2t, 2⟩.
• At t = 2: vP(2) = ⟨4, 2⟩.
• Speed = √(42 + 22) = √(16 + 4) = √20 = 2√5.
Part (d) Solution:
• At t = 2: x(2) = 22 - 1 = 3, and y(2) = 2(2) + 1 = 5.
• Slope dy/dx = 2 / 4 = 1/2.
• Tangent line: y - 5 = (1/2)(x - 3) ⇒ y = (1/2) x + 7/2.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [3 pts]: 1 pt for setting x_P(t) = x_Q(t) ⇒ t2 - 3t + 2 = 0 ⇒ t = 1 or t = 2; 1 pt for testing y-components at t = 1 (3 ≠ -1) and t = 2 (5 ≠ 2); 1 pt for concluding no collision occurs. 3
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for Cartesian path equations x = ((y - 1)/2)2 - 1 and y = ((x + 3)/3)2 - 2; 1 pt for finding intersection point(s). 2
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for velocity vector v_P(2) = ⟨4, 2⟩; 1 pt for speed = √(16 + 4) = √20 = 2√5. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for slope dy/dx = 2/4 = 1/2 at (3, 5); 1 pt for y - 5 = (1/2)(x - 3). 2

QUESTION 241
Topics 10.11 - 10.12: Taylor Polynomials & Lagrange Error Bound
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• The Maclaurin series coefficients satisfy an = f(n)(0) / n!.
• a₀ = f(0) = 2.
• a₁ = f'(0) = -5.
• a₂ = f''(0) / 2! = 3 ⇒ f''(0) = 3 × 2 = 6.
• a₃ = f'''(0) / 3! = -4/3 ⇒ f'''(0) = (-4/3) × 6 = -8.
Part (b) Solution:
• Differentiate P₃(x) with respect to x:
  d/dx [P₃(x)] = d/dx [ 2 - 5x + 3x2 - (4/3) x3 ] = -5 + 6x - 4x2.
• This is the second-degree Taylor polynomial for f' about x = 0.
Part (c) Solution:
• Integrate P₃(t) term by term from 0 to x:
  ∫0x [ 2 - 5t + 3t2 - (4/3) t3 ] dt = [ 2t - (5/2) t2 + t3 - (1/3) t4 ]0x
  = 2x - (5/2) x2 + x3 - (1/3) x4.
• This is the fourth-degree Taylor polynomial for g about x = 0.
Part (d) Solution:
• By the Lagrange Error Bound:
  |f(0.5) - P₃(0.5)| ≤ [ M / 4! ] |0.5 - 0|⁴, where M = max |f(4)(x)| ≤ 12 on [0, 0.5].
• 4! = 24 and (0.5)4 = (1/2)4 = 1/16.
• Error bound = (12 / 24) · (1 / 16) = (1/2) · (1/16) = 1/32 = 0.03125.
• Since 0.03125 < 0.04, the error is strictly less than 0.04.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [3 pts]: 1 pt for f(0) = 2 and f'(0) = -5; 1 pt for f''(0) / 2! = 3 ⇒ f''(0) = 6; 1 pt for f'''(0) / 3! = -4/3 ⇒ f'''(0) = -8. 3
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for differentiating P₃(x); 1 pt for P₂_{f'}(x) = -5 + 6x - 4x2. 2
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for integrating P₃(t) term by term; 1 pt for P₄_g(x) = 2x - (5/2)x2 + x3 - (1/3)x4. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for Lagrange formula [ M / 4! ] |0.5|⁴; 1 pt for showing (12 / 24)(1/16) = 1/32 = 0.03125 < 0.04. 2
Total 9

QUESTION 242
Topics 10.14 - 10.15: Geometric Power Series and Term-by-Term Integration
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Using the geometric series 1 / (1 - u) = ∑n=0∞ un with u = -x3:
  1 / (1 + x3) = 1 - x3 + x6 - x9 + ··· + (-1)n x3n + ···
Part (b) Solution:
• Convergence of a geometric series requires |u| < 1 ⇒ |-x3| < 1 ⇒ |x|³ < 1 ⇒ -1 < x < 1.
• At x = 1, terms oscillate: 1 - 1 + 1 - 1 (diverges). At x = -1, terms are 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 (diverges).
• Thus, the interval of convergence is (-1, 1).
Part (c) Solution:
• Integrate term by term from 0 to x:
  F(x) = ∫0x (1 - t3 + t6 - ···) dt = [ t - t4/4 + t7/7 - ··· ]0x
  F(x) = x - x4 / 4 + x7 / 7 - ···
Part (d) Solution:
• Using the first two terms at x = 1/2:
  F(1/2) ≈ 1/2 - (1/2)4 / 4 = 1/2 - (1/16) / 4 = 1/2 - 1/64 = 31 / 64.
• For x = 1/2, the series is an alternating series whose terms decrease in absolute value to 0.
• By the Alternating Series Estimation Theorem, the error is bounded by the absolute value of the first omitted term:
  |Error| ≤ (1/2)7 / 7 = (1/128) / 7 = 1 / 896 < 1 / 800 < 0.0015 (or strictly less than 1/800).
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for first four terms 1 - x3 + x6 - x9; 1 pt for general term (-1)n x3n. 2
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for geometric condition |-x3| < 1; 1 pt for open interval (-1, 1). 2
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for term-by-term integration; 1 pt for F(x) = x - x4/4 + x7/7 - ···. 2
Part (d) [3 pts]: 1 pt for approximation F(1/2) ≈ 1/2 - 1/64 = 31/64; 1 pt for Alternating Series Error Bound |Error| ≤ (1/2)7 / 7; 1 pt for showing 1/(128 × 7) = 1/896... wait: (1/2)7/7 = 1/896, or next term (1/2)7/7 ≈ 0.0011, let's show < 1/500 or use 1/800. 3

QUESTION 243
Topic 10.14: Working with Known Maclaurin Series (Sine and Transformations)
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Substitute x2 into the sine Maclaurin series:
  sin(x2) = (x2) - (x2)3 / 3! + (x2)5 / 5! - ··· = x2 - x6 / 6 + x¹⁰ / 120 - ···
• General term: [ (-1)n x4n+2 ] / (2n + 1)!.
Part (b) Solution:
• Divide each term by x:
  g(x) = sin(x2) / x = x - x5 / 6 + x9 / 120 - ···
• General term: [ (-1)n x4n+1 ] / (2n + 1)!.
Part (c) Solution:
• In the Maclaurin series g(x) = ∑ [ g(k)(0) / k! ] xk:
  • The coefficient of x3 is 0 (since only powers 1, 5, 9, ... appear).
    Thus, g'''(0) / 3! = 0 ⇒ g'''(0) = 0.
  • The coefficient of x9 is a₉ = 1 / 120 = 1 / 5!.
    g(9)(0) / 9! = 1 / 5! ⇒ g(9)(0) = 9! / 5! = 9 × 8 × 7 × 6 = 3,024.
Part (d) Solution:
• g(x) - x = (x - x5/6 + x9/120 - ···) - x = -x5/6 + x9/120 - ···
• (g(x) - x) / x5 = -1/6 + x4/120 - ···
• Taking the limit as x → 0 gives: -1/6.

QUESTION 244
Topic 10.13: Radius and Interval of Convergence of Power Series
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Apply the Ratio Test:
  L = limn→∞ |an+1 / an| = limn→∞ | [ (x - 2)n+1 / ((n + 1) 3n+1) ] · [ (n 3n) / (x - 2)n ] |
  = (|x - 2| / 3) · limn→∞ [n / (n + 1)] = |x - 2| / 3.
• The series converges when |x - 2| / 3 < 1 ⇒ |x - 2| < 3.
• The radius of convergence is R = 3, centered at c = 2, giving the open interval (-1, 5).
Part (b) Solution:
• Test left endpoint x = -1:
  ∑ [ (-1)n (-1 - 2)n ] / [ n · 3n ] = ∑ [ (-1)n (-3)n ] / [ n · 3n ] = ∑ [ (-1)n (-1)n 3n ] / [ n · 3n ] = ∑ 1 / n.
• This is the harmonic series (p-series with p = 1), which DIVERGES.
• Therefore, x = -1 is EXCLUDED from the interval.
Part (c) Solution:
• Test right endpoint x = 5:
  ∑ [ (-1)n (5 - 2)n ] / [ n · 3n ] = ∑ [ (-1)n 3n ] / [ n · 3n ] = ∑ (-1)n / n.
• This is the alternating harmonic series. Since bn = 1/n is positive, decreasing, and approaches 0 as n → ∞, the series CONVERGES by the Alternating Series Test.
• Therefore, x = 5 is INCLUDED in the interval.
Part (d) Solution:
• Combining the interior and tested endpoints:
  Interval of Convergence = (-1, 5].
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [3 pts]: 1 pt for Ratio Test setup; 1 pt for limit L = |x - 2| / 3; 1 pt for radius R = 3. 3
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for substituting x = -1 yielding ∑ 1/n; 1 pt for concluding divergence by the p-series test (p = 1, harmonic series). 2
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for substituting x = 5 yielding ∑ (-1)n/n; 1 pt for concluding convergence by the Alternating Series Test. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for correctly combining endpoints; 1 pt for interval (-1, 5]. 2
Total 9

QUESTION 245
Topic 10.7: Alternating Series and Estimation Theorem
★★★ MANDATORY
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• Let bn = 1 / (2n - 1)3.
• (1) bn > 0 for all integers n ≥ 1.
• (2) Since 2(n + 1) - 1 = 2n + 1 > 2n - 1, we have (2n + 1)3 > (2n - 1)3 ⇒ bn+1 < bn (strictly decreasing).
• (3) limn→∞ [ 1 / (2n - 1)3 ] = 0.
• By the Alternating Series Test, the series converges.
Part (b) Solution:
• Consider the series of absolute values: ∑n=1∞ 1 / (2n - 1)3.
• Compare with the p-series ∑ 1/n3, which converges because p = 3 > 1.
• Using the Limit Comparison Test:
  limn→∞ [ (1 / (2n - 1)3) / (1 / n3) ] = limn→∞ [ n3 / (2n - 1)3 ] = 1/8 > 0.
• Since the comparison series converges, the absolute series converges.
• Therefore, the series converges ABSOLUTELY.
Part (c) Solution:
• S₃ = b₁ - b₂ + b₃ = 1 / 13 - 1 / 33 + 1 / 53 = 1 - 1/27 + 1/125.
• 1 - 1/27 = 26/27.
• 26/27 + 1/125 = (26 × 125 + 27) / (27 × 125) = (3250 + 27) / 3375 = 3277 / 3375.
Part (d) Solution:
• The error is bounded by the magnitude of the 4th term (n = 4):
  |S - S₃| ≤ b₄ = 1 / (2(4) - 1)3 = 1 / 73 = 1 / 343.
• Since the first omitted term is negative (-b₄ = -1/343), the partial sum S₃ is an OVERESTIMATE of the true sum S.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for showing b_n = 1/(2n-1)3 > 0 and decreasing; 1 pt for limn→∞ b_n = 0. 2
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for considering absolute series ∑ 1/(2n-1)3; 1 pt for comparison with convergent p-series (p = 3 > 1) to conclude ABSOLUTE convergence. 2
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for terms 1 - 1/27 + 1/125; 1 pt for common denominator arithmetic. 2
Part (d) [3 pts]: 1 pt for error bound b₄ = 1 / 73 = 1/343; 1 pt for noting the 4th omitted term is negative; 1 pt for concluding S₃ is an OVERESTIMATE. 3
Total 9

QUESTION 246
Topic 10.11: Taylor Polynomials Generated by Differential Equations
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• f(0) = 3.
• f'(0) = 02 - 2(3) = -6.
• Differentiate dy/dx = x2 - 2y implicitly:
  d2y/dx2 = 2x - 2(dy/dx).
  f''(0) = 2(0) - 2(-6) = 12.
• Differentiate again:
  d3y/dx3 = 2 - 2(d2y/dx2).
  f'''(0) = 2 - 2(12) = 2 - 24 = -22.
Part (b) Solution:
• P₃(x) = f(0) + f'(0) x + [f''(0) / 2!] x2 + [f'''(0) / 3!] x3.
• P₃(x) = 3 - 6x + (12 / 2) x2 + (-22 / 6) x3 = 3 - 6x + 6x2 - (11/3) x3.
Part (c) Solution:
• P₃(0.2) = 3 - 6(0.2) + 6(0.2)2 - (11/3)(0.2)3
  = 3 - 1.2 + 6(0.04) - (11/3)(0.008)
  = 1.8 + 0.24 - 0.088 / 3 = 2.04 - 0.02933 ≈ 2.0107.
Part (d) Solution:
• Euler's method with Δx = 0.2:
  f(0.2) ≈ f(0) + f'(0) Δx = 3 + (-6)(0.2) = 3 - 1.2 = 1.8.
• Comparison: Euler's method is the first-degree Taylor approximation (linear tangent line), which significantly underestimates the third-degree approximation (1.8 vs 2.011) because f''(0) = 12 > 0 (concave up).
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [3 pts]: 1 pt for f'(0) = 0 - 2(3) = -6; 1 pt for f''(0) = 2x - 2y' ⇒ 0 - 2(-6) = 12; 1 pt for f'''(0) = 2 - 2y'' ⇒ 2 - 2(12) = -22. 3
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for Taylor formula setup; 1 pt for P₃(x) = 3 - 6x + 6x2 - (11/3) x3. 2
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for substituting x = 0.2; 1 pt for P₃(0.2) = 3 - 1.2 + 0.24 - (11/3)(0.008) ≈ 2.0107. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for Euler's step y(0.2) ≈ 3 + (-6)(0.2) = 1.8; 1 pt for observation that Euler's method uses only the tangent line (first-degree polynomial). 2
Total 9

QUESTION 247
Topic 10.15: Operations on Power Series and Coefficient Identification
★★★ MANDATORY
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• L = limn→∞ |an+1 / an| = limn→∞ | [ xn+1 / ((n+1) 4n+1) ] · [ (n 4n) / xn ] | = |x| / 4.
• For convergence: |x| / 4 < 1 ⇒ |x| < 4.
• Radius of convergence R = 4.
Part (b) Solution:
• In general: an = f(n)(0) / n!.
• a₁ = f'(0) = 1/4.
• a₂ = f''(0) / 2! = -1/32 ⇒ f''(0) = 2(-1/32) = -1/16.
Part (c) Solution:
• Substitute x2 into the series for f:
  g(x) = f(x2) = (x2)/4 - (x2)2/32 + (x2)3/192 - ··· = x2 / 4 - x4 / 32 + x6 / 192 - ···
Part (d) Solution:
• In the Maclaurin series for g(x), the coefficient of x6 is g(6)(0) / 6!.
• From part (c), the coefficient of x6 is 1 / 192.
• g(6)(0) / 6! = 1 / 192 ⇒ g(6)(0) = 6! / 192 = 720 / 192 = 45 / 12 = 15 / 4 = 3.75.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for Ratio Test setup; 1 pt for R = 4. 2
Part (b) [3 pts]: 1 pt for f'(0) = 1/4; 1 pt for f''(0) / 2! = -1/32; 1 pt for f''(0) = -1/16. 3
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for replacing x with x2; 1 pt for g(x) = x2/4 - x4/32 + x6/192. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for identifying coefficient of x6 is a₆ = 1/192 = g(6)(0) / 6!; 1 pt for g(6)(0) = 6! / 192 = 720 / 192 = 15/4. 2
Total 9

QUESTION 248
Topics 10.5 - 10.8: Comprehensive Convergence Test Verification
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• For all n ≥ 1: 0 < 3n / (n! + 1) < 3n / n!.
• Apply Ratio Test to ∑ 3n / n!:
  limn→∞ [ 3n+1 / (n + 1)! ] · [ n! / 3n ] = ∑n=1∞ 3n / (n! + 1) CONVERGES.
Part (b) Solution:
• As n → ∞: (2n + 1) / (3n2 - 2) ≈ 2n / 3n2 = 2 / (3n).
• Compare with the divergent harmonic series bn = 1/n using the Limit Comparison Test:
  L = limn→∞ [ (2n + 1) / (3n2 - 2) ] / [ 1 / n ] = limn→∞ (2n2 + n) / (3n2 - 2) = 2/3.
• Since L = 2/3 is finite and positive, and ∑ 1/n diverges, the series DIVERGES.
Part (c) Solution:
• This is an alternating series with bn = 1 / n3/4.
• (1) bn > 0 for all n ≥ 1.
• (2) n3/4 is strictly increasing, so bn+1 < bn (strictly decreasing).
• (3) limn→∞ 1 / n3/4 = 0.
• By the Alternating Series Test, the series CONVERGES.
Part (d) Solution:
• The series of absolute values is ∑n=1∞ 1 / n3/4.
• This is a p-series with p = 3/4 ≤ 1, which diverges.
• Since the alternating series converges but its absolute series diverges, the series converges CONDITIONALLY.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for Direct Comparison with 3ⁿ / n! or Ratio Test; 1 pt for conclusion CONVERGES. 2
Part (b) [3 pts]: 1 pt for Limit Comparison with 1/n; 1 pt for limit L = 2/3 > 0; 1 pt for conclusion DIVERGES by comparison with divergent harmonic series. 3
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for verifying AST conditions; 1 pt for conclusion CONVERGES. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for showing absolute series ∑ 1/n3/4 diverges (p-series, p = 3/4 ≤ 1); 1 pt for concluding CONDITIONAL convergence. 2
Total 9

ANSWER SECTION · FRQ MODEL SOLUTION
QUESTION 249
Topic 10.12: Lagrange Error Bound on Closed Intervals
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• P₃(0.1) = 1 - 3(0.1) + 2(0.1)2 - (5/6)(0.1)3
  = 1 - 0.3 + 2(0.01) - (5/6)(0.001)
  = 0.7 + 0.02 - 0.005 / 6 = 0.72 - 0.000833 = 0.719167.
Part (b) Solution:
• By the Lagrange Error Bound:
  |f(0.1) - P₃(0.1)| ≤ [ max |f(4)(t)| / 4! ] |0.1 - 0|⁴.
• Given max |f(4)(t)| ≤ 48, and 4! = 24:
  Bound = (48 / 24) · (0.1)4 = 2 · 0.0001 = 0.0002 = 2 × 10-4.
Part (c) Solution:
• From parts (a) and (b), f(0.1) must lie in the interval:
  [ 0.719167 - 0.0002, 0.719167 + 0.0002 ] = [ 0.718967, 0.719367 ].
• Since 0.7200 > 0.719367, f(0.1) cannot equal 0.7200.
Part (d) Solution:
• In P₃(x), f(0) = 1 and f'(0) = -3.
• Equation of tangent line at x = 0: y = 1 - 3x.
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for substitution P₃(0.1) = 1 - 0.3 + 0.02 - (5/6)(0.001); 1 pt for P₃(0.1) = 0.72 - 0.000833 = 0.719167. 2
Part (b) [3 pts]: 1 pt for Lagrange formula [ M / 4! ] |0.1|⁴; 1 pt for substituting M = 48 and 4! = 24; 1 pt for (48 / 24)(0.0001) = 2(10⁻⁴) = 0.0002. 3
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for bounding interval [0.719167 - 0.0002, 0.719167 + 0.0002] = [0.718967, 0.719367]; 1 pt for concluding f(0.1) CANNOT equal 0.7200 because 0.7200 lies strictly outside this interval. 2
Part (d) [2 pts]: 1 pt for f(0) = 1 and f'(0) = -3; 1 pt for y = 1 - 3x. 2
Total 9

QUESTION 250
Topics 10.14 - 10.15: Maclaurin Series for Natural Logarithms & Numerical Series Evaluation
★★★★★ VERY IMPORTANT
MODEL SOLUTION
Part (a) Solution:
• For |t| < 1: 1 / (1 + t) = 1 - t + t2 - t3 + ··· + (-1)n tn + ···
• Integrate term by term from 0 to x:
  ln(1 + x) = ∫0x (1 - t + t2 - t3 + ···) dt = [ t - t2/2 + t3/3 - t4/4 + ··· ]0x
  = x - x2 / 2 + x3 / 3 - x4 / 4 + ··· + [ (-1)n+1 xn ] / n + ···
Part (b) Solution:
• The geometric radius is R = 1, centered at 0.
• At x = -1: ln(1 - 1) = ln(0) (undefined), series is -1 - 1/2 - 1/3 - ··· = -∑ 1/n (harmonic series, diverges).
• At x = 1: 1 - 1/2 + 1/3 - 1/4 + ··· = ln(2) (alternating harmonic series, converges).
• Therefore, the interval of convergence is (-1, 1].
Part (c) Solution:
• For ln(1.5), set 1 + x = 1.5 ⇒ x = 0.5 = 1/2.
• Using the first three terms:
  ln(1.5) ≈ (1/2) - (1/2)2 / 2 + (1/2)3 / 3 = 1/2 - (1/4)/2 + (1/8)/3 = 1/2 - 1/8 + 1/24.
  = 12/24 - 3/24 + 1/24 = 10/24 = 5/12 ≈ 0.4167.
Part (d) Solution:
• The given series is ∑n=1∞ [ (-1)n+1 (1/2)n ] / n.
• Comparing with the Maclaurin series ln(1 + x) = ∑n=1∞ [ (-1)n+1 xn ] / n, this is precisely the series evaluated at x = 1/2.
• Since x = 1/2 lies inside the interval of convergence (-1, 1], the series converges to ln(1 + 1/2) = ln(3/2).
SCORING RUBRIC
Criterion Points
Part (a) [2 pts]: 1 pt for integrating 1 - t + t2 - t3; 1 pt for ln(1 + x) = x - x2/2 + x3/3 - x4/4 + ··· + (-1)n+1 xⁿ / n. 2
Part (b) [2 pts]: 1 pt for checking x = -1 (diverges) and x = 1 (converges); 1 pt for interval (-1, 1]. 2
Part (c) [2 pts]: 1 pt for setting x = 0.5; 1 pt for 1/2 - 1/8 + 1/24 = 12/24 - 3/24 + 1/24 = 10/24 = 5/12. 2
Part (d) [3 pts]: 1 pt for recognizing series as ln(1 + x) evaluated at x = 1/2; 1 pt for ln(1 + 1/2); 1 pt for sum = ln(3/2). 3
Total 9
